=== add test/test-git-commit-server.mjs
new 9c03a6ba9cb9384ff3239162
+/**
+ * Commit assistant — the SERVER flow (server.mjs Stage 2b/2c). A dirty repo eagerly gets LLM commit recs; the modal
+ * reads them from GET /api/git-commit-recs; POST /api/git-commit creates the commits and the badge count updates
+ * synchronously (0 uncommitted) before the response returns. Uses a mock adapter that returns a valid rec JSON.
+ * Run: node test/test-git-commit-server.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile, mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { execSync, spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+process.env.CB_GIT_SCAN_MS = '100000';   // high: the initial open-project scan still fires the eager recs, but the
+                                          // periodic background rescan stays quiet — so the stale-recs regression below
+                                          // exercises the ENDPOINT's own on-demand recompute, not the background catching up.
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+// Mock adapter: on the commit-rec prompt, extract the [unit-id] tokens and split them into two commits.
+const adapter = {
+  async complete({ messages }) {
+    const text = (messages || []).map((m) => m.content).join('\n');
+    if (/Reply with ONLY a JSON array|Group the units into commits/.test(text)) {
+      const ids = [...new Set([...text.matchAll(/\[([^\]\s]+)\]/g)].map((m) => m[1]).filter((id) => /#\d+$/.test(id) || /\.[a-z0-9]+$/i.test(id)))];
+      const half = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(ids.length / 2));
+      return { choices: [{ message: { content: JSON.stringify([{ message: 'feat: first group', units: ids.slice(0, half) }, { message: 'chore: second group', units: ids.slice(half) }]) } }] };
+    }
+    return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] };
+  },
+  async listModels() { return [{ id: 'm' }]; },
+};
+
+async function main() {
+  const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gcsrv-'));
+  execSync('git init -q', { cwd: proj });
+  execSync('git config user.email t@t && git config user.name t && git config commit.gpgsign false', { cwd: proj });
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'app.js'), Array.from({ length: 20 }, (_, i) => `line ${i + 1}`).join('\n') + '\n');
+  execSync('git add -A && git commit -qm base', { cwd: proj });
+  // two far-apart edits (2 hunks) + a new file → 3 units
+  const lines = Array.from({ length: 20 }, (_, i) => `line ${i + 1}`); lines[1] = 'A changed'; lines[17] = 'B changed';
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'app.js'), lines.join('\n') + '\n');
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'README.md'), '# readme\n');
+
+  const PORT = 29613, BASE = `http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}`;
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html: '<html></html>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: proj });
+  const getJSON = (p) => fetch(BASE + p).then((r) => r.json());
+  const post = (p, b) => fetch(BASE + p, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify(b) }).then((r) => r.json());
+  try {
+    // wait for the badge to show the repo, then for the eager recs to be ready
+    let gp; for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { await sleep(150); gp = (await getJSON('/api/state')).gitProjects?.[0]; if (gp) break; }
+    ok(gp && gp.uncommitted === 2, `badge shows the dirty repo (2 uncommitted: 1 modified + 1 new; got ${gp?.uncommitted})`);
+
+    let recs; for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { recs = await getJSON('/api/git-commit-recs?path=' + encodeURIComponent(proj)); if (recs.status === 'ready') break; await sleep(200); }
+    ok(recs.status === 'ready' && recs.commits.length === 2, `eager recs ready with 2 commits (status ${recs.status}, ${recs.commits?.length})`);
+    const allUnits = recs.commits.flatMap((c) => c.units);
+    ok(allUnits.length === 3 && allUnits.includes('README.md') && allUnits.some((u) => /app\.js#/.test(u)), `recs cover all 3 units (${allUnits.join(', ')})`);
+    // the changes behind them, for the popup (the developer, 2026-10-28: "It should show the diffs")
+    const dd = await getJSON('/api/git-commit-diffs?path=' + encodeURIComponent(proj));
+    const hunks = allUnits.filter((u) => /#/.test(u)).map((u) => dd.units[u]);
+    ok(dd.status === 'ready' && hunks.length === 2 && hunks.every((h) => h && h.file === 'app.js' && /^@@/.test(h.header)) && hunks.some((h) => h.lines.includes('+A changed')) && hunks.some((h) => h.lines.includes('+B changed') && h.lines.includes('-line 18')),
+      'each hunk the suggestions name comes back with its header and its lines');
+    ok(dd.units['README.md'] && dd.units['README.md'].untracked && dd.units['README.md'].lines.join('|') === '+# readme', `a new file comes back as its lines, each added (${JSON.stringify(dd.units['README.md'])})`);
+    ok((await fetch(BASE + '/api/git-commit-diffs?path=' + encodeURIComponent(proj + '-not-a-repo'))).status === 400, 'a folder that is not one of the repos is refused');
+
+    // REGRESSION (stale recs): after the eager recs are cached 'ready', a NEW file lands. Hitting the endpoint must
+    // RECOMPUTE on-demand (the diff changed) rather than return the stale cache that misses the new file. Background
+    // rescan is disabled (CB_GIT_SCAN_MS high), so a pass here proves the ENDPOINT itself recomputes. Before the fix,
+    // GET returned the cached 'ready' recs unchanged (no extra.js) and this would fail.
+    await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'extra.js'), 'module.exports = 1;\n');
+    let fresh; for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { fresh = await getJSON('/api/git-commit-recs?path=' + encodeURIComponent(proj)); if (fresh.status === 'ready' && fresh.commits.flatMap((c) => c.units).includes('extra.js')) break; await sleep(150); }
+    ok(fresh.status === 'ready' && fresh.commits.flatMap((c) => c.units).includes('extra.js'), `endpoint recomputes after a new file — recs now include extra.js (${fresh.commits?.flatMap((c) => c.units).join(', ')})`);
+
+    // edit a message + commit the FRESH recs (all 4 units → clean tree)
+    fresh.commits[0].message = 'feat: EDITED subject';
+    const done = await post('/api/git-commit', { path: proj, commits: fresh.commits });
+    ok(done.ok === true, `commits created ok (${JSON.stringify(done.results?.map((r) => r.ok))})`);
+    ok((done.gitProjects?.[0]?.uncommitted ?? -1) === 0, `badge count is 0 in the SAME response (before modal close) — got ${done.gitProjects?.[0]?.uncommitted}`);
+
+    const log = spawnSync('git', ['-C', proj, 'log', '--oneline'], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
+    ok(/feat: EDITED subject/.test(log), 'the EDITED commit message landed');
+    ok(log.trim().split('\n').length === 3, `3 commits total (base + 2) — got ${log.trim().split('\n').length}`);
+
+    // STALENESS GUARD: prepare recs, then change the working tree, then try to commit the OLD recs → 409 (would
+    // otherwise stage a different diff than was reviewed). Then a re-fetch + commit of the FRESH recs succeeds.
+    await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'newmod.js'), 'export const a = 1;\n');
+    let staleRecs; for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { staleRecs = await getJSON('/api/git-commit-recs?path=' + encodeURIComponent(proj)); if (staleRecs.status === 'ready' && staleRecs.commits.flatMap((c) => c.units).includes('newmod.js')) break; await sleep(150); }
+    ok(staleRecs.status === 'ready', 'prepared recs for the new change');
+    // change the tree AFTER the recs were prepared (a second new file)
+    await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'sneaky.js'), 'export const b = 2;\n');
+    const stale = await post('/api/git-commit', { path: proj, commits: staleRecs.commits });
+    ok(stale.stale === true && !stale.ok, `committing stale recs is REFUSED (409 stale) — ${JSON.stringify({ stale: stale.stale, err: (stale.error || '').slice(0, 40) })}`);
+    ok(spawnSync('git', ['-C', proj, 'log', '--oneline'], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim().split('\n').length === 3, 'nothing was committed by the refused stale request');
+  } finally { server.close(); await rm(proj, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e?.stack || e); process.exit(2); });
+

+/**
+ * git-commit.mjs — the commit-assistant plumbing. Collects a repo's changes as units (hunks of modified files + whole
+ * new files) and creates a list of commits with MANY-TO-MANY unit→commit assignment (a file's two hunks land in two
+ * different commits; a commit spans a hunk + a new file). Verifies the split actually produces the right commits.
+ * Run: node test/test-git-commit.mjs
+ */
+import { collectRepoChanges, parseDiffFiles, buildCommitPatch, applyCommits, parseCommitRecs, allUnitIds, buildCommitRecPrompt } from '../src/agent/git-commit.mjs';
+import { mkdtempSync, writeFileSync, rmSync, mkdirSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { execSync, spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const G = (dir, args) => spawnSync('git', ['-C', dir, ...args], { encoding: 'utf8' });
+
+const repo = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gc-'));
+try {
+  execSync('git init -q', { cwd: repo });
+  execSync('git config user.email t@t && git config user.name t && git config commit.gpgsign false', { cwd: repo });

+  writeFileSync(path.join(repo, 'app.js'), Array.from({ length: 20 }, (_, i) => `line ${i + 1}`).join('\n') + '\n');
+  execSync('git add -A && git commit -qm base', { cwd: repo });
+

+  const lines = Array.from({ length: 20 }, (_, i) => `line ${i + 1}`);
+  lines[1] = 'line 2 — CHANGED (hunk A)';
+  lines[17] = 'line 18 — CHANGED (hunk B)';
+  writeFileSync(path.join(repo, 'app.js'), lines.join('\n') + '\n');
+  writeFileSync(path.join(repo, 'README.md'), '# new readme\n');
+  mkdirSync(path.join(repo, '.code_boss'), { recursive: true }); writeFileSync(path.join(repo, '.code_boss', 'x.json'), '{}');
+
+  console.log('\n[A] collect changes as units (hunks + new files; .code_boss excluded)');

+  const appFile = files.find((f) => f.path === 'app.js');
+  ok(!!appFile && appFile.hunks.length === 2, `app.js parsed into 2 hunks (got ${appFile?.hunks.length})`);
+  ok(untracked.includes('README.md'), 'README.md offered as a new-file unit');

+
+  console.log('\n[B] MANY-TO-MANY: hunk A → commit 1, hunk B + new README → commit 2');
+  const res = applyCommits(repo, files, [
+    { message: 'fix line 2', units: [appFile.hunks[0].id] },
+    { message: 'change line 18 and add readme', units: [appFile.hunks[1].id, 'README.md'] },
+  ]);
+  ok(res.length === 2 && res.every((r) => r.ok), `both commits created (${res.map((r) => r.mode + (r.ok ? '' : ':FAIL ' + r.error)).join(', ')})`);
+
+  const log = G(repo, ['log', '--oneline']).stdout.trim().split('\n');
+  ok(log.length === 3, `3 commits total (base + 2) — got ${log.length}`);

+
+  // Commit 1 (2nd from HEAD) touched ONLY app.js's line-2 hunk; commit 2 touched app.js line-9 + README.md.

+  const c2 = G(repo, ['show', '--stat', '--name-only', 'HEAD']).stdout;
+  ok(/app\.js/.test(c1) && !/README\.md/.test(c1), 'commit 1 = app.js only (line-2 hunk)');
+  ok(/app\.js/.test(c2) && /README\.md/.test(c2), 'commit 2 = app.js (line-9 hunk) + README.md');
+  const c1diff = G(repo, ['show', 'HEAD~1']).stdout;
+  ok(/hunk A/.test(c1diff) && !/hunk B/.test(c1diff), 'commit 1 contains hunk A but NOT hunk B (a single file split across commits)');
+  const c2diff = G(repo, ['show', 'HEAD']).stdout;
+  ok(/hunk B/.test(c2diff) && !/hunk A/.test(c2diff), 'commit 2 contains hunk B but NOT hunk A');

+  ok(st === '', `no leftover dev changes (got "${st}")`);

+  ok(/^diff --git/m.test(patch) && /@@/.test(patch), 'a single-hunk patch has a diff header + a hunk');
+  ok(parseDiffFiles('').length === 0 && parseDiffFiles('not a diff').length === 0, 'parseDiffFiles tolerates empty/garbage input');

+  console.log('\n[E] parseCommitRecs — validation, fenced JSON, leftover assignment');
+  const ids = ['app.js#0', 'app.js#1', 'README.md'];

+  ok(r1.commits.length === 2 && r1.commits[1].units.includes('README.md'), 'parses fenced JSON into commits');
+  const r2 = parseCommitRecs('[{"message":"x","units":["app.js#0","bogus#9"]}]', ids);   // drops the bogus id, assigns the leftovers
+  ok(r2.commits[0].units.length === 1 && r2.commits.some((c) => c.units.includes('app.js#1') && c.units.includes('README.md')), 'drops unknown ids + assigns leftover units to a trailing commit');
+  ok(parseCommitRecs('not json', ids).error && parseCommitRecs('not json', ids).commits.length === 0, 'garbage reply → error, no commits');
+  ok(allUnitIds({ files, untracked }).length >= 3 && buildCommitRecPrompt({ files, untracked, repoName: 'r' })[0].content.includes('[app.js#0]'), 'prompt lists the unit ids');
+
+  console.log('\n[F] a FRESH repo with NO commits (no HEAD — the first-commit edge)');
+  const fresh = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gc-fresh-'));
+  execSync('git init -q', { cwd: fresh });

+  const fc = collectRepoChanges(fresh);
+  ok(fc.files.length === 0 && fc.untracked.length === 2, 'no-HEAD repo: all files are untracked units (no diff hunks)');
+  const fres = applyCommits(fresh, fc.files, [{ message: 'add one', units: ['one.txt'] }, { message: 'add two', units: ['two.txt'] }]);
+  ok(fres.every((r) => r.ok), `two commits made in a repo that had none (${fres.map((r) => r.ok).join(',')})`);
+  ok(G(fresh, ['log', '--oneline']).stdout.trim().split('\n').length === 2, 'exactly 2 commits (the first is the initial commit)');
+  rmSync(fresh, { recursive: true, force: true });
+
+  console.log('\n[G] a DELETION is captured + committed');
+  const del = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gc-del-'));
+  execSync('git init -q', { cwd: del });

+  writeFileSync(path.join(del, 'gone.txt'), 'x\ny\n'); execSync('git add -A && git commit -qm base', { cwd: del });
+  rmSync(path.join(del, 'gone.txt'), { force: true });

=== add test/test-git-cred-env.mjs
new 1109176e9366db1a76333fd2
+/**
+ * GIT CREDENTIALS, PER CALL, NEVER STORED IN THE CONTAINER (tools.mjs gitCredEnv - the developer's "option 1",
+ * 2026-10-10). Real git against a local HTTP server that records what each request carried:
+ *
+ *   [1] fetch / ls-remote to the developer's GitLab origin carries the credentials, as a header
+ *   [2] the same call to ANOTHER origin carries none - the token goes only to the developer's GitLab
+ *   [3] status, an alias, a call that starts with a flag: no credentials at all
+ *   [4] a clone's remote is the plain URL (nothing of user:token in .git/config)
+ *
+ *   node test/test-git-cred-env.mjs
+ */
+import http from 'node:http';
+import { mkdtempSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gitcred-home-'));
+const { gitCredEnv, plainGitUrl } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+const seen = [];
+const mkServer = () => http.createServer((req, res) => { seen.push({ port: req.socket.localPort, auth: req.headers.authorization || '' }); res.writeHead(404); res.end(); });
+const s1 = mkServer(), s2 = mkServer();
+await new Promise((r) => s1.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', r));
+await new Promise((r) => s2.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', r));
+const P1 = s1.address().port, P2 = s2.address().port;
+const GITLAB = `http://127.0.0.1:${P1}`;
+const creds = { user: 'dev-user', token: 'glpat-zq-TOKEN-4411' };
+// asynchronous: the HTTP servers git talks to are in this same process (spawnSync would hold them still)
+const git = (args, extra) => new Promise((resolve) => {
+  const c = spawn('git', args, { env: { ...process.env, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT: '0', ...(extra || {}) } });
+  let out = ''; c.stdout.on('data', (d) => { out += d; }); c.stderr.on('data', (d) => { out += d; });
+  const t = setTimeout(() => c.kill(), 30000);
+  c.on('close', (code) => { clearTimeout(t); resolve({ code, out }); });
+});
+const decode = (a) => { try { return Buffer.from(a.replace(/^Basic\s+/i, ''), 'base64').toString('utf8'); } catch { return ''; } };
+
+try {
+  console.log('\n[1] to the developer\'s GitLab: the credentials, as a header');
+  seen.length = 0;
+  await git(['ls-remote', `${GITLAB}/group/repo.git`], gitCredEnv(['ls-remote'], creds, process.env, [GITLAB]));
+  ok(seen.some((x) => x.port === P1 && decode(x.auth) === 'dev-user:glpat-zq-TOKEN-4411'), `git sent them (${seen.length} request(s))`);
+
+  console.log('\n[2] to another origin: nothing');
+  seen.length = 0;
+  await git(['ls-remote', `http://127.0.0.1:${P2}/group/repo.git`], gitCredEnv(['ls-remote'], creds, process.env, [GITLAB]));
+  ok(seen.length > 0 && seen.every((x) => !x.auth), `the other server got no credentials (${seen.length} request(s))`);
+
+  console.log('\n[3] no credentials for anything but fetch, pull, push, ls-remote, remote, submodule');
+  ok(gitCredEnv(['status'], creds, process.env, [GITLAB]) === null && gitCredEnv(['zqalias'], creds, process.env, [GITLAB]) === null && gitCredEnv(['-C', 'x', 'push'], creds, process.env, [GITLAB]) === null, 'status, an alias, and a call that starts with a flag get none');
+  ok(gitCredEnv(['push'], creds, process.env, []) === null && gitCredEnv(['push'], null, process.env, [GITLAB]) === null, 'none either with no GitLab known, or no credentials set');
+  const env = gitCredEnv(['push'], creds, { GIT_CONFIG_COUNT: '2' }, [GITLAB]);
+  const keys = Object.keys(env).filter((k) => k.startsWith('GIT_CONFIG_KEY_')).map((k) => env[k]);
+  ok(env.GIT_CONFIG_COUNT === String(2 + keys.length) && env.GIT_CONFIG_KEY_2 === 'credential.helper' && keys.includes('core.hooksPath') && keys.includes('http.' + GITLAB + '/.extraHeader'), `hooks and helpers are off for the call, the header is scoped to the origin, and the container's own settings come first (${keys.join(', ')})`);
+
+  console.log('\n[4] the URL a clone keeps is plain');
+  ok(plainGitUrl('https://u:p@gitlab.example.mil/g/r.git') === 'https://gitlab.example.mil/g/r.git' && plainGitUrl('https://gitlab.example.mil/g/r.git') === 'https://gitlab.example.mil/g/r.git', 'user:token is taken out of a URL');
+  const work = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gitcred-repo-'));
+  const bare = path.join(work, 'origin.git');
+  await git(['init', '--bare', bare]);
+  await git(['clone', bare, path.join(work, 'clone')]);
+  const cfg = readFileSync(path.join(work, 'clone', '.git', 'config'), 'utf8');
+  ok(!/glpat|dev-user/.test(cfg), 'a cloned repo\'s .git/config holds no credentials');
+} finally { s1.close(); s2.close(); }
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+
=== add test/test-git-pills-lift-e2e.mjs
new ad112379e3725289cc7509f2
+/**
+ * Git repo pills that are too wide for the docked status line must LIFT onto their own line above it (where they
+ * wrap), never crush the path or push the Edit/gear controls off-screen. fitGitPills() decided this from "the first
+ * pill spilled" OR "the path is crushed" — but its crush test required clientWidth > 0, so a path crushed to ZERO
+ * width by a long name (the worst case) never counted, and exactly the widest pills stayed put. Drives the real
+ * docked bar at several widths with long and short names. Run: node test/test-git-pills-lift-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { cleanupDir } from './_helpers.mjs';
+import { existsSync, mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-pills-home-'));
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29244;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;

+/**
+ * Git-project badges — the SERVER wiring (server.mjs). The active project (cwd) is scanned for git repos; the list
+ * (branch + live uncommitted count) is exposed on /api/state and refreshed on a timer, so an EXTERNAL change is
+ * picked up. A fast poll interval (CB_GIT_SCAN_MS) keeps the test quick.
+ * Run: node test/test-git-projects-server.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { execSync } from 'node:child_process';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+process.env.CB_GIT_SCAN_MS = '400';   // fast poll for the test
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const adapter = { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; }, async listModels() { return [{ id: 'm' }]; } };
+
+async function main() {
+  const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gpsrv-'));
+  execSync('git init -q', { cwd: proj });
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'a.txt'), 'x');
+  execSync('git add -A && git -c commit.gpgsign=false -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -qm init', { cwd: proj });
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'b.txt'), 'dirty');   // 1 uncommitted (real dev work)
+
+  const PORT = 29611, BASE = `http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}`;
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html: '<html></html>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: proj });
+  const state = async () => (await (await fetch(BASE + '/api/state')).json());
+  const open = async (p) => (await fetch(BASE + '/api/open-project', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ path: p }) })).status;
+  let projB = null, projC = null;
+  try {
+    let s;
+    for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { await sleep(150); s = await state(); if ((s.gitProjects || []).length) break; }
+    const gp = (s.gitProjects || [])[0];
+    ok(!!gp && gp.path === proj, '/api/state carries the active project as a git repo');
+    ok(gp && /^(main|master)$/.test(gp.branch || ''), `branch reported (${gp?.branch})`);
+    ok(gp && gp.uncommitted === 1, `1 uncommitted — .code_boss (created on open) is EXCLUDED (got ${gp?.uncommitted})`);
+
+    // external change → the timer re-scan updates /api/state
+    await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'c.txt'), 'y');
+    let after;
+    for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) { await sleep(150); after = (await state()).gitProjects?.[0]?.uncommitted; if (after !== 1) break; }
+    ok(after === 2, `an external change is picked up by the scan timer (1 → ${after})`);
+
+    // SWITCHING PROJECTS: the first project's repos were still on screen after opening a second one.
+    // The switch reset the broadcast signature but not the list, and a second project with NO repos rescans to
+    // the empty signature — equal to the reset one — so nothing ever replaced the stale list. The pills, the
+    // per-turn prompt and the commit/push endpoints all read that one field.
+    projB = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gpsrv-b-'));   // an ordinary folder, no git anywhere in it
+    ok((await open(projB)) === 200, 'a second project (no git repos) opens');
+    ok((await state()).gitProjects.length === 0, 'the first project\'s repos are gone from state IMMEDIATELY, not after a rescan');
+    await sleep(900);   // past the 300ms rescan and two poll ticks
+    ok((await state()).gitProjects.length === 0, 'and they stay gone once the new project has been scanned');
+
+    // And a third with its own repo shows THAT repo — not the first one's.
+    projC = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gpsrv-c-'));
+    execSync('git init -q', { cwd: projC });
+    await writeFile(path.join(projC, 'z.txt'), 'x');
+    execSync('git add -A && git -c commit.gpgsign=false -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -qm init', { cwd: projC });
+    ok((await open(projC)) === 200, 'a third project (with a repo) opens');
+    let sc;
+    for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { await sleep(150); sc = await state(); if ((sc.gitProjects || []).length) break; }
+    ok(sc.gitProjects.length === 1 && sc.gitProjects[0].path === projC, `state shows the THIRD project's repo (${sc.gitProjects.map((p) => path.basename(p.path)).join(',')})`);
+    ok(!sc.gitProjects.some((p) => p.path === proj), 'and nothing from the first');
+  } finally {
+    server.close();
+    for (const d of [proj, projB, projC]) if (d) await rm(d, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e?.stack || e); process.exit(2); });

+/**

=== add test/test-git-scan-async.mjs
new 12ff7c05893c787da41bd0fa
+/**
+ * THE GIT SCAN DOES NOT HOLD THE SERVER (the developer, 2026-10-11: 30-60 s of "code_boss" time between a reply and
+ * the next request). The repo scan - after every snippet and every 60 s - ran 2-3 `git` calls per repo with
+ * spawnSync on the server's one thread; in the container git is JGit (a JVM per call or a service), and a projects
+ * folder holds several repos. Now the calls are async:
+ *
+ *   [1] gitRepoInfoAsync / gitProjectsFromHitsAsync give the same answers as the sync ones
+ *   [2] a server with 15 repos in its project scans them (badges arrive) while its event loop stays free: the
+ *       longest stall is a fraction of what the git calls took in all
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-git-scan-async.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
+import { monitorEventLoopDelay } from 'node:perf_hooks';
+import path from 'node:path';
+process.env.CB_HOME = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gitasync-home-'));
+process.env.CB_GIT_SCAN_MS = '1500';   // the periodic scan, often: the window below holds a few
+const { gitRepoInfo, gitRepoInfoAsync, gitProjectsFromHitsAsync, scanGitProjects } = await import('../src/agent/git-projects.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const git = (cwd, ...a) => execFileSync('git', ['-c', 'user.name=t', '-c', 'user.email=t@t', ...a], { cwd, stdio: 'ignore' });
+
+const PROJ = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-gitasync-proj-'));
+const N = 15;
+for (let i = 0; i < N; i++) {
+  const r = path.join(PROJ, 'repo' + String(i).padStart(2, '0'));
+  mkdirSync(r); git(r, 'init', '-q'); writeFileSync(path.join(r, 'a.txt'), 'a\n'); git(r, 'add', '.'); git(r, 'commit', '-qm', 'one');
+  for (let k = 0; k < i % 4; k++) writeFileSync(path.join(r, `new${k}.txt`), 'x');   // 0-3 uncommitted
+}
+
+try {
+  console.log('\n[1] the async answers are the sync answers');
+  const r3 = path.join(PROJ, 'repo03');
+  const a = await gitRepoInfoAsync(r3), s = gitRepoInfo(r3);
+  ok(JSON.stringify(a) === JSON.stringify(s) && a.uncommitted === 3 && a.branch, `one repo: ${JSON.stringify(a)}`);
+  const syncAll = scanGitProjects(PROJ);
+  const asyncAll = await gitProjectsFromHitsAsync(syncAll.map((p) => ({ path: p.path, name: p.name })));
+  ok(syncAll.length === N && JSON.stringify(asyncAll) === JSON.stringify(syncAll), `all ${N} repos, the same branches and counts`);
+
+  console.log('\n[2] a server scanning them keeps its event loop free');
+  const t0 = Date.now(); for (let i = 0; i < N; i++) gitRepoInfo(path.join(PROJ, 'repo' + String(i).padStart(2, '0'))); const syncMs = Date.now() - t0;
+  const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+  const PORT = 29780 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 15);
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html: '<html></html>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter: { async *completeStream() {}, async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; } }, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: PROJ });
+  const badges = async () => { try { return (await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/state?limit=1`)).json()).gitProjects || []; } catch { return []; } };
+  let got = [];
+  for (let i = 0; i < 80 && got.length < N; i++) { await sleep(250); got = await badges(); }
+  ok(got.length === N && got.filter((p) => p.uncommitted === 3).length === 3, `the badges arrive: ${got.length} repos (${got.map((p) => p.uncommitted).join('')})`);
+  // after startup (loading the plugins holds the loop on its own): the periodic scans only, one with a change to find
+  await sleep(1000);
+  const h = monitorEventLoopDelay({ resolution: 10 }); h.enable();
+  for (let k = 0; k < 5; k++) writeFileSync(path.join(PROJ, 'repo00', `late${k}.txt`), 'x');
+  let seen = false;
+  for (let i = 0; i < 40 && !seen; i++) { await sleep(250); seen = (await badges()).some((p) => p.name === 'repo00' && p.uncommitted === 5); }
+  await sleep(2000);   // and one more scan
+  h.disable();
+  const maxLag = Math.round(h.max / 1e6);
+  ok(seen, 'a scan in the window found the change (repo00: 5 uncommitted)');
+  ok(maxLag < Math.max(250, syncMs / 3), `the longest event-loop stall while scanning was ${maxLag} ms; the same git calls made synchronously take ${syncMs} ms`);
+  server.close();
+} catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.stack || e); }
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

=== add test/test-gitlab-hardening-probe.mjs
new 82b86a3ec3fc936d4abfa98a
+/**
+ * Regression probes for GitLab client production hardening (confirmed bugs):
+ *  - readFile rejects an HTML SSO/login page returned with 200 (does not hand
+ *    the agent a login page as file content).
+ *  - apiGet error carries the real body text (single read, no 'body used already').
+ *  - UTF-16 / BOM'd text decodes as text, not "binary".
+ *  - a hung fetch is bounded by an internal timeout (does not block forever).
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-gitlab-hardening-probe.mjs
+ */
+import { createGitLabClient, decodeText } from '../src/git-api/gitlab.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+function ok(cond, label) {
+  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ok', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', label); }
+}
+
+function res({ ok: okFlag = true, status = 200, ctype = 'application/json', body = '', headers = {} }) {
+  const buf = Buffer.isBuffer(body) ? body : Buffer.from(String(body), 'utf8');
+  const h = new Map(Object.entries({ 'content-type': ctype, ...headers }).map(([k, v]) => [k.toLowerCase(), v]));
+  return {
+    ok: okFlag, status,
+    headers: { get: (k) => (h.has(k.toLowerCase()) ? h.get(k.toLowerCase()) : null) },
+    async text() { return buf.toString('utf8'); },
+    async json() { return JSON.parse(buf.toString('utf8')); },
+    async arrayBuffer() { return buf.buffer.slice(buf.byteOffset, buf.byteOffset + buf.byteLength); },
+  };
+}
+const creds = () => ({ user: 'u', token: 't' });
+
+async function main() {
+  console.log('\n[A] HTML login page (200) is rejected, not returned as content');
+  {
+    const client = createGitLabClient({
+      serverUrl: 'https://git.example',
+      getCredentials: creds,
+      fetchImpl: async () => res({ ok: true, status: 200, ctype: 'text/html', body: '<html><body>SSO login</body></html>' }),
+    });
+    let threw = false, msg = '';
+    try { await client.readFile({ projectPath: 'g/r', filepath: 'README.md' }); }
+    catch (e) { threw = true; msg = e.message; }
+    ok(threw, 'readFile threw instead of returning HTML as file content');
+    ok(/html|sso|login|interstitial/i.test(msg), `error explains the HTML page (got: ${msg})`);
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[B] error body text is preserved (single read)');
+  {
+    const client = createGitLabClient({
+      serverUrl: 'https://git.example',
+      getCredentials: creds,
+      fetchImpl: async () => res({ ok: false, status: 403, ctype: 'text/html', body: '<html>Forbidden by proxy policy</html>' }),
+    });
+    let msg = '';
+    try { await client.listTree({ projectPath: 'g/r' }); }
+    catch (e) { msg = e.message; }
+    ok(/403/.test(msg), `status in error (got: ${msg})`);
+    ok(/Forbidden by proxy policy/.test(msg), `real body text preserved (got: ${msg})`);
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[C] UTF-16 / BOM text decodes as text');
+  {
+    // UTF-16LE with BOM
+    const utf16le = Buffer.concat([Buffer.from([0xFF, 0xFE]), Buffer.from('plain readme text', 'utf16le')]);
+    const client16 = createGitLabClient({
+      serverUrl: 'https://git.example', getCredentials: creds,
+      fetchImpl: async () => res({ ok: true, status: 200, ctype: 'text/plain', body: utf16le, headers: { 'x-gitlab-ref': 'main' } }),
+    });
+    const r16 = await client16.readFile({ projectPath: 'g/r', filepath: 'readme.txt' });
+    ok(r16.encoding === 'utf8', `UTF-16LE classified as text (got: ${r16.encoding})`);
+    ok(r16.content === 'plain readme text', `UTF-16LE decoded correctly (got: ${JSON.stringify(r16.content)})`);
+
+    // UTF-8 BOM
+    const utf8bom = Buffer.concat([Buffer.from([0xEF, 0xBB, 0xBF]), Buffer.from('hello bom', 'utf8')]);
+    ok(decodeText(utf8bom) === 'hello bom', 'UTF-8 BOM stripped on decode');
+
+    // Genuine binary (PNG header + NULs) stays binary.
+    const png = Buffer.from([0x89, 0x50, 0x4E, 0x47, 0x0D, 0x0A, 0x1A, 0x0A, 0, 0, 0, 13, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
+    ok(decodeText(png) === null, 'binary with NULs stays binary');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[D] a hung fetch is bounded by the internal timeout');
+  {
+    // fetchImpl that never resolves UNLESS its signal aborts.
+    const client = createGitLabClient({
+      serverUrl: 'https://git.example', getCredentials: creds, timeoutMs: 400,
+      fetchImpl: (url, init) => new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
+        const sig = init && init.signal;
+        // A real fetch keeps the loop alive via its socket while pending;
+        // emulate that with a ref'd keepalive timer so AbortSignal.timeout
+        // (which is unref'd) actually gets a chance to fire in this test.
+        const keepalive = setTimeout(() => resolve(res({ ok: true, body: '{}' })), 10000);
+        if (sig) sig.addEventListener('abort', () => {
+          clearTimeout(keepalive);
+          reject(Object.assign(new Error('aborted'), { name: 'AbortError' }));
+        });
+      }),
+    });
+    const t0 = Date.now();
+    let settled = 'no';
+    try { await client.readFile({ projectPath: 'g/r', filepath: 'x' }); settled = 'resolved'; }
+    catch { settled = 'rejected'; }
+    const ms = Date.now() - t0;
+    ok(settled === 'rejected', `hung fetch rejected via timeout (got: ${settled})`);
+    ok(ms < 3000, `settled promptly (${ms}ms, timeout was 400ms)`);
+  }
+
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });

+/**
+ * HEADS UP, IN THE PAGE (mocked model): the developer types ten messages, a "Heads up" row appears; Accept puts its
+ * description in the message box, quoted, unsent; Dismiss and Not helpful are recorded; the next check is told each
+ * note and what came of it; a note that is not confident is not shown; the agent's own requests never carry a note.
+ *   [1] the flag on, ten messages typed in the page: the row, its description, Accept
+ *   [2] ten more: the next check carries N1 accepted; Dismiss
+ *   [3] ten more: N2 dismissed in the request; Not helpful; then a medium note is kept, not shown
+ *   [4] nothing of any note in the agent's requests; the outcomes survive a reload of the page
+ * Run: node test/test-heads-up-e2e.mjs
+ */

+/**
+ * HEADS UP (src/agent/heads-up.mjs; flag headsUp, off by default): every 10 developer messages a side agent reads the
+ * recent session as the agent saw it and raises at most one note. The developer, 2026-10-20.
+ *   [1] the count: a check after every 10th developer message; the state carried across a restart, checked
+ *   [2] what it reads: the task history kept, whole exchanges from the end within the limits
+ *   [3] the request: what it looks for, its past notes with their outcomes, the answer it must give
+ *   [4] reading a reply: a note, none, neither; a retry; only a confident note is shown; outcomes recorded
+ *   [5] a heads-up event never reaches the model's history
+ * Run: node test/test-heads-up.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+const home = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-headsup-'));

+const hu = await import('../src/agent/heads-up.mjs');
+const { historyFromChatEvents } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+console.log('\n[1] the count');
+{
+  const st = hu.newHeadsUpState();
+  const due = [];
+  for (let i = 1; i <= 25; i++) if (hu.headsUpCount(st)) { due.push(i); hu.headsUpChecked(st); }
+  ok(due.join() === '10,20', `a check after messages 10 and 20 (${due})`);
+  // (the developer, 2026-10-30: a check when idle resets the count - the next is ten messages after IT)
+  const s2 = hu.newHeadsUpState(), due2 = [];
+  for (let i = 1; i <= 25; i++) { if (hu.headsUpCount(s2)) { due2.push(i); hu.headsUpChecked(s2); } if (i === 4) hu.headsUpChecked(s2); }
+  ok(due2.join() === '14,24', `checked when idle after message 4: the next ones after 14 and 24 (${due2})`);
+  ok(s2.devSince === 25 && hu.normalizeHeadsUpState({ messages: 13 }).since === 3, 'the dev check counts on its own; a state from before keeps what the old rhythm had left to count');
+  const back = hu.normalizeHeadsUpState(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify({ ...st, notes: [{ id: 3, title: 't', description: 'd', confidence: 'high', shown: true, outcome: 'dismissed', atMessage: 10 }, { id: 'x' }, { id: 4, title: '' }] })));
+  ok(back.messages === 25 && back.notes.length === 1 && back.notes[0].outcome === 'dismissed' && back.nextId === 4, 'the count and the notes come back after a restart; a malformed note is dropped');
+  ok(hu.normalizeHeadsUpState(null).messages === 0 && hu.normalizeHeadsUpState({ messages: -3, notes: 'no' }).notes.length === 0, 'nothing usable: a fresh state');

+/**
+ * THE IMAGE WINDOW (src/release-ui.mjs, mode 'image'; the developer, 2026-10-30: the setup's window is for the command
+ * they run - `code-boss-setup.mjs image <switches>` - "all the --keep --no-push --run --kill-existing... can the ui take
+ * care of all this? again the flags set the config only if it's never been set before?"). In a real browser, against a
+ * stand-in for the creator:
+ *   [A] a first run: every switch's value in its field and saved; the switches ticked as given; push off for --no-push;
+ *       the switches that do nothing now said so, and not kept
+ *   [B] Build: the creator run as `image --no-ui` with the saved switches, its output line by line; --uninstall and
+ *       --yes only for that run
+ *   [C] a later run: the saved values and switches win; a different value, or a switch turned on, is a note to take
+ *   [D] the pure parts: imgParse knows every switch the creator takes (sandbox-image-cli SBI_VALUED / SBI_FLAGS), and
+ *       imgArgs gives back a command line the creator reads the same
+ * Run: node test/test-image-window-e2e.mjs
+ */

=== add test/test-imports-resolve.mjs
new 0121770258fe03e5e7aa1cd2
+/**
+ * Guard: every test and arena script must be able to find the modules it imports.
+ *
+ * WHY THIS EXISTS. On 2026-08-14 a live run against sonnet was set up to use test/_transcript-session.mjs and it
+ * could not start: the file imports src/agent/prompted.mjs and src/agent/git-workflow.mjs, both deleted on
+ * 2026-07-01 in two deliberate refactors. It had been dead for six weeks, and five other live suites with it,
+ * and nothing said so — because these files are not part of the CI run (`_`-prefixed probes and `live-*` suites
+ * need a proxy and a model, so run-all skips them). Rot in a thing nobody runs is invisible until somebody needs
+ * it, which is always the moment they can least afford to debug it.
+ *
+ * A test that CANNOT START is worse than one that fails: the failure looks like the caller's mistake. This is
+ * the cheapest possible thing that would have caught it on the day — resolve every relative import and check the
+ * file is there.
+ *
+ * STATIC ONLY, deliberately. These scripts boot servers, spawn browsers and call live models on import; running
+ * them to find out whether they parse would be a different and much worse test. Reading the import statements
+ * costs milliseconds and catches the entire class.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-imports-resolve.mjs
+ */
+import { readdir, readFile, stat } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+
+const ROOT = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
+
+async function scripts(dir) {
+  const out = [];
+  let entries = [];
+  try { entries = await readdir(path.join(ROOT, dir), { withFileTypes: true }); } catch { return out; }
+  for (const e of entries) {
+    const rel = dir + '/' + e.name;
+    // (a hidden folder is not ours: test/pii-contest/.cache holds a downloaded package, with an import of its own that
+    // does not resolve - 2026-10-28)
+    if (e.isDirectory()) { if (!e.name.startsWith('.')) out.push(...await scripts(rel)); continue; }
+    if (e.name.endsWith('.mjs')) out.push(rel);
+  }
+  return out;
+}
+
+// Static `import ... from '…'`, bare `import '…'`, and dynamic `import('…')` — all three appear in these files.
+const SPECIFIERS = /(?:^|\n)\s*import\s+(?:[\s\S]*?\sfrom\s*)?['"]([^'"]+)['"]|import\s*\(\s*['"]([^'"]+)['"]\s*\)/g;
+
+/**
+ * KNOWN DEBT, and the reason this is a list rather than a pass.
+ *
+ * These import modules that were deleted deliberately — prompted.mjs (the old agent loop, replaced by
+ * minimal-worker on 2026-07-01), plan-review.mjs and tasks.mjs (features removed outright). Repointing an import
+ * cannot fix them: they drive an agent through an interface that no longer exists, so each needs porting or
+ * retiring on its own merits, and several may simply be obsolete.
+ *
+ * Listing them rather than waiting to fix them is the point. The value of this guard is catching the NEXT
+ * deletion on the day it happens, and that value is available now; blocking it behind a dozen ports would mean
+ * the same six-week silence for whatever breaks next. A name here is a debt someone wrote down, not a name
+ * someone hid — and the list only shrinks.
+ *
+ * 2026-08-14, four came off: _transcript-session, _adversarial-skim-bench, live-powershell-write-repro and
+ * quality/harness now drive runMinimalWorker (which also unblocked harness's four importers). What each port
+ * dropped, and why, is commented at its call site.
+ *
+ * _cc-shadow-live-keystone was looked at in the same pass and deliberately NOT ported. It is not blocked on the
+ * agent interface: its entire pass bar is stats.shadowDiverge === 0 AND stats.shadowAgree > 0, emitted by the
+ * CC-MODEL shadow observer, which no longer exists anywhere in src/ — nor does its deterministic sibling
+ * test-cc-shadow-observer.mjs, nor the .code_boss/tasks/state.json it reads (the task store went with
+ * tasks.mjs in 63bcc76). Pointing it at runMinimalWorker would leave a probe whose one assertion reads an
+ * always-undefined field: it would fail forever, or pass vacuously once someone "fixed" that. It is a probe for
+ * a migration that was overtaken, and it should be retired rather than ported — but retiring it is a deletion,
+ * so it stays named here until someone decides that deliberately.
+ *
+ * BRANCH SKEW — read this before "cleaning up" the six names below that look dead. On flat-transport, checks [2]
+ * and [3] are RED for reasons that predate the port above, and both are cross-branch artifacts rather than debt
+ * anyone can pay here:
+ *   · _cc-marker-emission-probe, _live-manager-registry-capture, _live-plan-review-capture,
+ *     _plan-review-corpus-eval, live-protocol-experiment, measure-prompt-layout-cache do not exist on THIS
+ *     branch — 63bcc76 pruned the test corpus 233 → 79 — but they DO still exist, and still break, on main.
+ *     This list was written against main's file set. Deleting the names here would drop real debt on the merge.
+ *   · _role-flip-probe's import is repointed (prompted.mjs → worker-prompt.mjs) in an UNCOMMITTED working-tree
+ *     change. Taking it off the list before that lands turns check [2] amber into check [1] red.
+ * Both resolve themselves when the branches converge. Until then the honest state is a guard that names what it
+ * knows, not one made green by forgetting.
+ */
+const KNOWN_BROKEN = new Set([
+  // The only one left. Swapping its call to runMinimalWorker is trivial; the problem is that its entire pass bar
+  // is stats.shadowDiverge === 0 AND stats.shadowAgree > 0 from the CC-MODEL shadow observer, which appears
+  // nowhere in src/ — nor does its deterministic sibling, nor the task-store state file it reads. Ported, one
+  // assertion would pass vacuously and the other would fail forever. It is a probe for a migration that was
+  // overtaken and it should be RETIRED, which is a deletion someone should make deliberately rather than as a
+  // side effect of a port.
+  'test/_cc-shadow-live-keystone.mjs',
+]);
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[1] every relative import points at a file that exists\n');
+{
+  const files = [...await scripts('test'), ...await scripts('arena')];
+  const broken = [], known = [];
+  for (const f of files) {
+    const src = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, f), 'utf8');
+    // Comments would otherwise contribute phantom specifiers — several of these files document their own history.
+    const code = src.split('\n').filter((l) => { const t = l.trim(); return !(t.startsWith('//') || t.startsWith('*') || t.startsWith('/*')); }).join('\n');
+    for (const m of code.matchAll(SPECIFIERS)) {
+      const spec = m[1] || m[2];
+      if (!spec || !spec.startsWith('.')) continue;          // bare specifiers are node builtins or deps, not ours
+      // A cache-busting query (`./x.mjs?a=1`) is a legitimate specifier; strip it before touching the disk.
+      const target = path.resolve(path.dirname(path.join(ROOT, f)), spec.split('?')[0]);
+      try { await stat(target); } catch { (KNOWN_BROKEN.has(f) ? known : broken).push(`${f} -> ${spec}`); }
+    }
+  }
+  ok(broken.length === 0, `all ${files.length - KNOWN_BROKEN.size} live script(s) resolve${broken.length ? `\n      ${broken.join('\n      ')}` : ''}`);
+
+  // A name on the list that no longer breaks has been fixed, and leaving it there would let it rot again in
+  // silence — the exact failure this file exists to prevent, one level up. Same for one that was deleted.
+  const stale = [...KNOWN_BROKEN].filter((f) => files.includes(f) && !known.some((k) => k.startsWith(f + ' ')));
+  ok(stale.length === 0, `nothing on the known-broken list has been fixed without being taken off it${stale.length ? ' — remove: ' + stale.join(', ') : ''}`);
+  const gone = [...KNOWN_BROKEN].filter((f) => !files.includes(f));
+  ok(gone.length === 0, `and nothing on it has been deleted without its entry going too${gone.length ? ' — remove: ' + gone.join(', ') : ''}`);
+  if (known.length) process.stdout.write(`  (${known.length} known-broken import(s) across ${KNOWN_BROKEN.size} file(s) — listed in this file, with why)\n`);
+}
+
+process.stdout.write(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed\n`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

=== add test/test-infinite-scroll-multiround-probe.mjs
new ddde147686fbe68893c0f3f6
+/**
+ * Multi-round infinite-scroll backfill. The single-round case is covered by
+ * test-large-conversation-restore; this seeds 250 events (so backfill needs
+ * MULTIPLE rounds: 100 shown + 50 + 50 + 50 = 250) and drives real scroll-to-top
+ * repeatedly, asserting the very first message becomes reachable and the
+ * client correctly arms/disarms the scroll-back flags from hasMoreOlder.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-infinite-scroll-multiround-probe.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile, writeFile, mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { startServer } from '../src/server.mjs';
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29240;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+function ok(cond, label) {
+  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ok', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', label); }
+}
+function findChrome() {
+  const c = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe',
+             (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'];
+  return c.find(p => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const chrome = findChrome();
+  if (!chrome) { console.error('Chrome not found'); process.exit(2); }
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-msr-'));
+  await mkdir(path.join(dir, '.code_boss'), { recursive: true });
+  const N = 250;
+  const fakeEvents = [];
+  let id = 1;
+  fakeEvents.push({ id: `e${id++}`, appendedAtVersion: 1, type: 'user-message', content: 'THE FIRST MESSAGE' });
+  for (let i = 0; i < N - 2; i++) {
+    fakeEvents.push({ id: `e${id++}`, appendedAtVersion: id, type: 'assistant-text', content: `filler ${i}` });
+  }
+  fakeEvents.push({ id: `e${id++}`, appendedAtVersion: id, type: 'user-message', content: 'THE LAST MESSAGE' });
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, '.code_boss', 'conversation.json'), JSON.stringify({
+    version: 1, savedAt: new Date().toISOString(), nextEventId: id, chatEvents: fakeEvents,
+  }), 'utf8');
+
+  const html = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8');
+  const mosaicSrc = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'agent', 'mosaic-layout.mjs'), 'utf8');
+  const mosaicClean = mosaicSrc.replace(/^import\s+[^;]+;[ \t]*\r?\n?/gm, '')
+    .replace(/^export\s+(async\s+function|function|class|const|let|var)\b/gm, '$1');
+  const htmlWithConfig = html.replace('</head>',
+    '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script>' +
+    '<script>' + mosaicClean + '</script></head>');
+
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html: htmlWithConfig, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm',
+    port: PORT, adapter: { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: '' } }] }; } },
+    modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: dir,
+  });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    await page.setViewport({ width: 1100, height: 800 });
+    page.on('pageerror', (e) => console.log('     [pageerror]', e.message));
+    await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 500));
+
+    const initial = await page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.events?.length);
+    ok(initial === 100, `init window is 100 (got ${initial})`);
+    const armed = await page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.hasMoreOlder);
+    ok(armed === true, `hasMoreOlder true on init (got ${armed})`);
+
+    // Drive scroll-to-top repeatedly until either all 250 are loaded or we
+    // exhaust a sane number of rounds. Each round: set scrollTop=0, wait for
+    // the count to grow.
+    let last = initial;
+    let rounds = 0;
+    // Scroll back until the OLDEST SEEDED event is in the window. Counting to N instead stopped a round early as
+    // soon as the session's own rows (a plugin-load row on open) made up the difference, leaving the first two
+    // seeded messages unreachable — which is the exact thing this probe exists to catch.
+    const reachedTop = () => page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.events?.[0]?.id === 'e1');
+    while (rounds < 8) {
+      if (await reachedTop()) break;
+      const before = await page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.events?.length || 0);
+      await page.evaluate(() => { document.getElementById('logScroll').scrollTop = 0; });
+      try {
+        await page.waitForFunction((b) => (window.__sessionRef?.events?.length || 0) > b, { timeout: 4000 }, before);
+      } catch { break; }
+      rounds++;
+      last = await page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.events?.length || 0);
+    }
+    ok(last >= N, `all ${N} seeded events loaded after ${rounds} backfill rounds (got ${last})`);
+
+    // The very first message should now be in the DOM.
+    const firstUser = await page.$$eval('.bubble.user .bubble-content', (els) => els.map((e) => e.textContent.trim()));
+    ok(firstUser[0] === 'THE FIRST MESSAGE', `first message reachable at top (got: ${JSON.stringify(firstUser)})`);
+
+    // After exhausting, scroll-back should be disarmed (no more older).
+    const noMore = await page.evaluate(() => {
+      // _noMoreOlder isn't on window; infer by calling /api/events for the oldest.
+      return window.__sessionRef?.events?.[0]?.id;
+    });
+    ok(noMore === 'e1', `oldest is e1 (got ${noMore})`);
+
+  } finally {
+    await browser.close();
+    server.close();
+    await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }
+
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });
+
=== add test/test-input-draft-history-e2e.mjs
new 8038a732a0b303b2c6541d59
+/**
+ * THE CHAT BOX REMEMBERS (the developer, 2026-10-11) - the real UI, a scripted model.
+ *
+ *   [1] what is typed is kept per project, on the server (<project>/.code_boss/chat-input.json): a reload gives it
+ *       back - and so does the page at ANOTHER address (localStorage was per address: a port change lost it)
+ *   [2] sent messages are a history: on the top line, ONE ↑ does nothing to the text; TWO quick ↑ show the last
+ *       message and turn on one-press mode - then ↑ (top line) goes older, ↓ (bottom line) newer, and ↓ past the
+ *       newest puts back what was being typed and ends the mode
+ *   [3] a message of two lines: the first ↑ moves the caret up inside it, the next goes older
+ *   [4] a sent message clears the saved draft
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-input-draft-history-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+
+const ROOT = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
+process.env.CB_HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-inputhist-home-'));
+const PROJ = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-inputhist-proj-'));
+const PORT = 29840 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 20);
+const B = 'http://127.0.0.1:' + PORT;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+const adapter = {
+  async *completeStream(req) {
+    const c = String(req.messages[req.messages.length - 1].content);
+    const tag = (/--- you ([0-9a-z]{3,8}) ---\s*$/.exec(c) || [])[1] || 'zzzz';
+    yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: 'Noted.\n/--- you ' + tag + ' ---' } }] };
+  },
+  async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; },
+};
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8')).replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+const { server } = await startServer({ html, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: PROJ });
+const chrome = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe', (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'].find((p) => existsSync(p));
+const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+const page = await browser.newPage();
+const errs = []; page.on('pageerror', (e) => errs.push(e.message));
+const val = () => page.$eval('#input', (el) => el.value);
+const caret = () => page.$eval('#input', (el) => el.selectionStart);
+const status = async () => (await (await fetch(B + '/api/state?limit=1')).json()).status;
+const idle = async () => { await sleep(300); for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) { if ((await status()) !== 'running') return; await sleep(150); } };
+const typeIt = async (t) => { await page.focus('#input'); await page.keyboard.type(t); };
+const sendIt = async (t) => { await page.$eval('#input', (el) => { el.value = ''; }); await typeIt(t); await page.keyboard.press('Enter'); await idle(); };
+const up = () => page.keyboard.press('ArrowUp');
+const down = () => page.keyboard.press('ArrowDown');
+
+try {
+  await page.setViewport({ width: 1200, height: 900 });
+  await page.goto(B + '/'); await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 20000 }); await sleep(1200);
+
+  console.log('\n[1] the draft survives a reload');
+  await typeIt('half a thought about the tables');
+  await sleep(500);
+  await page.reload(); await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 20000 }); await sleep(1200);
+  ok((await val()) === 'half a thought about the tables', `after a reload the box has it back (${JSON.stringify(await val())})`);
+  const ciFile = path.join(PROJ, '.code_boss', 'chat-input.json');
+  const ci = () => { try { return JSON.parse(readFileSync(ciFile, 'utf8')); } catch { return {}; } };
+  ok(ci().draft === 'half a thought about the tables', `kept in the project's .code_boss/chat-input.json (${JSON.stringify(ci())})`);
+
+  console.log('\n[4] sending clears the saved draft');
+  await sendIt('first message');
+  await sleep(300);
+  ok(ci().draft === '' && (ci().history || []).includes('first message'), `sent: the saved draft is cleared, and the message is in the history (${JSON.stringify(ci())})`);
+  await sendIt('second message');
+  await sendIt('third message');
+
+  console.log('\n[2] a double ↑ goes back; then one press at a time');
+  await typeIt('what I am typing');
+  await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'what I am typing', 'one ↑ on the top line leaves the text alone');
+  await sleep(700); await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'what I am typing', 'two ↑ far apart do too');
+  await sleep(700); await up(); await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'third message', `two quick ↑: the last message (${await val()})`);
+  await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'second message', `then ONE ↑ goes older (${await val()})`);
+  await up(); await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'first message', `and stops at the oldest (${await val()})`);
+  await down();
+  ok((await val()) === 'second message', `one ↓ goes newer (${await val()})`);
+  await down(); await down();
+  ok((await val()) === 'what I am typing', `↓ past the newest puts back what was being typed (${await val()})`);
+  await sleep(700); await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'what I am typing', 'and the mode is over: one ↑ does nothing again');
+
+  // HOLDING ↑ (the developer, 2026-10-28: "holding the up arrow in the input text counts as double arrow and goes to
+  // the prev message in history ... holding shouldn't go to history"): the key's repeats are not presses
+  await sleep(700);
+  for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) await page.keyboard.down('ArrowUp');   // (held: after the first, each is a repeat)
+  await page.keyboard.up('ArrowUp');
+  ok((await val()) === 'what I am typing', `holding ↑ stays in the message (${JSON.stringify(await val())})`);
+  await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'what I am typing', 'and the one press right after letting go only arms');
+  await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'third message', `the press after that, soon, goes back as a double ↑ does (${await val()})`);
+  await down();
+  ok((await val()) === 'what I am typing', 'and ↓ comes back to what was being typed');
+
+  console.log('\n[3] a message of two lines');
+  await page.$eval('#input', (el) => { el.value = ''; });
+  await page.focus('#input');
+  await page.keyboard.type('line one'); await page.keyboard.down('Shift'); await page.keyboard.press('Enter'); await page.keyboard.up('Shift'); await page.keyboard.type('line two');
+  await page.keyboard.press('Enter'); await idle();
+  await sleep(700); await up(); await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'line one\nline two' && (await caret()) === 'line one\nline two'.length, `two quick ↑ show it, the caret at its end (${JSON.stringify(await val())})`);
+  await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'line one\nline two', 'the next ↑ moves the caret up inside it (it was on the bottom line)');
+  await up();
+  ok((await val()) === 'third message', `on the top line, one ↑ goes older (${await val()})`);
+  await down();
+  ok((await val()) === 'line one\nline two', `↓ comes back to it (${JSON.stringify(await val())})`);
+  console.log('\n[5] at another address (another port, another browser): the same draft and history');
+  await page.$eval('#input', (el) => { el.value = ''; }); await typeIt('typed at one address'); await sleep(700);
+  const other = await browser.newPage();

+  await other.setViewport({ width: 1200, height: 900 });

+  ok((await other.$eval('#input', (el) => el.value)) === 'line one\nline two', `and so is the history: a double ↑ shows the last message (${JSON.stringify(await other.$eval('#input', (el) => el.value))})`);

+/**
+ * THE CHAT INPUT PAINTS ITS OWN TEXT.
+ *
+ * For the retired /workflow command the textarea rendered with `color: transparent` over a backdrop div that
+ * mirrored its text and rainbow-highlighted the word "workflow". Removing the backdrop without removing the
+ * transparent rule would leave an input you can type into and never see — so this pins the visible colour, the
+ * absence of the mirror, and that a user bubble no longer sprouts per-letter spans around the word.

+import { mkdtemp, readFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';

+  'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',

+  'C:/Program Files (x86)/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });

+async function main() {
+  if (!chrome) { console.log('SKIP: Chrome not found'); process.exit(0); }
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-paint-'));
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8'))

+    html, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' },
+    defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter: { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; } },
+    modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir,
+  });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    const errors = [];
+    page.on('pageerror', (e) => errors.push(String(e && e.message || e)));
+    await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+    await page.waitForSelector('#input', { timeout: 10000 });
+
+    console.log('\n[1] the page script parses and the input is a normal, visible textarea');
+    ok(errors.length === 0, `no page errors on load (${errors.join(' | ').slice(0, 120)})`);
+    await page.type('#input', 'a workflow of my own');
+    const paint = await page.evaluate(() => {
+      const el = document.getElementById('input');
+      const cs = getComputedStyle(el);
+      return { color: cs.color, value: el.value, mirror: !!document.getElementById('inputHighlight') };
+    });
+    ok(paint.value === 'a workflow of my own', 'typing lands in the textarea');
+    ok(!/rgba\(\d+, \d+, \d+, 0\)|transparent/.test(paint.color), `and the text is painted, not transparent (${paint.color})`);
+    ok(!paint.mirror, 'there is no backdrop mirror to keep in step with it');
+
+    console.log('\n[2] the rotating placeholder no longer offers a command that does not exist');
+    const tips = await page.evaluate(() => (typeof IDLE_TIPS !== 'undefined' ? IDLE_TIPS : []));
+    ok(tips.length > 0, `the tips list is reachable (${tips.length})`);
+    ok(!tips.some((t) => /workflow/i.test(t)), 'and none of them mentions /workflow');
+    // the developer, 2026-10-23: "let's include the double tap of up arrow to go into history mode"
+    ok(tips.some((t) => /↑↑/.test(t) && /earlier messages/.test(t)), 'one tells of ↑↑ for the earlier messages');
+
+    console.log('\n[2b] the window back in focus: the message box, the cursor where it was (the developer, 2026-10-23: "if the code boss window wasn\'t in focus, but comes into focus, let\'s focus the text input w/ the cursor where it was previously")');
+    const back = await page.evaluate(async () => {
+      const i = document.getElementById('input');
+      i.value = 'first line\nsecond line'; i.focus(); i.setSelectionRange(6, 6);
+      i.blur();   // the window lost focus with the cursor in it, or the developer clicked elsewhere first
+      window.dispatchEvent(new Event('focus'));
+      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 50));
+      const a = { focused: document.activeElement === i, at: i.selectionStart };
+      // counter-example: a dialog open - left alone
+      i.blur();
+      const m = document.createElement('div'); m.className = 'modal-bg show'; document.body.appendChild(m);
+      window.dispatchEvent(new Event('focus'));
+      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 50));
+      const b = { focused: document.activeElement === i };
+      m.remove(); i.value = '';
+      return { a, b };
+    });
+    ok(back.a.focused && back.a.at === 6, `back in focus: the message box has it, the cursor where it was (${JSON.stringify(back.a)})`);
+    ok(!back.b.focused, 'not while a dialog is open');
+
+    console.log('\n[3] a user bubble renders the word plainly');
+    const spans = await page.evaluate(() => {
+      const d = document.createElement('div');
+      d.innerHTML = window.renderInline('start the workflow now', { userInput: true });
+      return { rainbow: d.querySelectorAll('.rainbow-word').length, text: d.textContent };
+    });
+    ok(spans.rainbow === 0 && /start the workflow now/.test(spans.text), 'no per-letter rainbow spans, the words intact');
+  } finally {
+    await browser.close();
+    server.close();
+    await rm(projectDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+    await rm(HOME, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });

+/**
+ * THE INSPECTOR (TEMPORARY - goes when src/inspector.mjs goes): a second agent the developer asks about the running
+ * app. It only looks; it has its own conversation; and - by the developer's explicit decision - what it looks at is
+ * sent to the model with REAL values unless a look asks for the agent's view.
+ *   [1] the protocol: <look .../> tags, rounds, what is kept of an earlier question's looks
+ *   [2] through a real server: real values by default, the agent's masked view on request, credentials refused,
+ *       nothing of it in the main conversation
+ * Run: node test/test-inspector.mjs

+/**
+ * WHAT THE AGENT IS TOLD ABOUT AN INTERRUPTION, and what happens when the developer types twice.
+ *
+ * The note used to fire on EVERY interruption and arrive as its own developer turn. A run cut off while the
+ * model was still writing has nothing to warn about — the partial reply is dropped, nothing ran — so the note
+ * was pure noise, and standing alone it stacked a second instruction on top of whatever the developer typed
+ * next. That is the shape behind the reported four-times-over reply.
+ *
+ * Now: only a KILLED TOOL is worth saying (the working tree may have changed half way), and it is prepended to
+ * the developer's next message rather than competing with it. Run: node test/test-interrupt-and-merge.mjs
+ */
+import { historyFromChatEvents, interruptNote } from '../src/server.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const text = (msgs) => msgs.map((m) => String(m.content || '')).join('\n');
+const devs = (msgs) => msgs.filter((m) => m.role === 'user' && !String(m.content).startsWith('<remote-workspace-result'));

+console.log('\n[1] interrupted while WRITING — nothing is said');

+    { type: 'assistant-text', content: 'Starting on it.', taskId: 'c', turn: 1 },
+    { type: 'run-interrupted', taskId: 'c', reason: 'error' },
+    { type: 'user-message', content: 'continue', taskId: 'c', turn: 2 },
+  ]);
+  ok(!/was cut off/.test(text(h)), 'no note — no tool ran, so there is nothing for it to check');
+  ok(devs(h).length === 1, `and the two developer turns merged into one (${devs(h).length})`);
+  ok(/do the thing/.test(text(h)) && /continue/.test(text(h)), 'both requests are still there');
+}

+console.log('\n[2] interrupted mid-TOOL — it is told, prepended to the next message');

+    { type: 'user-message', content: 'rewrite the config', taskId: 'c', turn: 1 },
+    { type: 'tool-call', verb: 'write', args: { path: 'config.js' }, callId: 'w1', taskId: 'c', turn: 1 },
+    // no tool-result for w1 — that is what "killed mid-flight" looks like in the record
+    { type: 'run-interrupted', taskId: 'c', reason: 'cancelled' },
+    { type: 'user-message', content: 'what happened?', taskId: 'c', turn: 2 },

+  ok(/what happened\?$/.test(String(last).trim()), 'it is PREPENDED to their message, which stays last');
+  ok(devs(h).length === 1 || !/^\[Note:/.test(String(devs(h)[0].content)), 'it is not a developer turn of its own');

+}

new c1aec7630a9f1237f918c7e0
+/**
+ * PRESS ESC AND SEE THAT YOU DID.
+ *
+ * The interruption is recorded — that part worked. It was in the wrong PLACE. /api/cancel appends the marker the
+ * instant Esc is pressed, but the aborted LLM call records its own transparency rows as the stream unwinds a
+ * moment later, so the marker ended up buried above them and the last row on screen was an llm receipt reading
+ * "1552 chars". Observed live: user-message, assistant-text, run-interrupted, tool-call, tool-result.
+ *
+ * Pressing Esc and seeing no sign of it is the entire complaint. The marker is appended immediately (a
+ * deferred version was tried and lost the marker outright when a run settled first), and the only rows allowed
+ * to land after it are the model-call receipts, which are hidden by default.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-interrupt-marker-order.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-int-home-'));
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+const PORT = 29291;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const state = async () => (await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/state`)).json());
+const events = async () => { const j = await state(); return j.events || j.chatEvents || []; };
+
+/**
+ * An adapter that streams slowly and — crucially — records its transaction AFTER the abort propagates, which is
+ * what the real anthropic adapter does in its `finally { emitTx() }`. Without that late row the bug cannot happen
+ * and the test would pass against the broken code.
+ */
+function lateTxAdapter(onTransaction) {
+  let workFirst = true;   // the first run runs a quick snippet, so its cancel lands on a run that DID something
+  return {
+    async *completeStream() {
+      try {
+        if (workFirst) { workFirst = false; yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: 'Checking.\n<remote-workspace><js>return 1</js></remote-workspace>' } }] }; return; }
+        for (let i = 0; i < 200; i++) { await sleep(120); yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: 'thinking… ' } }] }; }
+      } finally {
+        // The transparency pair the developer sees as "llm(worker turn 1 …) / ⎿ N chars".
+        try { onTransaction?.({ type: 'chat_completions', streamed: true, durationMs: 1, request: { model: 'm', messages: [] }, response: { choices: [{ message: { content: 'partial' } }] } }); } catch {}
+      }
+    },
+    async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; },
+  };
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-int-'));
+  let onTx = null;
+  const adapter = lateTxAdapter((t) => onTx?.(t));
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html: '<html></html>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' },
+    defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir,
+  });
+  // The server wraps the adapter with its own transaction hook; reach it the same way the real adapter would.
+  onTx = (t) => { try { adapter.__tx?.(t); } catch {} };
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[1] a cancelled run leaves the marker as the LAST event');
+    {
+      await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/message`, {
+        method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
+        body: JSON.stringify({ text: 'write a long design document', model: 'm' }),
+      });
+      for (let i = 0; i < 40 && (await state()).status !== 'running'; i++) await sleep(150);
+      ok((await state()).status === 'running', 'the run started');
+
+      // (an Esc before anything answered takes the message back instead - [1b]; this run does its snippet first)
+      for (let i = 0; i < 60 && !(await events()).some((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.verb !== 'llm'); i++) await sleep(150);
+      await sleep(600);
+      await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/cancel?scope=foreground`, { method: 'POST' });
+      for (let i = 0; i < 60 && (await state()).status === 'running'; i++) await sleep(200);
+      await sleep(1200);   // let every late row land
+
+      const ev = await events();
+      const idx = ev.map((e) => e.type).lastIndexOf('run-interrupted');
+      ok(idx >= 0, 'the interruption was recorded at all');
+      // WHAT MAY FOLLOW IT. Deferring the marker to the run's finally was tried and reverted: the run can
+      // settle between the abort and that finally, and then the marker never appeared at all - verified
+      // live, a real cancel produced none. Appending immediately is reliable, and the rows that used to
+      // bury it (the model-call transparency pair) are hidden by default now, so nothing lands VISIBLY
+      // after it. That is the invariant worth holding: anything appended later must be one of those.
+      const after = ev.slice(idx + 1);
+      const visibleAfter = after.filter((e) => !(e.verb === 'llm' || e.kind === 'llm'));
+      ok(visibleAfter.length === 0,
+        `only hidden model-call rows may follow it (found ${JSON.stringify(visibleAfter.map((e) => e.type))})`);
+      ok(after.every((e) => e.verb === 'llm' || e.kind === 'llm'),
+        `${after.length} row(s) after the marker, all of them hidden receipts`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[1b] a cancel before anything answered takes the message back, late rows included');
+    {
+      const before = (await events()).length;
+      await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/message`, {
+        method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
+        body: JSON.stringify({ text: 'NEVER_ANSWERED question', model: 'm' }),
+      });
+      for (let i = 0; i < 40 && (await state()).status !== 'running'; i++) await sleep(150);
+      await sleep(600);
+      const r = await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/cancel?scope=foreground`, { method: 'POST' })).json();
+      for (let i = 0; i < 60 && (await state()).status === 'running'; i++) await sleep(200);
+      await sleep(1200);   // the aborted call's receipt rows arrive after the cancel
+      const ev = await events();
+      ok(r.retracted === 'NEVER_ANSWERED question', `the cancel hands the text back (${JSON.stringify(r.retracted)})`);
+      ok(!ev.some((e) => e.type === 'user-message' && /NEVER_ANSWERED/.test(e.content || '')), 'the message is gone from the conversation');
+      ok(ev.length === before, `and nothing of that run is left, late receipts included (${before} -> ${ev.length}: ${JSON.stringify(ev.slice(before).map((e) => e.type + ':' + (e.verb || '')))})`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[2] cancelling with nothing running still records it immediately');
+    {
+      // The deferred path hangs off a run's finally; with no run there is no finally to hang off, so this must
+      // still take the direct path rather than silently recording nothing.
+      const before = (await events()).length;
+      await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/cancel?scope=foreground`, { method: 'POST' });
+      await sleep(400);
+      const ev = await events();
+      // Nothing was running, so `any` is false and there is nothing to record — the important part is that it
+      // does not throw, hang, or append a phantom interruption to a conversation that was never interrupted.
+      ok(ev.length === before, `no phantom marker for a cancel with nothing to cancel (${before} -> ${ev.length})`);
+      ok((await state()).status !== 'running', 'and the session is not left claiming to run');
+    }
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { server.close(); await rm(projectDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+
+  console.log('\n' + pass + ' passed, ' + fail + ' failed');
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();
