=== add test/test-interrupted-restart.mjs
new 8b3e0b1717b81414f4e69e02
+/**
+ * A RUN KILLED BY THE APP CLOSING MUST NOT COME BACK LOOKING FINISHED.
+ *
+ * Reported: repeated, re-worded answers — several model calls, each rephrasing the last, all landing in one
+ * message block — "after I cancel a response that's generating, or when the app closes".
+ *
+ * The app-closing half had nothing recording it. A cancel at least appends run-interrupted; a process that dies
+ * mid-reply appends nothing, because there is no code left running to append it. So the conversation came back
+ * ending on a half-finished assistant turn with no sign it never completed — and historyFromChatEvents drops an
+ * abandoned reply ONLY when it sees an interruption marker. With no marker, the model reads its own truncated
+ * sentence as a considered, acknowledged turn, and answers the next message by restating it.
+ *
+ * The fix is one flag: record whether a run was in flight at save time, and on load turn that into a real
+ * run-interrupted event. One append fixes both halves — the UI draws it, and the existing history logic uses it
+ * to drop the abandoned reply from the prompt.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-interrupted-restart.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-restart-home-'));
+const { startServer, historyFromChatEvents } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+const PARTIAL = 'Here is how the locking works, step by step. First, every mutating operation acquires';
+/** A conversation that ends exactly where a killed process would leave it: mid-reply, nothing after. */
+const EVENTS = [
+  { id: 1, type: 'user-message', taskId: 'conv', content: 'explain the locking' },
+  { id: 2, type: 'assistant-text', taskId: 'conv', turn: 1, content: PARTIAL },
+];
+
+async function boot(projectDir, port) {
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html: '<html></html>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' },
+    defaultModel: 'm', port, adapter: { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; } },
+    modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir,
+  });
+  for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { const r = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${port}/api/state`).then((x) => x.json()).catch(() => null); if (r) break; await sleep(100); }
+  return server;
+}
+// CONVERSATION events only. Opening a project also appends a plugin-load row (a tool-call and its result) so the
+// developer can see what is active — real, wanted, and nothing to do with what this test is about.
+const eventsOf = async (port) => {
+  const j = await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${port}/api/state`)).json();
+  return (j.events || j.chatEvents || []).filter((e) => e.verb !== 'plugin');
+};
+
+async function seed(projectDir, { runInFlight }) {
+  const dir = path.join(projectDir, '.code_boss');
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'conversation.jsonl'), EVENTS.map((e) => JSON.stringify(e)).join('\n') + '\n', 'utf8');
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'conversation.meta.json'),
+    JSON.stringify({ version: 2, savedAt: new Date().toISOString(), runInFlight, nextEventId: 3 }), 'utf8');
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-restart-'));
+  let server = await boot(projectDir, 29301);     // creates .code_boss
+  server.close();
+  await sleep(300);
+
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[1] a session that died MID-RUN comes back with the interruption recorded');
+    {
+      await seed(projectDir, { runInFlight: true });
+      server = await boot(projectDir, 29302);
+      const ev = await eventsOf(29302);
+      const marker = ev.find((e) => e.type === 'run-interrupted');
+      ok(!!marker, 'a run-interrupted event was reconstructed on load');
+      ok(marker?.reason === 'restart', `and says WHY, so the developer is not blamed for a stop they did not do (${marker?.reason})`);
+      ok(ev[ev.length - 1].type === 'run-interrupted', 'it sits at the end, after the reply it cut off');
+      server.close(); await sleep(300);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[2] and the half-finished reply is dropped from what the MODEL reads');
+    {
+      // This is the half that produces the symptom. Without the marker the model sees its own truncated sentence
+      // as its last considered turn and restates it; with it, the partial never reaches the prompt at all.
+      const withMarker = historyFromChatEvents([...EVENTS, { id: 3, type: 'run-interrupted', taskId: 'conv', reason: 'restart' }]);
+      const without = historyFromChatEvents(EVENTS);
+      const has = (msgs) => msgs.some((m) => String(m.content || '').includes('every mutating operation acquires'));
+      ok(has(without), 'without the marker, the abandoned reply IS in the prompt (the bug)');
+      ok(!has(withMarker), 'with it, the abandoned reply is gone (the fix)');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[3] a session that ended CLEANLY is left alone');
+    {
+      // The failure mode of an over-eager fix: marking every reload as an interruption would tell the developer
+      // their finished work was cut off, and would strip a completed reply out of the model's history.
+      await seed(projectDir, { runInFlight: false });
+      server = await boot(projectDir, 29303);
+      const ev = await eventsOf(29303);
+      ok(!ev.some((e) => e.type === 'run-interrupted'), 'no interruption invented for a clean shutdown');
+      ok(ev[ev.length - 1].type === 'assistant-text', 'and the last reply is still the last reply');
+      server.close(); await sleep(300);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[4] reloading twice does not stack markers');
+    {
+      await seed(projectDir, { runInFlight: true });
+      server = await boot(projectDir, 29304);
+      const first = (await eventsOf(29304)).filter((e) => e.type === 'run-interrupted').length;
+      server.close(); await sleep(400);
+      // The marker is now the last event, and the meta written on close reflects an idle session — so a second
+      // load must add nothing. A marker per restart would turn one interruption into a growing pile.
+      server = await boot(projectDir, 29305);
+      const second = (await eventsOf(29305)).filter((e) => e.type === 'run-interrupted').length;
+      ok(second === first, `still ${first} marker(s) after a second load (${first} -> ${second})`);
+      server.close(); await sleep(300);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[5] the flag is actually written when a run is in flight');
+    {
+      // If the save never records it, everything above is dead code.
+      const meta = JSON.parse(await readFile(path.join(projectDir, '.code_boss', 'conversation.meta.json'), 'utf8').catch(() => '{}'));
+      ok('runInFlight' in meta, `the meta carries the flag (${JSON.stringify(meta.runInFlight)})`);
+    }
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { try { server?.close(); } catch {} await rm(projectDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+
+  console.log('\n' + pass + ' passed, ' + fail + ' failed');
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();
+
=== add test/test-javac-javap-e2e.mjs
new 916cc6da3ddd692e98222f46
+/**
+ * javac + javap THROUGH THE REAL UI, compiling a real class with a real JDK.
+ *
+ * They are <js> host functions, like git/mvn/npm/node/java — NOT XML verbs. There is no <java> or <mvn> tag
+ * either; the only process verb is <run>, which is off in JS mode. So this drives them the way the agent
+ * actually reaches them: the model emits a <js> snippet in the browser, the snippet calls javac() and javap(),
+ * and the developer watches the result land in the chat.
+ *
+ * Asserted:
+ *   [1] javac COMPILES — a .class file appears on disk, and the snippet sees exit 0
+ *   [2] javap READS IT BACK — the disassembly names the method that was in the source
+ *   [3] a compile ERROR is DATA, not a throw — {code} is non-zero and stderr carries the compiler's message,
+ *       so a snippet can branch on it (the contract every runner here has)
+ *   [4] they are NOT XML verbs, and <javac …/> as a tag does not silently no-op
+ *   [5] the model is told they exist
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-javac-javap-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { cleanupDir } from './_helpers.mjs';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { startServer } from '../src/server.mjs';
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29263;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const chrome = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe', (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+
+const GOOD = `public class Greeter {
+  public String greet(String who) { return "hello " + who; }
+  public static void main(String[] a) { System.out.println(new Greeter().greet("world")); }
+}
+`;
+const BAD = `public class Broken {
+  public String oops() { return 1; }
+}
+`;
+
+// Keyed to the DEVELOPER MESSAGE that should trigger each, not to a call counter. Flat transport sends one user
+// message per request, so a mock that advances per model CALL runs every snippet inside the first developer
+// message — which is exactly what the first version of this test did. Route on the last marker instead.
+const SNIPPETS = [
+  ['compile Greeter', `Compiling it.
+<remote-workspace><js>
+const c = await javac(["-d", "out", "Greeter.java"]);
+console.log("javac exit " + c.code);
+return { step: "compile", code: c.code, stderr: (c.stderr || "").slice(0, 300) };
+</js></remote-workspace>`],
+  ['now javap it', `Reading the class back.
+<remote-workspace><js>
+const p = await javap(["-cp", "out", "Greeter"]);
+return { step: "javap", code: p.code, out: (p.stdout || "").slice(0, 600) };
+</js></remote-workspace>`],
+  ['compile the broken one', `Now the broken one.
+<remote-workspace><js>
+const b = await javac(["-d", "out", "Broken.java"], { allowFail: true });
+return { step: "bad", code: b.code, stderr: (b.stderr || "").slice(0, 300) };
+</js></remote-workspace>`],
+  ['try the tag', `And the tag form.
+<remote-workspace><javac src="Greeter.java"/></remote-workspace>`],
+];
+function pick(full) {
+  let best = null, at = -1;
+  for (const [key, body] of SNIPPETS) { const i = full.lastIndexOf(key); if (i > at) { at = i; best = body; } }
+  if (!best) return 'Nothing to do.';
+  if (/<remote-workspace-result/.test(full.slice(at))) return 'That step is done.';
+  return best;
+}
+const adapter = {
+  async *completeStream(req) {
+    const full = (req?.messages || []).map((m) => String(m?.content || '')).join('\n');
+    yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: pick(full) } }] };
+  },
+  async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'summary' } }] }; },
+  async listModels() { return [{ id: 'm' }]; },
+};
+
+async function main() {
+  if (!chrome) { console.log('SKIP — Chrome not found'); process.exit(0); }
+  try { execFileSync('javac', ['-version'], { stdio: 'ignore' }); }
+  catch { console.log('SKIP — no javac on PATH'); process.exit(0); }
+
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-javac-'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(projectDir, 'Greeter.java'), GOOD);
+  await writeFile(path.join(projectDir, 'Broken.java'), BAD);
+  await mkdir(path.join(projectDir, 'out'), { recursive: true });
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8'))
+    .replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  const B = `http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}`;
+  const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+  const settle = async (ms = 60000) => { const t0 = Date.now(); while (Date.now() - t0 < ms) { const s = await (await fetch(B + '/api/state')).json(); if (s.status !== 'running') return s; await sleep(150); } throw new Error('run did not settle'); };
+  const say = async (text) => { await fetch(B + '/api/message', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ text, model: 'm' }) }); return settle(); };
+  const jsResults = (s) => (s.events || []).filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.name === 'js_eval').map((e) => String(e.content || ''));
+
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    await page.setViewport({ width: 1200, height: 900 });
+    await page.goto(B + '/');
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+
+    console.log('\n[1] javac compiles a real class — the .class file lands on disk');
+    let s = await say('compile Greeter');
+    const r1 = jsResults(s).pop() || '';
+    ok(/"code":\s*0/.test(r1), `javac came back exit 0 (${r1.slice(0, 120)})`);
+    ok(existsSync(path.join(projectDir, 'out', 'Greeter.class')), 'out/Greeter.class exists — it really compiled, this is not a mock');
+    // What the developer SEES in the chat. A snippet's console.log streams live over SSE while the step runs,
+    // but progress is deliberately not persisted, so a SETTLED log shows the call and its result rather than the
+    // narration. Assert the part that is durable.
+    const shown1 = await page.evaluate(() => document.getElementById('log').textContent.replace(/\s+/g, ' '));
+    ok(/await javac\(/.test(shown1), 'the chat shows the javac call the snippet made');
+    ok(/js\(code=/.test(shown1) && /chars/.test(shown1), 'as a js tool row with a result, which is how the developer follows a run');
+
+    console.log('\n[2] javap reads the compiled class back');
+    s = await say('now javap it');
+    const r2 = jsResults(s).pop() || '';
+    ok(/"code":\s*0/.test(r2), `javap came back exit 0 (${r2.slice(0, 90)})`);
+    ok(/greet/.test(r2), 'the disassembly names the greet method that was in the source');
+    ok(/class Greeter/.test(r2), 'and the class itself');
+
+    console.log('\n[3] with {allowFail: true} a compile ERROR is data the snippet can branch on, not a throw');
+    s = await say('compile the broken one');
+    const r3 = jsResults(s).pop() || '';
+    ok(!/^ERROR:/.test(r3.trim()), `the snippet completed and returned its object rather than blowing up (${r3.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    ok(/"code":\s*[1-9]/.test(r3), 'the non-zero exit code came back as data');
+    ok(/incompatible types|error:/i.test(r3), 'with the compiler\'s own message in stderr');
+    ok(!existsSync(path.join(projectDir, 'out', 'Broken.class')), 'and nothing was written for the file that failed to compile');
+
+    console.log('\n[4] they are <js> functions, NOT XML verbs — same as java/mvn/npm/node');
+    s = await say('try the tag');
+    const tagEvents = (s.events || []).filter((e) => e.verb === 'javac');
+    const tagErr = tagEvents.find((e) => e.type === 'tool-result');
+    ok(tagEvents.some((e) => e.type === 'tool-call'), 'the unknown tag becomes a visible call row rather than vanishing from the transcript');
+    ok(tagErr && tagErr.status === 'error', `and its result is an ERROR, so the agent is told instead of believing it worked (${tagErr ? tagErr.status : 'no result row'})`);
+    ok(tagErr && /javac/i.test(String(tagErr.content || '')), `the message names the verb it could not run (${String((tagErr || {}).content || '').slice(0, 110)})`);
+    const { CORE_VERBS } = await import('../src/agent/tool-parser.mjs');
+    ok(!CORE_VERBS.includes('javac') && !CORE_VERBS.includes('javap'), `the parser has no javac/javap verb (${CORE_VERBS.filter((v) => /^java/.test(v)).join(',') || 'none'})`);
+    ok(!['java', 'mvn', 'npm', 'node'].some((v) => CORE_VERBS.includes(v)), 'and none for java/mvn/npm/node either — the new pair matches the family it joined, this is not an omission');
+
+    console.log('\n[5] the model is told they exist');
+    const { buildStaticPrompt } = await import('../src/agent/worker-prompt.mjs');
+    const prompt = String(buildStaticPrompt({ discussMode: false, workerMemory: null, checklist: null }));
+    ok(/javac/.test(prompt) && /javap/.test(prompt), 'the worker prompt names javac and javap');
+    const toolsSrc = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'agent', 'tools.mjs'), 'utf8');
+    ok(/javac\/javap\(argv\[\]/.test(toolsSrc), 'the js_eval tool description lists them alongside the other runners');
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.stack || e); }
+  finally {
+    await browser.close().catch(() => {});
+    await new Promise((r) => server.close(r));
+    await cleanupDir(projectDir).catch(() => {});
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();

+// GIT IN THE CONTAINER IS JGIT: our command line over it (sandbox-launcher/src/jgit) must answer as git does. Each
+// check runs the same command through real git and through JGit, on the same repository (reads) or on two identical
+// copies (writes), and compares what they printed - exactly where code_boss reads it back (status --porcelain,
+// rev-parse, diff, log --format, apply --cached --recount), and the resulting repository otherwise, read by real git.

+const must = (r, what) => { if (r.status !== 0) throw new Error(`${what}: exit ${r.status}: ${r.stderr || r.stdout}`); return r; };

+/**
+ * copy / move / remove calls fold into a line like reads do; a file WRITTEN or EDITED always keeps its own row.
+ *
+ * Live, the long session, 2026-10-09: "Delete the 200 generated notes" drew two hundred remove(…) rows; from then more
+ * than five folded. The developer, 2026-11-03: "File creates and edits should be the only thing that doesn't get
+ * rolled up" - now any number fold.
+ *
+ *   [1] 7 removes and 1 write: the write has its own row; the removes are one line ("7 files removed"), all seven
+ *       still there when it is opened
+ *   [2] a copy, a move and a remove: one line, no rows of their own
+ *   [3] after a reload the same
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-js-bulk-file-ops-e2e.mjs
+ */

+/**
+ * A SNIPPET IS ONE COLLAPSED LINE THAT SAYS WHAT IT DID.
+ *
+ * A <js> snippet used to render as a tool row full of code plus a result row, with nothing about the reads,
+ * writes and process runs inside it. Now it is one line with the dot — "Running…" — that becomes
+ * "2 reads, write, git" as the inner calls report in (a count of one is just the word - the developer, 2026-10-10) (each process by its name, comma separated), and a click opens it into the js
+ * row, its result, and one row per inner call. Drives the real UI with a real snippet.
+ *
+ *   [1] while it runs: ONE visible line, the summary, pulsing, with its "⎿ Running…" result line under it — the
+ *       js row's own result is hidden inside
+ *   [2] the summary counts the inner calls in the developer's words, as they complete
+ *   [3] it settles with the final tally and stops pulsing; every line has its ⎿ result line: the head line's is what
+ *       the snippet returned, a later line's is how many calls it holds (build 2156)
+ *   [3b] FILE CHANGES STAND ALONE (build 1961): write/edit are rows of their own between the summary lines, with
+ *        their diffs, visible without opening anything; the calls after them fold into a second line
+ *   [4] the RESULT line is what you click, as on a tool row (pointer, hover): it opens the js row + the folded calls,
+ *       in order, and closes them; a click on the line itself does the same, as on a call inside it
+ *   [5] a failed inner call is marked, and the snippet's own error turns the line's dot and result line red
+ *   [6] the open state survives a re-render; a reload shows the finished lines, each with its result line, closed
+ *       (as a tool row's open result is after a reload)
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-js-collapsed-rows-e2e.mjs
+ */

+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29277;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;

+// PLUGIN TOOLS BY THEIR OWN NAMES (the developer, 2026-10-11: "6 plugin calls" said nothing): two calls of one tool,
+// one of another, and a failed one of the second - "2 pick_color, pick_shape" and pick_shape struck through; and

+await pick_color({});
+await pick_shape({});
+await pick_shape({ fail: true }).catch(() => "caught");

+const PLUGIN_SRC = "export default { description: 'test tools', tools: ["
+  + "{ verb: 'pick_color', name: 'pick_color', schema: { description: 'pick a colour', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } }, impl: async () => ({ content: 'red' }) },"
+  + "{ verb: 'pick_shape', name: 'pick_shape', schema: { description: 'pick a shape', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: { fail: { type: 'boolean' } } } }, impl: async (a) => { if (a && a.fail) throw new Error('no shape'); return { content: 'square' }; } }"
+  + "] };";
+function pick(full) {
+  const iG = full.lastIndexOf('do the work'), iB = full.lastIndexOf('do the broken one'), iE = full.lastIndexOf('do the bad edit'), iP = full.lastIndexOf('do the plugin ones');
+  const at = Math.max(iG, iB, iE, iP); if (at < 0) return 'Nothing.';
+  if (/<remote-workspace-result/.test(full.slice(at))) return 'That is done.';
+  return at === iP ? PLUG : at === iE ? BADEDIT : iB > iG ? BROKEN : SNIP;
+}
+const adapter = {
+  async *completeStream(req) { const full = (req?.messages || []).map((m) => String(m?.content || '')).join('\n'); yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: pick(full) } }] }; },
+  async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 's' } }] }; },
+  async listModels() { return [{ id: 'm' }]; },
+};

+    ok(bad.parts.length === 2 && bad.parts[1].text === 'js' && bad.parts.every((p) => p.struck), `the script's own failure is "js", red and struck through, after the failed read (${JSON.stringify(bad.parts)})`);
+    ok(bad.stepErr.length === 2 && bad.stepErr[0] === false && bad.stepErr[1] === true, `the missing-file read is marked failed, the good read is not (${bad.stepErr.join(',')})`);

+    // 2026-10-30: "red is the script failed, green is it finished normally (maybe recovering from tool failures)")
+    ok(badEdit.caught && !badEdit.err, `marked failed, caught - muted, not red (${JSON.stringify({ err: badEdit.err, caught: badEdit.caught })})`);
+    ok(badEdit.head === 'read', `and not counted in the line before it (${JSON.stringify(badEdit.head)})`);

+    });

+    ok(!reloaded.open && !reloaded.segOpen, 'closed after a reload, as an opened tool result is (the open state lives in the page, not the conversation)');
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.stack || e); }
+  finally {
+    await browser.close().catch(() => {});
+    await new Promise((r) => server.close(r));
+    await cleanupDir(projectDir).catch(() => {});
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

=== add test/test-js-eval.mjs

+/**
+ * <js> capability verb — Phase 1 (JS-CAPABILITY-API-SPEC.md). Covers:
+ *   A. heredoc preprocessing (<<<TERM … TERM → literal string)
+ *   B. parser raw-text carve-out: <js> body with </>, backticks, <<< and ${ survives; several per block in order
+ *      (old-style tags beside them are no calls); no flag gates the one construct; unclosed <js>.
+ *   C. engine + host functions: search/read/git, return serialization.
+ *   D. RED-TEAM: a snippet cannot read a file outside cwd (PII boundary); cannot freeze the loop
+ *      (sync while(true) and async hang are both terminated); has no process/require in scope.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-js-eval.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile, mkdir, stat, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import { execSync } from 'node:child_process';
+import { createServer } from 'node:http';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+// Set BEFORE importing the engine (env is read at module load) so the red-team freeze test settles fast.
+process.env.CB_JS_EVAL_SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS = '1500';
+
+const { preprocessHeredocs, runJsEval } = await import('../src/agent/js-eval.mjs');
+// executeForModel, aliased: these assert each tool's ERROR TEXT, which is what the model reads. execute() itself
+// now THROWS for a call that cannot be made (inside a <js> snippet that is what a failure is) — see tools.mjs.
+const { executeForModel: execute, extractContent } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+const { parseToolCalls } = await import('../src/agent/tool-parser.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const jsCall = (text) => parseToolCalls(text).find((c) => c.name === 'js_eval');
+// A snippet's result is now FOLLOWED by the record of every call it made (run-record.mjs) — these checks are about
+// what the snippet RETURNED, so the record is cut off here; test-js-run-record.mjs is where the record is checked.
+const RECORD_HEAD = '\n\nWhat the snippet did, in order:\n';
+const resultOnly = (s) => { const i = String(s).indexOf(RECORD_HEAD); return i < 0 ? String(s) : String(s).slice(0, i); };
+const runIn = (code, ctx, budgetMs) => execute('js_eval', { code }, {}, ctx).then(extractContent).then(resultOnly).catch((e) => 'THREW: ' + e.message);
+
+async function main() {
+  const root = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-jseval-'));
+  const proj = path.join(root, 'proj');
+  await mkdir(proj, { recursive: true });
+  await writeFile(path.join(root, 'SECRET.txt'), 'PII: 555-12-3456');                 // OUTSIDE the project
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'a.js'), 'const x = 1; // TODO: fix\nexport {};\n');
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'b.md'), '# hello\nTODO later\n');
+  const ctx = { cwd: proj };
+  try {
+    // ── A. heredoc preprocessing ──
+    {
+      const out = preprocessHeredocs('const m = <<<END\nline "1"\n`tick` and ${x}\nEND\nfoo(m);');
+      ok(out.startsWith('const m = "line ') && out.includes('${x}') && out.includes('`tick`') && !out.includes('<<<') && out.includes('foo(m);'), `A1: <<<heredoc → literal JSON string, backticks/$\{} inert (${out.split('\n')[0]})`);
+      ok(preprocessHeredocs('x << y') === 'x << y', 'A2: a real left-shift `x << y` is untouched (only <<< is a heredoc)');
+      ok(preprocessHeredocs('no heredoc here') === 'no heredoc here', 'A3: code with no <<< is returned unchanged');
+      // A4-A7: a heredoc-SHAPED pattern inside a string/template/comment is the author's content — NOT substituted.
+      const inTpl = 'await write("f.txt", `title\n<<<END\nbody\nEND\n`);';
+      ok(preprocessHeredocs(inTpl) === inTpl, 'A4: a heredoc shape inside a template literal is left VERBATIM (not corrupted)');
+      const inStr = 'const s = "a <<<END\\nx\\nEND";';   // (single-line string, escaped newlines — no real heredoc)
+      ok(preprocessHeredocs(inStr) === inStr, 'A5: a <<< inside a double-quoted string is untouched');
+      const inComment = '// see <<<END\n// body\n// END\nlet y = 1;';
+      ok(preprocessHeredocs(inComment) === inComment, 'A6: a <<< inside line comments is untouched');
+      // A7: a REAL heredoc in code + a decoy inside an adjacent template → only the code one is substituted.
+      const mixed = 'const a = <<<Q\nhi\nQ\n; const b = `<<<Q\nnope\nQ\n`;';
+      const mo = preprocessHeredocs(mixed);
+      ok(mo.startsWith('const a = "hi\\n"') && mo.includes('`<<<Q\nnope\nQ\n`'), `A7: the code heredoc is substituted, the template decoy is preserved (${mo.replace(/\n/g, '⏎')})`);
+      // A8: a template with ${…} still scans correctly (nesting doesn't derail the code/literal tracking).
+      const withExpr = 'const z = `a ${ f(1) } b`; const h = <<<T\nZ\nT\n;';
+      ok(preprocessHeredocs(withExpr).includes('const h = "Z\\n"') && preprocessHeredocs(withExpr).includes('`a ${ f(1) } b`'), 'A8: ${…} template nesting is tracked; a following heredoc still substitutes');
+    }
+
+    // ── B. parser raw-text carve-out ──
+    {
+      const body = 'const a = 1 < 2 && 3 > 2;\nconst t = `x ${a} </js is fine inside a string-ish>`;\nreturn a;';
+      const c = jsCall(`<remote-workspace><js>${body}</js></remote-workspace>`);
+      ok(c && c.args.code.includes('1 < 2 && 3 > 2') && c.args.code.includes('`x ${a}'), 'B1: JS body with </>, &&, template literal is captured verbatim');
+      ok(c && !/data-jsref/.test(JSON.stringify(c.args)), 'B1b: the data-jsref placeholder is resolved away');
+    }
+    {
+      const c = jsCall('<remote-workspace><js>const p = <<<P\n--- a/x\n+++ b/x\nP\nreturn p;</js></remote-workspace>');
+      ok(c && c.args.code.includes('<<<P') && c.args.code.includes('--- a/x'), 'B2: a <<<heredoc inside <js> is kept in the body (preprocessed later, not by the parser)');
+    }
+    {
+      // The snippet is the ONE construct: several in a block parse in document order, and an old-style tag beside
+      // them (read/write) is not a call any more — it parses to nothing (the worker nudges it into a snippet).
+      const calls = parseToolCalls('<remote-workspace><read path="a.js"/><js>return 1;</js><write path="n.txt">hi</write><js>return 2;</js></remote-workspace>', { jsEval: true });
+      ok(calls.map((c) => c.name).join(',') === 'js_eval,js_eval' && calls.map((c) => c.args.code).join('|') === 'return 1;|return 2;', `B3: every <js> in a block parses, in document order, and the tags beside them are no calls (${calls.map((c) => c.verb).join(',')})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // There is no flag that turns the only construct off: the opts the old parser honoured are accepted and ignored.
+      const off = parseToolCalls('<remote-workspace><js>return 1;</js></remote-workspace>', { jsEval: false });
+      ok(off.length === 1 && off[0].name === 'js_eval', 'B4: with the (retired) flag OFF, <js> still parses — the snippet is the one construct, nothing gates it');
+    }
+    {
+      const c = jsCall('<remote-workspace><js>return 42; /* no close tag */');   // unclosed <js>
+      ok(c && c.args.code.includes('return 42'), 'B5: an unclosed <js> still captures its body as code');
+    }
+
+    // ── C. engine + host functions ──
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const hits = await search({ content: "TODO" }); return hits.map(h => h.file).sort();', ctx);
+      ok(/a\.js/.test(r) && /b\.md/.test(r), `C1: search({content}) returns structured hits (${r.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('return await search({ name: "*.md" });', ctx);
+      ok(/b\.md/.test(r) && !/a\.js/.test(r), 'C2: search({name}) does a filename search');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const t = await read("a.js"); return t.includes("TODO");', ctx);
+      ok(r === 'true', `C3: read() reads a file inside the project (${r})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // requestTool() was RETIRED: it only ever returned "noted — continue with the tools you have", which taught
+      // the agent to dead-end instead of building the capability with <author-plugins/>. It must be gone entirely.
+      const r = await runIn('const x = await requestTool("need keytool"); return x;', ctx);
+      ok(/requestTool is not defined/.test(r), `C4: requestTool is retired — no longer in scope (${r.slice(0, 70).replace(/\n/g, ' ')})`);
+      ok(!/Available here:.*requestTool/.test(r), 'C4b: it is not advertised in the sandbox-error function list either');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('return { a: 1, b: [2, 3], nested: { ok: true } };', ctx);
+      ok(/"a":1/.test(r) && /"nested":\{"ok":true\}/.test(r), `C5: an object return is JSON-serialized (${r})`);
+    }
+    // git host fn against a real temp repo
+    {
+      execSync('git init -q', { cwd: proj });
+      execSync('git config user.email t@t.co && git config user.name t', { cwd: proj, shell: 'cmd.exe' });

+      ok(/"code":0/.test(r) && /"hasA":true/.test(r), `C6: git(argv) runs in the project repo (${r.slice(0, 90)})`);
+    }

+    }

+    {

+      ok(/LEAKED:/.test(r) && /555-12-3456/.test(r), `D1: read() of a file outside the project is answered - where the agent may go is the container's decision now (${r.slice(0, 40)})`);
+    }

+    }
+    {

+      ok(/"p":"undefined"/.test(r) && /"g":"undefined"/.test(r), `D4: the snippet has NO process / globalThis.process (${r})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // require EXISTS but only as a guard that throws a helpful message — it must never RESOLVE a module. Proving
+      // it grants nothing matters more than proving it is undefined (that was the old, less useful assertion).
+      // A require() is now refused by the host-side PRE-FLIGHT before the snippet runs at all, so the snippet's
+      // own try/catch never even gets to see it — the guarantee (no Node module ever resolves) is stronger, not
+      // weaker: the code did not execute.
+      const r = await runIn('try { const fs = require("fs"); return { escaped: typeof fs }; } catch (e) { return { threw: true }; }', ctx);
+      ok(/refused BEFORE running/.test(r) && !/escaped/.test(r), `D4b: require() never resolves a module — refused pre-flight (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const t0 = Date.now();
+      const r = await runJsEval({ code: 'while (true) {}', ctx, hostFns: {}, budgetMs: 8000 });
+      ok(/ERROR/.test(r) && (Date.now() - t0) < 6000, `D5: a sync while(true) is terminated (${Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 100) / 10}s, ${r.slice(0, 60)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const t0 = Date.now();
+      const r = await runJsEval({ code: 'await new Promise(() => {}); return 1;', ctx, hostFns: {}, budgetMs: 2000 });
+      ok(/ERROR/.test(r) && /budget|terminated/i.test(r) && (Date.now() - t0) < 5000, `D6: an async hang hits the wall-clock budget and is terminated (${Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 100) / 10}s)`);
+    }
+    {
+      // A STOP DOES NOT UNDO A CALL ALREADY MADE (live, 2026-10-30: Esc during an edit - it landed a moment later, and the

+      const ac = new AbortController(), seen = [];
+      const slow = async () => { await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 600)); return { ok: true }; };
+      setTimeout(() => ac.abort(), 200);
+      const t0 = Date.now();
+      const r = await runJsEval({ code: 'await slowEdit(); await slowEdit(); return 1;', ctx: { ...ctx, signal: ac.signal, onStep: (e) => seen.push(e.phase + ':' + e.step) }, hostFns: { slowEdit: slow }, budgetMs: 10000 });
+      const ms = Date.now() - t0;
+      ok(/aborted/.test(String(r)) && seen.join(',') === 'start:1,end:1' && ms >= 500 && ms < 3000, `D6b: stopped mid-call, the call ends and is reported before the result; the next never starts (${seen.join(',')}, ${ms}ms)`);
+    }
+    {
+      // A snippet that RETURNS "ERROR: …" succeeded: the string is its data, not a failure of the call.
+      const r = await runJsEval({ code: 'return "ERROR: 3 tests failed"', ctx, hostFns: {} });
+      ok(typeof r === 'object' && r.content === 'ERROR: 3 tests failed', 'D7: a returned "ERROR: …" string comes back as { content } data, not as the bare string a failure is');
+      const f = await runJsEval({ code: 'throw new Error("boom")', ctx, hostFns: {} });
+      ok(typeof f === 'string' && /^ERROR: /.test(f) && /boom/.test(f), 'D7b: a snippet that THROWS is still the "ERROR: …" failure string');
+    }
+    {
+      // A PROMISE PUT INTO TEXT is refused, not passed on (live, 2026-10-24: a file named "[object Promise]" in the project)
+      const ran = [];
+      const host = { tmp: async () => 'D:/x/.code_boss/tmp/a.csv', node: async ([argv]) => { ran.push(argv); return { code: 0, stdout: 'ok' }; } };
+      const r = await runJsEval({ code: 'const f = tmp("a.csv"); await node(["-e", `copy(\'${f}\')`]); return "done";', ctx, hostFns: host });
+      ok(typeof r === 'string' && /node\(\): an argument holds "\[object Promise\]"[^]*await it first/.test(r) && !ran.length, `D7c: an un-awaited value put into an argument stops the call, and says to await it (${String(r).slice(0, 120)})`);
+      const good = await runJsEval({ code: 'const f = await tmp("a.csv"); await node(["-e", `copy(\'${f}\')`]); return "done";', ctx, hostFns: host });
+      ok(good === 'done' && ran.length === 1 && /a\.csv/.test(ran[0][1]), 'D7d: awaited, it goes through');
+    }
+
+    // ── E. RED-TEAM HARDENING (from the adversarial review) ──
+    {
+      // fork-bomb: a total-spawn ceiling fails fast instead of spawning thousands of children.
+      process.env.CB_JS_ARGV_MAX_TOTAL = '3';
+      const r = await runIn('try { await Promise.all([1,2,3,4,5,6].map(() => git(["--version"]))); return "ALL RAN"; } catch (e) { return "CAPPED: " + e.message; }', ctx);
+      delete process.env.CB_JS_ARGV_MAX_TOTAL;
+      ok(/CAPPED/.test(r) && /too many process.network calls/i.test(r), `E1: a process fork-bomb hits the per-eval spawn ceiling (${r.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // the concurrency gate serializes without deadlocking — 8 git calls all complete.
+      const r = await runIn('const rs = await Promise.all(Array.from({length:8}, () => git(["--version"]))); return rs.every(x => x.code === 0);', ctx);
+      ok(r === 'true', `E2: the concurrency gate runs many calls to completion without deadlock (${r})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await git(["-c", "alias.x=!calc", "x"]); return "RAN"; } catch (e) { return "REFUSED: " + e.message; }', ctx);
+      ok(/REFUSED/.test(r) && /config injection/i.test(r), `E3: git -c runtime config injection is refused (arbitrary-exec vector) (${r.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // mvn via cmd.exe /c: a bare & token would inject an arbitrary command — refused BEFORE spawning (no Maven needed).
+      const r = await runIn('const x = await mvn(["clean", "&", "calc"]); return x.stderr;', ctx);
+      ok(/metacharacter/i.test(r) && /&/.test(r), `E4: a cmd.exe metacharacter token in mvn() is refused (${r.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await git(["add", "a/../../../SECRET.txt"]); return "RAN"; } catch (e) { return "BLOCKED"; }', ctx);
+      ok(r === 'BLOCKED', 'E5: a MID-token `..` traversal in an argv is bound-checked (not just leading `..`)');
+    }
+    {
+      // parser: a </remote-workspace> inside a <js> body must NOT cut the block early (carve-out runs first).
+      const c = jsCall('<remote-workspace><js>const s = "</remote-workspace>"; return s.length;</js></remote-workspace>');
+      ok(c && c.args.code.includes('"</remote-workspace>"') && c.args.code.includes('return s.length'), 'E6: </remote-workspace> inside a <js> string does not truncate the snippet');
+    }
+    {
+      // parser: a > inside a <js> attribute value does not mis-close the open tag (quote-aware match).
+      const c = jsCall('<remote-workspace><js note="a>b">return 7;</js></remote-workspace>');
+      ok(c && c.args.code.includes('return 7'), 'E7: a `>` inside a <js> attribute does not mis-close the tag');
+    }
+
+    // ── F. NEW FUNCTIONS (npm/node/java/curl + fs mutations + clone) ──
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const n = await node(["-e", "process.stdout.write(\'NODE_OK\')"]); return { code: n.code, out: n.stdout };', ctx);
+      ok(/"code":0/.test(r) && /NODE_OK/.test(r), `F1: node(argv) runs the node binary (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await curl("file:///C:/Windows/win.ini"); return "RAN"; } catch (e) { return "REFUSED: " + e.message; }', ctx);
+      ok(/REFUSED/.test(r) && /http/i.test(r), `F2: curl refuses file:// (no local-file read via curl) (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // a live local server: curl fetches it and gets the body
+      const srv = createServer((req, res) => { res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/plain' }); res.end('PONG ' + req.method); });
+      await new Promise((r) => srv.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', r));
+      const port = srv.address().port;
+      try {
+        const r = await runIn(`const x = await curl("http://127.0.0.1:${port}/health"); return { status: x.status, body: x.body };`, ctx);
+        ok(/"status":200/.test(r) && /PONG GET/.test(r), `F3: curl GETs a live http server (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+      } finally { srv.close(); }
+    }
+    {
+      await runIn('await mkdir("newdir/sub"); return 1;', ctx);
+      ok(existsSync(path.join(proj, 'newdir', 'sub')), 'F4: mkdir creates a directory inside the project');
+    }
+    {
+      await runIn('await copy("a.js", "copy-of-a.js"); return 1;', ctx);
+      ok(existsSync(path.join(proj, 'copy-of-a.js')), 'F5: copy duplicates a file inside the project');
+    }
+    {
+      await runIn('await move("copy-of-a.js", "newdir/moved.js"); return 1;', ctx);
+      ok(existsSync(path.join(proj, 'newdir', 'moved.js')) && !existsSync(path.join(proj, 'copy-of-a.js')), 'F6: move relocates a file');
+    }
+    {
+      await runIn('await remove("newdir"); return 1;', ctx);
+      ok(!existsSync(path.join(proj, 'newdir')), 'F7: remove(dir) deletes a directory tree, no flag');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await mkdir("../escape"); return "RAN"; } catch (e) { return "BLOCKED"; }', ctx);
+      ok(r === 'RAN' && existsSync(path.join(root, 'escape')), 'F8: a mutation (mkdir) outside the project is done');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await copy("a.js", "../copied-out.txt"); return "RAN"; } catch (e) { return "BLOCKED"; }', ctx);
+      ok(r === 'RAN' && existsSync(path.join(root, 'copied-out.txt')), 'F9: copy to an out-of-project destination is done');
+    }
+
+    // ── G. BACKGROUND HANDLES ({background:true} → handle with waitFor/status/kill) ──
+    {
+      const code = [
+        'const srv = await node(["-e", "console.log(\'SERVER-READY\'); setInterval(()=>{}, 1000);"], { background: true });',
+        'try {',
+        '  const w = await srv.waitFor(/SERVER-READY/, { timeoutMs: 6000 });',
+        '  const st = await srv.status();',
+        '  return { hasId: typeof srv.id === "string", hasPid: !!srv.pid, matched: w.matched, alive: st.processes[0].alive };',
+        '} finally { await srv.kill(); }',
+      ].join('\n');
+      const r = await runIn(code, ctx);
+      ok(/"hasId":true/.test(r) && /"hasPid":true/.test(r) && /"matched":true/.test(r) && /"alive":true/.test(r), `G1: {background:true} returns a handle; waitFor matches streamed output; status shows alive; kill stops it (${r.slice(0, 110)})`);
+    }
+
+    {
+      // portInUse: a fresh high port is free; a port we're listening on reads as in-use.
+      const s2 = createServer(() => {}); await new Promise((r) => s2.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', r));
+      const usedPort = s2.address().port;
+      try {
+        const r = await runIn(`return { used: await portInUse(${usedPort}), free: await portInUse(59999) };`, ctx);
+        ok(/"used":true/.test(r) && /"free":false/.test(r), `G2: portInUse detects a listening vs free port (${r.slice(0, 60)})`);
+      } finally { s2.close(); }
+    }
+
+    // ── H. there is NO shell, under any name (the PII boundary: no raw shell to escape a function) ──
+    {
+      const { tools: allTools } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+      ok(!allTools.run && !allTools.powershell && !allTools.spawn_background, 'H1: no run/powershell/spawn_background tool exists any more');
+      const refused = extractContent(await execute('run', { command: 'Get-Content ../secret.txt' }, {}, ctx));
+      ok(/unknown tool/i.test(refused), `H1b: executing "run" is an unknown-tool error (${refused.slice(0, 60)})`);
+    }
+
+    // ── I. write/edit in <js> (delegating to the safe impls) + edit→build gating + red-team-#2 fixes ──
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const w = await write("gen/out.txt", "hello\\nworld"); return { ok: w.ok, hasDiff: !!w.diff };', ctx);
+      ok(/"ok":true/.test(r) && /"hasDiff":true/.test(r) && existsSync(path.join(proj, 'gen', 'out.txt')), `I1: write() creates a file (boundary + diff) (${r.slice(0, 60)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const e = await edit("a.js", "const x = 1", "const x = 2"); return { ok: e.ok };', ctx);
+      ok(/"ok":true/.test(r), `I2: edit() applies an exact-match change (${r})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const e = await edit("a.js", "NOT_PRESENT_ANYWHERE", "x"); if (!e.ok) return { skippedBuild: true, err: e.error }; return { ranBuild: true };', ctx);
+      ok(/"skippedBuild":true/.test(r) && /not found/i.test(r) && !/ranBuild/.test(r), `I3: a FAILED edit gates OUT the build (edit→build chaining) (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await mvn(["clean","&","calc"], { background: true }); return "RAN"; } catch (e) { return "REFUSED: " + e.message; }', ctx);
+      ok(/REFUSED/.test(r) && /metacharacter/i.test(r), `I4: a cmd metacharacter in a BACKGROUND mvn/npm is refused (red-team #2 fix) (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+    }
+
+    // ── K. process-call OPTIONS: cwd alias + no silently-ignored keys ──────────────────────────────────────────
+    // Seen live: npm(["install"], { cwd: "./browser_testing_plugin" }) ran in the PROJECT ROOT and died with an
+    // ENOENT for the root's package.json — `cwd` was silently dropped because the API key is `dir`. A dropped
+    // option is the worst failure mode: the call "succeeds" while doing something else entirely.
+    await mkdir(path.join(proj, 'sub'), { recursive: true });
+    await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'sub', 'whereami.js'), 'console.log(process.cwd());');
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const r = await node(["whereami.js"], { cwd: "sub" }); return r.stdout.trim();', ctx);
+      ok(/[\\/]sub$/.test(r.trim()), `K1: { cwd } is honored as an alias for { dir } (${r.trim().slice(-40)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const r = await node(["whereami.js"], { dir: "sub" }); return r.stdout.trim();', ctx);
+      ok(/[\\/]sub$/.test(r.trim()), `K2: { dir } still works (${r.trim().slice(-40)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const r = await node(["whereami.js"], { dir: "sub", cwd: "." }); return r.stdout.trim();', ctx);
+      ok(/[\\/]sub$/.test(r.trim()), 'K3: an explicit dir WINS over cwd (no silent override)');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await node(["whereami.js"], { workingDir: "sub" }); return "NO ERROR"; } catch (e) { return e.message; }', ctx);
+      ok(/unknown option/i.test(r) && /"workingDir"/.test(r) && /would have been IGNORED/i.test(r) && /dir \(or cwd/.test(r),
+        `K4: an unknown option is REFUSED with the accepted list, not silently ignored (${r.slice(0, 90)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // The whole point: a misspelled option must never run the command in the wrong place.
+      const r = await runIn('try { await node(["whereami.js"], { cwdir: "sub" }); return "RAN ANYWAY"; } catch (e) { return "REFUSED"; }', ctx);
+      ok(r.includes('REFUSED'), 'K5: a near-miss spelling does NOT run the command in the wrong directory');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const r = await npm(["--version"], { cwd: "sub" }); return String(r.code);', ctx);
+      ok(r.trim() === '0', `K6: npm accepts { cwd } too (the reported case) (code ${r.trim()})`);
+    }
+    // Seen live (gemini-3.1-pro, 2026-08-10): git(["grep","-i","distance"], "portlet-pcm") — the string in the
+    // options slot, which is what execFile(cmd, args, cwd) muscle memory produces. It was spread into character
+    // keys, so the model got back `unknown options "0","1",…,"10"` — one index per letter of its own directory
+    // name, saying nothing about the fix. dir is the only string-shaped option there is, so read it as dir.
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const r = await node(["whereami.js"], "sub"); return r.stdout.trim();', ctx);
+      ok(/[\\/]sub$/.test(r.trim()), `K7: a BARE STRING in the option slot is read as dir (${r.trim().slice(-40)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // If some other non-object ever lands there, the message must name the CAUSE, not enumerate indices.
+      const r = await runIn('try { await node(["whereami.js"], ["sub"]); return "NO ERROR"; } catch (e) { return e.message; }', ctx);
+      ok(/non-object/.test(r) && !/"0", "1"/.test(r), `K8: a non-object option bag is explained, not listed index by index (${r.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    }
+
+    // ── K2. list() — the readdir the sandbox was missing ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    // Seen live in the same run: `const files = fs.readdirSync(dir)`. It was not carelessness — <js> had NO
+    // directory listing, so there was nothing correct to reach for; search({name}) is a glob, not a listing.
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const e = await list("sub"); return JSON.stringify(e);', ctx);
+      ok(/"name":"whereami\.js"/.test(r) && /"dir":false/.test(r), `K9: list(dir) returns the entries as objects (${r.slice(0, 80)})`);
+      ok(/"size":\d+/.test(r), 'K10: file entries carry a byte size');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const e = await list("."); return JSON.stringify(e.filter((x) => x.dir).map((x) => x.name));', ctx);
+      ok(/"sub"/.test(r) && !/\//.test(r), 'K11: directories are flagged dir:true and the trailing slash is stripped from the name');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const e = await list(); return String(Array.isArray(e) && e.length > 0);', ctx);
+      ok(r.trim() === 'true', 'K12: list() with no argument lists the project root');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const e = await list("sub"); return String(e.length);', ctx);
+      const n = Number(r.trim());
+      ok(Number.isFinite(n) && n >= 1, `K13: the count matches what is really there (${r.trim()})`);
+    }
+
+    // ── L. curl ARGV form + insecure ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    // Seen live: curl(["-k","-I","https://localhost:7102/x"]) → "invalid URL: -k,-I,https://…". Every OTHER
+    // runner takes an argv array and curl is a CLI the model knows cold, so the array shape is the natural
+    // reach; and -k was the POINT (a self-signed localhost dev server was otherwise unreachable at all).
+    {
+      const { curlArgvToOpts } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+      const t = curlArgvToOpts(['-k', '-I', 'https://localhost:7102/milconnect']);
+      ok(t.url === 'https://localhost:7102/milconnect' && t.opt.insecure === true && t.opt.method === 'HEAD',
+        `L1: the reported argv translates (url + insecure + HEAD) (${JSON.stringify(t.opt)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const { curlArgvToOpts } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+      const t = curlArgvToOpts(['-X', 'POST', '-H', 'Content-Type: application/json', '-d', '{"a":1}', 'http://h/x']);
+      ok(t.opt.method === 'POST' && t.opt.headers['Content-Type'] === 'application/json' && t.opt.body === '{"a":1}', 'L2: -X / -H / -d translate');
+      const t2 = curlArgvToOpts(['-d', 'x=1', 'http://h/x']);
+      ok(t2.opt.method === 'POST', 'L3: -d implies POST when no -X is given');
+    }
+    {
+      const { curlArgvToOpts } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+      let threw = ''; try { curlArgvToOpts(['--zorp', 'http://h/']); } catch (e) { threw = e.message; }
+      ok(/unsupported flag/.test(threw) && /--insecure/.test(threw), `L4: an unsupported flag is REFUSED with the supported list, never ignored (${threw.slice(0, 60)})`);
+      let t2 = ''; try { curlArgvToOpts(['-o', 'f.txt', 'http://h/']); } catch (e) { t2 = e.message; }
+      ok(/write\(path, res\.body\)/.test(t2), 'L5: -o points at write(path, res.body) instead of silently dropping the redirect');
+      let t3 = ''; try { curlArgvToOpts(['-k']); } catch (e) { t3 = e.message; }
+      ok(/no URL in the argv/.test(t3), 'L6: argv with no URL is a clear error');
+      // -i asks real curl to print the response headers above the body. We ALWAYS return {status, ok, headers,
+      // body}, so it asks for nothing we are not already doing — but it was rejected as an unsupported flag,
+      // which is how a working request came back as an error (reported 2026-09-19). -I (HEAD) was supported the
+      // whole time, so the lowercase sibling failing was the kind of inconsistency nobody can guess their way past.
+      const ti = curlArgvToOpts(['-i', 'http://h/x']);
+      ok(ti.url === 'http://h/x' && ti.opt.method === undefined, `L6b: -i is accepted and changes nothing about the request (${JSON.stringify(ti.opt)})`);
+      const tI = curlArgvToOpts(['-I', 'http://h/x']);
+      ok(tI.opt.method === 'HEAD', 'L6c: and -I still means HEAD — the two are not confused with each other');
+      const tmix = curlArgvToOpts(['-i', '-s', '--compressed', '-N', '-k', '-X', 'PUT', 'http://h/x']);
+      ok(tmix.opt.method === 'PUT' && tmix.opt.insecure === true, `L6d: -i/-s/--compressed/-N pass through beside the flags that DO matter (${JSON.stringify(tmix.opt)})`);
+      let t4 = ''; try { curlArgvToOpts(['--zorp', 'http://h/']); } catch (e) { t4 = e.message; }
+      ok(/-i/.test(t4) && /headers/.test(t4), `L6e: and the unsupported-flag message now names -i, so the next model does not have to guess (${t4.slice(-90)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('try { await curl(["--nope", "http://x/"]); return "NO ERROR"; } catch (e) { return e.message; }', ctx);
+      ok(/unsupported flag/.test(r), `L7: the argv form is wired into curl() end to end (${r.slice(0, 60)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // END TO END against a real server: curl -i must come back with a real response, and the headers the flag
+      // asks for must be in it — that is WHY -i can be ignored rather than implemented.
+      const http = await import('node:http');
+      const srv = http.createServer((req, res) => { res.writeHead(201, { 'content-type': 'text/plain', 'x-probe': 'yes' }); res.end('BODY-OK ' + req.method); });
+      await new Promise((r) => srv.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', r));
+      const port = srv.address().port;
+      try {
+        const r = await runIn(`const res = await curl(["-i", "http://127.0.0.1:${port}/x"]); return JSON.stringify({ status: res.status, ok: res.ok, body: res.body, ct: res.headers && (res.headers["content-type"] || res.headers["Content-Type"]), probe: res.headers && (res.headers["x-probe"] || res.headers["X-Probe"]) });`, ctx);
+        ok(/"status":201/.test(r) && /BODY-OK GET/.test(r), `L7b: curl -i performs a normal GET and returns the body (${r.slice(0, 90)})`);
+        ok(/"ct":"text\/plain/.test(r) && /"probe":"yes"/.test(r), `L7c: with the response headers -i asks for already in the result — which is why it is a no-op, not a gap (${r.slice(0, 120)})`);
+      } finally { await new Promise((r) => srv.close(r)); }
+    }
+    {
+      // The insecure path returns a STRUCTURED result (not a throw) so the agent can branch on it.
+      const r = await runIn('const res = await curl(["-k", "https://127.0.0.1:1/nothing"]); return JSON.stringify({ ok: res.ok, status: res.status, insecure: res.insecure, hasErr: !!res.error });', ctx);
+      ok(/"ok":false/.test(r) && /"insecure":true/.test(r) && /"hasErr":true/.test(r),
+        `L8: the insecure path returns a structured result and marks itself insecure (${r.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    }
+    {
+      // -k is SCOPED: skipping certificate verification is a local-dev affordance, never a way to accept any
+      // certificate on a routable host (that would defeat the data boundary this product exists for).
+      const { isPrivateHost } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+      ok(['localhost', '127.0.0.1', '::1', '10.1.2.3', '192.168.1.5', '172.20.0.9', '169.254.1.1', 'box.local'].every(isPrivateHost), 'L9: loopback/private/link-local/.local count as LOCAL');
+      ok(!['example.com', '8.8.8.8', 'api.github.com', '172.32.0.1', '11.0.0.1'].some(isPrivateHost), 'L10: public hosts (incl. 172.32/11.x just outside the private ranges) do NOT');
+      // A HOSTNAME beginning "fc"/"fd" must NOT be mistaken for an IPv6 unique-local address (audit sev3).
+      ok(!['fcm.googleapis.com', 'fd00.evil.com', 'fc-cdn.example.net', 'feed.example.com'].some(isPrivateHost), 'L10b: hostnames starting fc/fd/feed are PUBLIC (only a real fcXX:/fdXX: hextet is ULA)');
+      ok(['fc00::1', 'fd12:3456::1', 'fe80::1'].every(isPrivateHost), 'L10c: real IPv6 ULA/link-local literals ARE local');
+      const r = await runIn('try { await curl(["-k", "https://example.com/"]); return "ALLOWED"; } catch (e) { return e.message; }', ctx);
+      ok(/only allowed for a LOCAL host/.test(r) && /would accept ANY certificate/.test(r), `L11: -k against a PUBLIC host is refused with the reason (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+      const r2 = await runIn('const res = await curl("https://127.0.0.1:1/x", { insecure: true }); return String(res.insecure);', ctx);
+      ok(r2.trim() === 'true', 'L12: the option form { insecure: true } is scoped the same way and still works locally');
+    }
+
+    // ── M. PRE-FLIGHT: a Node reach is refused before ANY of the snippet runs ──────────────────────────────────
+    {
+      await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'preflight.txt'), 'ORIGINAL');
+      const r = await runIn('await write("preflight.txt", "MUTATED"); const fs = require("fs"); return fs.readdirSync(".");', ctx);
+      ok(/refused BEFORE running/.test(r), `M1: require() later in the snippet is caught up front (${r.slice(0, 70)})`);
+      const after = await readFile(path.join(proj, 'preflight.txt'), 'utf8');
+      ok(after === 'ORIGINAL', 'M2: NOTHING executed — the earlier write did not happen (no half-done work)');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const os = require("os"); return os.tmpdir();', ctx);
+      ok(/use tmp\(\)/.test(r) && /os\.tmpdir\(\) replacement/.test(r),
+        'M3: require("os") points at the tmp() scratch function — a real replacement, not "you do not need one"');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = await runIn('const m = await import("node:fs"); return "x";', ctx);

+    }
+    {

+    }

+      // (allowFail: a killed command is a non-zero exit, which THROWS by default — see J7b — so the fields are read this way)

+      const thrown = await runIn('await node(["-e", "setTimeout(() => {}, 20000)"], { timeoutMs: 700 }); return "carried on";', ctx);

+      const r7b = await runIn('const x = await node(["-e", "setTimeout(() => {}, 20000)"], { timeoutMs: 700, allowFail: true }); return JSON.stringify({ code: x.code, timedOut: x.timedOut });', ctx);

+    }
+
+    // [W] A SNIPPET WRITTEN AS A MODULE. The skills folder made this common and gave it an obvious cause: a skill
+    // FILE is `export default async function (args, api)`, so the shape is right and only its destination is
+    // wrong. Before this the agent got a bare "Unexpected token 'export'" — a parser burp that says nothing about
+    // what to do, on a snippet it had every reason to think was correct.
+    console.log('\n[W] a snippet written as if it were a module');
+    {

+      ok(/refused BEFORE running/.test(r) && /NOTHING was executed/.test(r), 'W1: an export in the snippet is refused before anything runs');
+      ok(/INSIDE of an async function, not a module/.test(r), 'W2: and says what the body actually is');
+      ok(/IF YOU MEANT TO SAVE A SKILL/.test(r) && /<<<SKILL/.test(r), 'W3: and names the reason it is usually written — a skill file — and where those exports belong');

+      const r3 = await runIn('exports.foo = 1; return 1;', ctx);
+      ok(/refused BEFORE running/.test(r3), 'W5: and so does exports.foo');
+    }

+      // still work — the heredoc body is text, not code, and nothing in it is a statement.
+      const body = 'const body = <<<SKILL' + String.fromCharCode(10)
+        + "export const purpose = 'counts things';" + String.fromCharCode(10)
+        + 'export async function pre(args, api) {}' + String.fromCharCode(10)
+        + 'export default async function (args, api) { return 1; }' + String.fromCharCode(10)

+      const inString = 'const b = "export default async function () {}"; return String(b.length > 0);';
+      ok((await runIn(inString, ctx)).trim() === 'true', 'W7: and so is one in an ordinary string');
+      const named = 'const exported = 1; const o = { exports: {} }; o.exports.x = 2; return String(exported + o.exports.x);';
+      ok((await runIn(named, ctx)).trim() === '3', 'W8: an identifier merely CONTAINING export, and a property called exports, are untouched');
+    }
+    // [V] NARRATION. console.log inside a snippet streams to the developer LIVE through ctx.onProgress — the same
+    // channel a build's stdout uses — so the prompt can tell the model to log milestones. Each call arrives as its
+    // own chunk, in order, BEFORE the snippet returns; the final return is separate.
+    console.log('\n[W] found by fuzzing (test/_fuzz-snippets.mjs): thrown objects, snippets that can never go on, missing paths, heavy logging');
+    {
+      const run = (code, extra = {}) => execute('js_eval', { code }, {}, { cwd: proj, projectDir: proj, onLog: () => {}, ...extra }).then(extractContent);
+      // a variable that hides a function says so (live, 2026-10-24: `const tmp = await tmp("x.js")`)
+      const hid = await run('const tmp = await tmp("x.js"); return tmp;');
+      ok(/declares a variable named tmp, which hides the function tmp\(\)/.test(hid), `W0: a variable named like a function says why it failed (${hid.slice(0, 160)})`);
+      const thrown = await run('throw { code: 42, why: "object thrown" };');
+      ok(/"code":42/.test(thrown) && /object thrown/.test(thrown), `W1: a thrown object says what it was, not [object Object] (${thrown.slice(0, 120)})`);
+      let t0 = Date.now();
+      const stuck = await run('await new Promise(() => {}); return "never";');
+      ok(Date.now() - t0 < 15000 && /stopped making progress/.test(stuck), `W2: a snippet awaiting a promise nothing settles ends in seconds, saying why (${((Date.now() - t0) / 1000).toFixed(1)} s)`);
+      t0 = Date.now();
+      const waited = await run('await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 4500)); return "waited";');
+      ok(/waited/.test(waited) && Date.now() - t0 >= 4400, `W3: one waiting on a timer is left to wait (${waited.slice(0, 60)})`);
+      t0 = Date.now();
+      const spin = await run('await list("."); while (true) {}');
+      ok(Date.now() - t0 < 40000 && /20 seconds without once pausing/.test(spin), `W3b: a loop that never yields AFTER an await is stopped too (${((Date.now() - t0) / 1000).toFixed(1)} s)`);
+      // W3c (the developer, 2026-10-11: "the js ran 20 sec w/o responding" and was skipped): the SERVER's thread held for
+      // 22 s by one synchronous call - the worker beat all along, its beats just waited unread - is not the snippet
+      // spinning, and the snippet goes on to its result
+      t0 = Date.now();
+      const held = await runJsEval({ code: 'const v = await hold(); return "went on after " + v;', ctx: { cwd: proj, projectDir: proj }, hostFns: { hold: async () => { const end = Date.now() + 22000; while (Date.now() < end) { /* the server, busy */ } return 'the hold'; } } });
+      ok(/went on after the hold/.test(held) && !/without once pausing/.test(held), `W3c: 22 s of the SERVER being busy is not the snippet spinning: ${held.slice(0, 90)} (${((Date.now() - t0) / 1000).toFixed(1)} s)`);
+      const noPath = await run('return await read();');
+      ok(/read\(path\): which file\?/.test(noPath), `W4: read() with no path says it needs one (${noPath.slice(0, 80)})`);
+      const chunks = [];
+      t0 = Date.now();
+      const loud = await run('for (let i = 0; i < 6000; i++) console.log("line " + i + " " + "y".repeat(100)); return "end";', { onProgress: (s) => chunks.push(String(s)) });
+      ok(/end/.test(loud), `W5: 6000 logged lines, and the snippet's own result still comes back (${loud.slice(0, 40)}) - the record's cap is in test-js-run-record`);
+      ok(chunks.length < 600 && chunks.join('').split('\n').filter(Boolean).length === 6000, `W6: and they are handed on in batches, every line once (${chunks.length} batches)`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[L] console.log in a snippet streams live, in order, before the result');
+    {
+      const chunks = [];
+      let firstAt = 0;
+      const r = await execute('js_eval', { code: [
+        'console.log("step 1: reading");',
+        'await new Promise((res) => setTimeout(res, 250));',
+        'console.log("step 2: found", 3, "rows");',
+        'console.error("warn:", "slow query");',
+        'return { ok: true };',
+      ].join('\n') }, {}, { cwd: proj, projectDir: proj, onLog: () => {}, onProgress: (s) => { if (!firstAt) firstAt = Date.now(); chunks.push(String(s)); } }).then(extractContent).then(resultOnly);
+      const doneAt = Date.now();
+      // (lines written within 40 ms of each other arrive TOGETHER - console output is handed on in batches, so a snippet
+      // that logs 20000 lines does not hold the server for seconds; the sleep between step 1 and step 2 separates them)
+      ok(chunks.length >= 2 && /^step 1: reading\n$/.test(chunks[0]), `V1: the first log line arrived before the rest, on its own (${JSON.stringify(chunks[0])})`);
+      ok(chunks.join('').includes('step 2: found 3 rows\n'), 'V2: several arguments join with spaces, like console.log');
+      ok(chunks.join('') === 'step 1: reading\nstep 2: found 3 rows\nwarn: slow query\n', `V3: console.error streams too, in order (${JSON.stringify(chunks.join(''))})`);
+      // Measured from the END, not from the call. "Live" means the first line was already out while the snippet
+      // still had its 250ms sleep to do — so the gap between that line and the run finishing must span the sleep.
+      // Timing it from the call instead folded in sandbox STARTUP, which under a loaded full suite pushed a healthy
+      // run past the 200ms ceiling and failed a test of streaming for a reason that had nothing to do with streaming
+      // (seen in run-all 1925 at 213ms; it passed on the retry). Startup delays the first line and the finish
+      // equally and the sleep sits strictly between them, so this holds however slow the machine is — while a
+      // batched-at-the-end implementation still fails it, since there the gap collapses to ~0.
+      ok(firstAt && doneAt - firstAt >= 200, `V4: the first line was out a whole sleep before the run finished — live, not batched at the end (${doneAt - firstAt}ms before the end)`);
+      ok(/"ok": *true/.test(r) && !/step 1/.test(r), 'V5: the returned result is the summary alone — the narration is not repeated into it');
+    }
+  } finally {
+    for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) { try { await rm(root, { recursive: true, force: true }); break; } catch { await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 200)); } }
+  }
+
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e?.stack || e); process.exit(2); });

+/**
+ * parallel() IN A SNIPPET (src/agent/js-eval.mjs). Prod, 2026-10-28: the agent wrote
+ *   const [dsJs, serverJs] = await parallel({ ds: () => read("src/datasource.js"), srv: () => read("src/server.js") });
+ * and got "(intermediate value) is not iterable" - the results were an object, and it spent a step finding that out.
+ *   [1] the results by name, as before
+ *   [2] and by position, in the order the branches were given - array destructuring works
+ *   [3] an array of functions is taken too, and gives an array
+ *   [4] the results still print as the plain object they are (nothing extra in them)
+ *   [5] a failed branch still names itself, the others still finish
+ * Run: node test/test-js-parallel.mjs
+ */
+const { runJsEval } = await import('../src/agent/js-eval.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const hostFns = { read: async ([p]) => `contents of ${p}` };
+const run = (code) => runJsEval({ code, ctx: {}, hostFns });
+
+console.log('\n[1] by name');
+let out = await run('const { ds, srv } = await parallel({ ds: () => read("a.js"), srv: () => read("b.js") }); return ds + " | " + srv;');
+ok(/contents of a\.js \| contents of b\.js/.test(out), `const { ds, srv } = await parallel({...}) (${out.split('\n')[0]})`);
+
+console.log('\n[2] by position (the prod snippet)');
+out = await run('const [dsJs, serverJs] = await parallel({ ds: () => read("src/datasource.js"), srv: () => read("src/server.js") }); return dsJs + " | " + serverJs;');
+ok(/contents of src\/datasource\.js \| contents of src\/server\.js/.test(out) && !/not iterable/.test(out), `const [dsJs, serverJs] = await parallel({...}) works, in the order given (${out.split('\n')[0]})`);
+
+console.log('\n[3] an array of functions');
+out = await run('const r = await parallel([() => read("x"), () => read("y")]); return Array.isArray(r) + " " + r.join(" + ");');
+ok(/^true contents of x \+ contents of y/.test(out), `parallel([f, g]) gives an array (${out.split('\n')[0]})`);
+
+console.log('\n[4] the results print as they are');
+out = await run('const r = await parallel({ a: async () => 1, b: async () => 2 }); return JSON.stringify(r) + " " + Object.keys(r).join(",");');
+ok(/^\{"a":1,"b":2\} a,b/.test(out), `JSON and keys unchanged (${out.split('\n')[0]})`);
+out = await run('return await parallel({ a: async () => 1, b: async () => 2 });');
+ok(/^\{"a":1,"b":2\}/.test(out) && !/Symbol/.test(out), `returned whole: the plain object, nothing extra (${out.split('\n')[0]})`);
+
+console.log('\n[5] a failure');
+out = await run('return await parallel({ good: async () => 1, bad: async () => { throw new Error("nope"); } });');
+ok(/parallel branch failed \(the others finished\) - "bad": nope/.test(out), `the failed branch is named (${out.split('\n')[0].slice(0, 100)})`);
+out = await run('return await parallel([async () => 1, async () => { throw new Error("nope"); }]);');
+ok(/"1": nope/.test(out), 'in an array, by its position');
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
