=== add test/test-js-plugin-bridge.mjs
new a7e55e155a77ad6a4e7e46fe
+/**
+ * PLUGIN TOOLS ARE <js> FUNCTIONS TOO. A plugin's tool is callable from a <js> snippet by its tool name with one
+ * args object, through the same execute() path a tag call takes:
+ *   [1] a plugin tool called from <js> runs and its result content comes back to the snippet
+ *   [2] the plugin's schema still gates it — a missing required arg THROWS; [2b] a tool that throws stops the
+ *       snippet at that line; [2c] a tool that RETURNS "ERROR: …" still returns it (its choice of shape)
+ *   [3] a host function wins a name clash (a plugin tool named `list` never shadows the host `list`)
+ *   [4] in Discuss (read-only) mode only tools the plugin marked readOnly are exposed (pluginJsFns directly)
+ * Run: node test/test-js-plugin-bridge.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { runMinimalWorker } from '../src/agent/minimal-worker.mjs';
+import { pluginJsFns } from '../src/agent/tools.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+function mockAdapter(script) {
+  let i = 0;
+  return { async complete() { const c = script[Math.min(i++, script.length - 1)]; return { choices: [{ message: { content: c } }], usage: { total_tokens: 5 } }; } };
+}
+const calls = [];
+const extraTools = {
+  echo_tool: {
+    schema: { description: 'echo the args', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: { x: { type: 'number' }, label: { type: 'string' } }, required: ['x'] } },
+    impl: async (args) => { calls.push(args); return { content: 'echo:' + JSON.stringify(args) }; },
+  },
+  // the browser-plugin case, 2026-09-23: a step that fails THROWS, and nothing after it may run
+  fail_tool: {
+    schema: { description: 'a step that fails', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } },
+    impl: async () => { calls.push('fail_tool'); throw new Error('the page never loaded'); },
+  },
+  // a failure REPORTED as the result (a test report, say) — the plugin's choice of shape, still data
+  soft_tool: {
+    schema: { description: 'reports a failure as its result', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } },
+    impl: async () => ({ content: 'ERROR: 3 tests failed' }),
+  },
+  list: {   // deliberately shadows the host function name
+    schema: { description: 'clash', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } },
+    impl: async () => ({ content: 'PLUGIN-LIST-SHOULD-NOT-WIN' }),
+  },
+};
+async function run(dir, script, extra = {}) {
+  const events = [];
+  const result = await runMinimalWorker({
+    adapter: mockAdapter(script), model: 'test', signal: { aborted: false },
+    messages: [{ content: 'do the thing' }], projectDir: dir,
+    requireBranch: () => 'ok', requireApproval: () => true,
+    onChatEvent: (e) => events.push(e), onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {},
+    extraTools, extraVerbs: { echo_tool: 'echo_tool', list_plugin: 'list' },
+    ...extra,
+  });
+  return { result, events };
+}
+// the snippet's own value: since build 1963 its result is followed by the record of what it did (run-record.mjs)
+const jsResult = (events) => events.filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.name === 'js_eval').map((e) => String(e.content || '').replace(/\n\nWhat the snippet did, in order:\n[\s\S]*$/, ''));
+const withDir = async (fn) => { const d = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-jsbridge-')); try { await fn(d); } finally { await rm(d, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); } };
+
+console.log('\n[1] a plugin tool called from <js> runs and hands its result content back to the snippet');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  calls.length = 0;
+  const { events } = await run(dir, [
+    'Calling it. <remote-workspace><js>const r = await echo_tool({ x: 41, label: "hi" }); return "GOT " + r + " / " + typeof r;</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out = jsResult(events)[0] || '';
+  ok(calls.length === 1 && calls[0].x === 41 && calls[0].label === 'hi', `the plugin impl ran with the snippet's args (${JSON.stringify(calls[0])})`);
+  ok(/GOT echo:\{"x":41,"label":"hi"\} \/ string/.test(out), `the result content came back to the snippet as a string (${out.slice(0, 80)})`);
+});
+
+console.log('\n[2] the plugin schema still gates the call — a missing required arg THROWS, and the snippet stops there');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  calls.length = 0;
+  const { events } = await run(dir, [
+    'Calling it wrong. <remote-workspace><js>const r = await echo_tool({ label: "no x" }); return "R=" + r;</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out = jsResult(events)[0] || '';
+  ok(calls.length === 0, 'the impl never ran');
+  ok(/^ERROR: .*echo_tool\(\) failed: .*required parameter "x"/.test(out) && !/R=/.test(out), `the refusal throws, named, and the line after it never ran (${out.slice(0, 110)})`);
+  ok(/ONE args object/.test(out) && !/tag body|attribute/.test(out), 'and it says how to fix it in a function call, not in tag syntax that no longer exists');
+});
+
+console.log('\n[2b] a plugin tool that THROWS stops the snippet at that line (the browser-plugin case)');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  calls.length = 0;
+  const { events } = await run(dir, [
+    'Testing the page. <remote-workspace><js>await fail_tool({}); await echo_tool({ x: 1 }); return "AFTER";</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out = jsResult(events)[0] || '';
+  ok(/^ERROR: .*fail_tool\(\) failed: the page never loaded/.test(out), `the model sees which call failed, and why (${out.slice(0, 90)})`);
+  ok(!/AFTER/.test(out) && calls.length === 1 && calls[0] === 'fail_tool', 'and NOTHING after it ran — the whole point');
+  const { events: ev2 } = await run(dir, [
+    'With a fallback. <remote-workspace><js>try { await fail_tool({}); } catch (e) { return "CAUGHT " + e.message; }</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out2 = jsResult(ev2)[0] || '';
+  ok(out2 === 'CAUGHT fail_tool() failed: the page never loaded', `a snippet that HAS a fallback can still catch it (${out2})`);
+});
+
+console.log('\n[2c] a plugin tool that RETURNS "ERROR: …" still returns it — the plugin chose that shape');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  const { events } = await run(dir, [
+    'Reading the report. <remote-workspace><js>const r = await soft_tool({}); return "R=" + r;</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out = jsResult(events)[0] || '';
+  ok(out === 'R=ERROR: 3 tests failed', `returned as data, as before (${out})`);
+});
+
+console.log('\n[3] a host function wins a name clash');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'a.txt'), 'a');
+  const { events } = await run(dir, [
+    'Listing. <remote-workspace><js>const r = await list("."); return Array.isArray(r) ? "HOST " + r.map((f) => f.name).join(",") : "OTHER " + r;</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out = jsResult(events)[0] || '';
+  ok(/^HOST /.test(out.trim()) && /a\.txt/.test(out) && !/PLUGIN-LIST/.test(out), `list(".") is the host's readdir, not the plugin tool (${out.slice(0, 60)})`);
+});
+
+console.log('\n[4] Discuss (read-only) mode exposes only the tools the plugin marked readOnly');
+{
+  const fns = pluginJsFns({ readOnly: true, extraTools: { peek: { impl: async () => ({ content: 'p' }), readOnly: true }, poke: { impl: async () => ({ content: 'q' }) } } });
+  ok(Object.keys(fns).sort().join(',') === 'peek', `only the readOnly tool is exposed (${Object.keys(fns).join(',') || 'none'})`);
+  const all = pluginJsFns({ readOnly: false, extraTools: { peek: { impl: async () => ({ content: 'p' }), readOnly: true }, poke: { impl: async () => ({ content: 'q' }) } } });
+  ok(Object.keys(all).sort().join(',') === 'peek,poke', 'outside Discuss mode both are');
+  ok(Object.keys(pluginJsFns({ extraTools: { 'bad-name': { impl: async () => 'x' }, fine_name: { impl: async () => 'y' } } })).join(',') === 'fine_name', 'a tool name that is not a JS identifier is left out (it could not be called anyway)');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[6] host.callTool reached for from a <js> snippet is explained, naming the plugin functions to use instead');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  // Seen repeatedly (2026-09-18): an agent that has just read the plugin brief writes the PLUGIN API inside a
+  // snippet. The sandbox has no `host`; the error must say so and name the tool functions that ARE there.
+  calls.length = 0;
+  const { events } = await run(dir, [
+    'Testing my plugin. <remote-workspace><js>const r = await host.callTool("echo", "echo_tool", { x: 1 }); return r;</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out = jsResult(events)[0] || '';
+  ok(/host is not defined/.test(out), `the ReferenceError is reported (${out.slice(0, 60)})`);
+  ok(/PLUGIN API/.test(out) && /inside a plugin FILE/.test(out), 'and explained: host is the plugin-file API, a snippet has none');
+  ok(/await echo_tool\(\{ … \}\)/.test(out) && /Plugin tools available in this snippet: echo_tool/.test(out), `and it names the plugin functions that ARE in scope (${out.slice(-160)})`);
+  ok(calls.length === 0, 'nothing ran');
+  const { events: ev2 } = await run(dir, [
+    'Again. <remote-workspace><js>return await ctx.host.use("pii-testdata").listFamilies();</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done.',
+  ]);
+  const out2 = jsResult(ev2)[0] || '';
+  ok(/ctx is not defined/.test(out2) && /PLUGIN API/.test(out2), `ctx.host in a snippet gets the same explanation (${out2.slice(0, 60)})`);
+});
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

=== add test/test-js-run-record.mjs
new 27dc9a2e7262c79d91dc745d
+/**
+ * THE RUN RECORD + THROWING EXITS — what a <js> snippet did comes back with its result.
+ *
+ * A snippet is the agent continuing its turn on the client, and it used to get back ONLY the value its own code
+ * chose to return: a 30-step script that went wrong at step 2 came back as a guess. These checks pin the two
+ * halves of the fix:
+ *   A. run-record.mjs renders calls, values and console lines; caps are honoured and announced.
+ *   B. every call a snippet makes is in the result, in CALL order, with what it returned or why it failed —
+ *      including a snippet that threw, one aborted mid-flight (open calls say so), and console.log lines where
+ *      they happened.
+ *   C. a non-zero exit THROWS (so the snippet stops there), {allowFail: true} gives back {code,stdout,stderr},
+ *      and either way the not-finished trailer (bookkeeper off) is told a process RAN (ctx.jsInfo.execRan) so a failing test run still counts.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-js-run-record.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, mkdir, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+const { renderRunRecord, formatStepValue } = await import('../src/agent/run-record.mjs');
+const { runJsEval } = await import('../src/agent/js-eval.mjs');
+const { execute, extractContent } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+async function main() {
+  const root = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-runrec-'));
+  const proj = path.join(root, 'proj');
+  await mkdir(proj, { recursive: true });
+  await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'a.txt'), 'hello\n');
+  try {
+    // ── A. the renderer ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    console.log('A. run-record.mjs');
+    {
+      const r = renderRunRecord([
+        { kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'read', label: 'a.txt', status: 'ok', value: 'hello', ms: 3 },
+        { kind: 'log', text: 'reading done\n' },
+        { kind: 'call', step: 2, fn: 'mvn', label: '-q install', status: 'ok', value: { code: 1, stdout: 'x', stderr: 'BUILD FAILURE' }, ms: 2000 },
+      ]);
+      ok(/\[1\] read\(a\.txt\)/.test(r.text), 'a call reads as [n] fn(label)');
+      ok(r.text.includes('console: reading done'), 'a console line is marked and kept in place');
+      ok(r.text.indexOf('[1]') < r.text.indexOf('console:') && r.text.indexOf('console:') < r.text.indexOf('[2]'), 'entries stay in the order they happened');
+      ok(r.text.includes('exit 1') && r.text.includes('BUILD FAILURE'), 'a process shows its exit code and stderr');
+      ok(/· 2\.0s/.test(r.text), 'a slow call shows how long it took');
+      ok(r.cut === false, 'nothing short was cut');
+    }
+    {
+      const big = 'x'.repeat(50 * 1024);
+      const v = formatStepValue({ code: 0, stdout: big, stderr: '' });
+      ok(v.cut && v.text.length < 9 * 1024, 'a long stdout is capped');
+      ok(v.text.includes('last ') && v.text.includes('of 50.0 KB'), 'the cap says how much of what it kept');
+      // (the developer, 2026-10-28: "we shouldn't send along the content of the tool results ... Maybe show the result if
+      // it's under a certain budget, a small one. Also, if a tool errors, give the full output back.")
+      const r = renderRunRecord([{ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'read', label: 'big', status: 'ok', value: big }]);
+      ok(r.withheld === 1 && /\[1\] read\(big\) → ok · 1 line$/.test(r.text) && !/xxxx/.test(r.text), `a value past the small budget is left out: its size instead (${r.text})`);
+      const small = renderRunRecord([{ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'info', label: 'a.js', status: 'ok', value: { size: 12, isFile: true } }]);
+      ok(small.withheld === 0 && /→ \{"size":12,"isFile":true\}/.test(small.text), 'a small one is shown');
+      const longErr = 'npm ERR! ' + 'line of the failure\n'.repeat(1500) + 'THE LAST LINE';
+      const er = renderRunRecord([{ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'npm', label: 'test', status: 'error', error: longErr }, { kind: 'call', step: 2, fn: 'read', label: 'big', status: 'ok', value: big }]);
+      ok(er.text.includes(longErr) && /THE LAST LINE/.test(er.text), `a failure is shown in full (${longErr.length} characters of it)`);
+      const huge = 'start\n' + 'y'.repeat(200 * 1024) + '\nTHE END';
+      const hr = renderRunRecord([{ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'node', label: 'x', status: 'error', error: huge }]);
+      ok(/^\[1\] node\(x\) → FAILED:\nstart/.test(hr.text) && /THE END$/.test(hr.text) && /left out here - all of it is in the log/.test(hr.text) && hr.cut, 'past 64 KB: its start and its end, and the log has the rest');
+    }
+    {
+      // found by fuzzing: 20000 logged lines sent 2.4 MB to the model - each was shortened past the budget, but still
+      // written, one line each. Console output now arrives in batches (js-eval.mjs); past twice the budget nothing
+      // more is written, only counted - and a failed call is never left out.
+      const batch = (from, n) => ({ kind: 'log', text: Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => 'line ' + (from + i) + ' ' + 'y'.repeat(100)).join('\n') });
+      const entries = [];
+      for (let b = 0; b < 200; b++) entries.push(batch(b * 100, 100));
+      entries.push({ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'read', label: 'x', status: 'ok', value: 'fine' });
+      entries.push({ kind: 'call', step: 2, fn: 'mvn', label: 'install', status: 'error', error: 'BUILD FAILURE here' });
+      const r = renderRunRecord(entries);
+      ok(r.text.length < 200 * 1024 && r.cut, `20000 logged lines: the record stays small (${(r.text.length / 1024).toFixed(0)} KB)`);
+      ok(/more lines\)/.test(r.text), 'and says how many lines each shortened batch held');
+      // one line per entry, as before batching: past twice the budget nothing more is written - only counted
+      const single = [];
+      for (let i = 0; i < 20000; i++) single.push({ kind: 'log', text: 'line ' + i + ' ' + 'y'.repeat(100) });
+      const r1 = renderRunRecord(single);
+      ok(r1.text.length < 200 * 1024 && /\d+ console lines not shown/.test(r1.text), `20000 single-line entries: ${(r1.text.length / 1024).toFixed(0)} KB, and it says how many lines it left out`);
+      ok(/BUILD FAILURE here/.test(r.text), 'a failed call past the budget is still shown');
+    }
+    {
+      const err = 'mvn install exited with code 1\nstderr:\nboom';
+      const r = renderRunRecord([{ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'mvn', label: 'install', status: 'error', error: err }]);
+      ok(r.text.includes('FAILED') && r.text.includes('boom'), 'a failed call shows why');
+      const shown = renderRunRecord([{ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'mvn', label: 'install', status: 'error', error: err }], { errorShown: 'ERROR: ' + err });
+      ok(shown.text.includes('the error above') && !shown.text.includes('boom'), 'the failure that stopped the snippet is not printed twice');
+    }
+    {
+      const r = renderRunRecord([{ kind: 'call', step: 1, fn: 'mvn', label: 'install' }]);
+      ok(r.text.includes('did not finish'), 'a call still open when the snippet stopped says so');
+    }
+
+    // ── B. every call comes back with the result ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    console.log('B. the record rides out with the result');
+    const jsResult = (code, extra = {}) => execute('js_eval', { code }, {}, { cwd: proj, keepRecord: true, ...extra }).then(extractContent);
+    {
+      const out = await jsResult('const t = await read("a.txt"); console.log("read " + t.length + " chars"); return { len: t.length };');
+      ok(out.includes('"len"'), 'the snippet result is still first');
+      ok(out.includes('What the snippet did, in order:'), 'the record follows it');
+      ok(/\[1\] read\(a\.txt\)/.test(out), 'the call is named with its argument');
+      ok(out.includes('console: read 6 chars'), 'the console line is in the record');
+      ok(out.indexOf('"len"') < out.indexOf('What the snippet did'), 'the result comes before the record');
+    }
+    {
+      const out = await jsResult('const a = await read("a.txt"); await read("does-not-exist.txt"); return a;');
+      ok(out.startsWith('ERROR:'), 'a snippet that throws still reports the error first');
+      ok(/\[1\] read\(a\.txt\)/.test(out) && /\[2\] read\(does-not-exist\.txt\)/.test(out), 'the calls BEFORE the failure are in the record');
+      ok(/\[2\][^\n]*(FAILED|the error above)/.test(out), 'the call that failed is marked');
+    }
+    {
+      // aborted mid-flight: the calls that got through are exactly what the model needs to see
+      const ac = new AbortController();
+      const p = jsResult('await read("a.txt"); console.log("step one done"); await new Promise(() => {}); return 1;', { signal: ac.signal });
+      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 500));
+      ac.abort();
+      const out = await p;
+      ok(out.startsWith('ERROR: JS eval aborted'), 'the abort is reported');
+      ok(/\[1\] read\(a\.txt\)/.test(out) && out.includes('console: step one done'), 'an aborted snippet still shows how far it got');
+    }
+    {
+      const out = await jsResult('const r = await Promise.all([read("a.txt"), read("a.txt"), read("a.txt")]); return r.length;');
+      ok(/\[1\] read/.test(out) && /\[2\] read/.test(out) && /\[3\] read/.test(out), 'parallel calls are all recorded');
+    }
+    {
+      const out = await jsResult('return 1;');
+      ok(!out.includes('What the snippet did'), 'a snippet that called nothing gets no record');
+    }
+
+    // ── C. a non-zero exit throws ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    console.log('C. non-zero exits throw');
+    const nodeArgs = (js) => JSON.stringify(['-e', js]);
+    {
+      const out = await jsResult('await node(' + nodeArgs('console.error("nope"); process.exit(3)') + '); return "reached";');
+      ok(!out.includes('reached'), 'the snippet stopped at the failing call');
+      ok(/exited with code 3/.test(out), 'the error names the exit code');
+      ok(out.includes('nope'), 'the error carries the end of stderr');
+      ok(out.includes('{allowFail: true}'), 'the error says how to keep a non-zero exit as data');
+    }
+    {
+      const out = await jsResult('const r = await node(' + nodeArgs('process.exit(3)') + ', {allowFail: true}); return { code: r.code };');
+      ok(/"code":\s*3/.test(out), 'allowFail returns {code} instead of throwing');
+    }
+    {
+      const out = await jsResult('const r = await node(' + nodeArgs('console.log("fine")') + '); return r.code;');
+      ok(!out.startsWith('ERROR:'), 'a zero exit is unchanged');
+      ok(out.includes('exit 0') && out.includes('fine'), 'the record shows the exit code and output of a call that worked');
+    }
+    {
+      // the not-finished trailer (bookkeeper off): a test run that RAN and failed still counts as a verify
+      const info = {};
+      await jsResult('await node(' + nodeArgs('process.exit(1)') + ');', { jsInfo: info });
+      // (on this line the WORKER notes that a process ran, from the step events)
+      const info2 = {};
+      await jsResult('await read("a.txt");', { jsInfo: info2 });
+      ok(!info2.execRan, 'a snippet that ran no process does not claim one');
+    }
+
+    // ── D. the record cannot grow without bound ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    console.log('D. bounds');
+    {
+      const out = await runJsEval({ code: 'for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) await big(); return "done";', ctx: {}, hostFns: { big: async () => 'y'.repeat(600 * 1024) } });
+      ok(out.includes('done'), 'the snippet ran');
+      // (the value budget belonged to production's record inside the snippet runner; here the worker keeps the record)
+      ok(out.length < 200 * 1024, 'the record is bounded');
+    }
+
+    // ── E. a pause (the developer, 2026-11-02: "the agent is trying to call await sleep(millis) and it doesn't exist") ──
+    console.log('E. sleep()');
+    {
+      const t0 = Date.now();
+      const out = await runJsEval({ code: 'const a = Date.now(); await sleep(250); return "slept " + (Date.now() - a >= 240);', ctx: {}, hostFns: {} });
+      ok(/slept true/.test(out) && Date.now() - t0 >= 240, `await sleep(ms) pauses the snippet (${out.slice(0, 40)})`);
+      const neg = await runJsEval({ code: 'await sleep(-5); await sleep("x"); return "fine";', ctx: {}, hostFns: {} });
+      ok(/fine/.test(neg), 'a negative or non-number wait is no wait, not an error');
+    }
+  } finally {
+    await rm(root, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });

=== add test/test-js-scan.mjs
new 6947fe5dee5b32ec876511c0
+/**
+ * js-scan.mjs — the stateful CODE-vs-NOT-CODE scanner behind the <js> pre-flight guard.
+ *
+ * The property under test: a construct that appears inside a STRING, TEMPLATE, COMMENT or REGEX must be
+ * invisible, while the same construct in real code must be visible. The agent legitimately authors source
+ * containing require() — `await write("app.js", "const fs = require('fs')")` must never be refused — and the
+ * naive blanker this replaced DESYNCED on a regex literal containing a quote (/['"]/), reading the rest of the
+ * snippet as a string and going blind.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-js-scan.mjs
+ */
+import { blankNonCode } from '../src/agent/js-scan.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+// Does a BARE require(/import( survive the blanking? (the exact test the pre-flight makes)
+const RE = /(?<![\w$.])require\s*\(/;
+const IRE = /(?<![\w$.])import\s*\(/;
+const sees = (src) => { const b = blankNonCode(src); return b == null ? null : RE.test(b) || IRE.test(b); };
+
+console.log('\n[A] real code is SEEN');
+ok(sees('const fs = require("fs");') === true, 'A1: a bare require() is seen');
+ok(sees('const m = await import("node:fs");') === true, 'A2: a dynamic import() is seen');
+ok(sees('await npm(["i"]);\nconst fs = require("fs");') === true, 'A3: seen when it comes after other statements');
+ok(sees('if (x) { const p = require("path"); }') === true, 'A4: seen inside a block');
+
+console.log('\n[B] the same text inside NOT-code is INVISIBLE');
+ok(sees('await write("app.js", "const fs = require(\'fs\');");') === false, 'B1: inside a double-quoted string (the authoring case)');
+ok(sees("await write('a.js', 'require(\"fs\")');") === false, 'B2: inside a single-quoted string');
+ok(sees('await write("a.js", `const fs = require("fs");`);') === false, 'B3: inside a template literal');
+ok(sees('// const fs = require("fs");\nreturn 1;') === false, 'B4: inside a line comment');
+ok(sees('/* require("fs")\n   import("x") */\nreturn 1;') === false, 'B5: inside a block comment');
+ok(sees('const s = "he said \\"require(\'fs\')\\" loudly";') === false, 'B6: inside a string containing ESCAPED quotes');
+
+console.log('\n[C] REGEX literals — the case that broke the naive blanker');
+ok(sees('const re = /[\'"]/; return re.test("x");') === false, 'C1: a regex containing quotes does not desync the scan');
+ok(sees('const re = /[\'"]/; const fs = require("fs");') === true, 'C2: …and a REAL require after such a regex is still seen');
+ok(sees('const re = /a\\/b/; const fs = require("fs");') === true, 'C3: an escaped slash inside a regex does not end it early');
+ok(sees('const re = /[/]/; const fs = require("fs");') === true, 'C4: a slash inside a CHARACTER CLASS does not end the regex');
+ok(sees('const x = 10 / 2 / 1; const fs = require("fs");') === true, 'C5: division is not mistaken for a regex');
+ok(sees('const y = (a + b) / 2; return y;') === false, 'C6: division after ")" stays code (no false require)');
+
+console.log('\n[D] TEMPLATE nesting — ${ … } is CODE again');
+ok(sees('const s = `a ${ require("fs") } b`;') === true, 'D1: a require inside ${ … } IS code and is seen');
+ok(sees('const s = `a ${ `b ${ require("fs") }` } c`;') === true, 'D2: seen through NESTED template interpolation');
+ok(sees('const s = `outer ${ x } require("fs") tail`;') === false, 'D3: text after a ${…} is template again, not code');
+ok(sees('const s = `${ {a:1}.a } require("fs")`;') === false, 'D4: braces INSIDE ${ … } do not end the interpolation early');
+
+console.log('\n[E] the negative-lookbehind cases (a call merely ENDING in require)');
+ok(sees('const o = { require: (x) => x }; return o.require("hi");') === false, 'E1: o.require(...) is not a Node reach');
+ok(sees('function myRequire(x){return x} return myRequire("a");') === false, 'E2: myRequire(...) is not a Node reach');
+
+console.log('\n[F] structure is preserved (offsets + lines still line up)');
+{
+  const src = 'const a = "xx";\nconst b = 1;\n// c\nreturn a;';
+  const b = blankNonCode(src);
+  ok(b.length === src.length, 'F1: output length matches the input exactly');
+  ok(b.split('\n').length === src.split('\n').length, 'F2: line count is preserved');
+  ok(b.includes('const b = 1;'), 'F3: real code is left verbatim');
+  ok(!b.includes('xx'), 'F4: string CONTENT is blanked');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[H] division-vs-regex ambiguity after ")" — never FALSE-flag a regex-expression-statement');
+// `for (…) /re/.test(x)` is a regex used as an expression statement; regexAllowed() wrongly says "division" after
+// ")", so the regex body would be read as code. If it contains require(/import( that would false-flag legitimate
+// authoring work — the one thing the scanner must never do. The guard bails to null (caller uses the runtime guard).
+ok(blankNonCode('for (const l of lines) /require(\\w+)/.test(l);') === null, 'H1: an ambiguous regex-stmt containing require() bails to null (no false flag)');
+ok(sees('for (const l of lines) /require(\\w+)/.test(l);') === null, 'H1b: …so the pre-flight sees null and defers, never a false require');
+// Pure division is unaffected: no second "/" on the line, so no bail — and a REAL require after it is still seen.
+ok(sees('const y = (a + b) / 2; const fs = require("fs");') === true, 'H2: ordinary division does not trigger the bail (real require still caught)');
+ok(sees('const y = (a + b) / 2; return y;') === false, 'H3: division with no require nearby stays clean (no false positive)');
+
+console.log('\n[G] unscannable input returns null (never a guess)');
+ok(blankNonCode('const s = "unterminated') === null, 'G1: an unterminated string → null');
+ok(blankNonCode('/* unterminated') === null, 'G2: an unterminated block comment → null');
+ok(blankNonCode('const s = `unterminated') === null, 'G3: an unterminated template → null');
+ok(sees('const s = "unterminated') === null, 'G4: the caller sees null and can fall back to the runtime guard');
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+

+/**
+ * INSIDE AN OPENED SNIPPET, EACH CALL IS A TOOL ROW YOU CAN OPEN (build 2153).
+ *
+ * The developer: "When you expand a collapsed set of tool uses it shows the js snippet and then I was expecting for

+ * handed it), and a process that is still running can be opened to follow its output live.
+ *

=== add test/test-js-timeout-and-bg-handoff.mjs
new a39d52b86881d0f9d992b637
+/**
+ * Two developer asks (2026-09-18):
+ *   [A] <js timeout_ms="…">: every process call inside a snippet waits up to 5 min by default (git/node/java used to
+ *       default to 2), and the attribute sets the default for every call in that snippet — a long build gets its time
+ *       from ONE number. Proven with a node process that sleeps: 1.5s under a 300ms per-call timeout is killed;
+ *       under <js timeout_ms="4000"> it completes; a per-call {timeoutMs} still wins over the attribute.
+ *   [B] pressing ⇱ (background this step) hands the TURN to the developer: the run ends after that tool batch with a
+ *       one-line closer instead of carrying on to another model call. Driven through the real worker with a scripted
+ *       model: turn 1 runs a slow <js>; the test presses ⇱ via backgroundToolCall; the run must END (no turn 2), the
+ *       closer names the bg id, and the model's next call never happened.
+ * Run: node test/test-js-timeout-and-bg-handoff.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-jsto-home-'));
+const { execute, backgroundToolCall } = await import('../src/agent/tools.mjs');
+const { runMinimalWorker } = await import('../src/agent/minimal-worker.mjs');
+const { bgStatus, killBackground } = await import('../src/agent/bg-procs.mjs');
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+async function runJs(dir, code, extraArgs = {}) {
+  const r = await execute('js_eval', { code, ...extraArgs }, {}, { projectDir: dir, cwd: dir, onLog: () => {}, onProgress: () => {} });
+  return typeof r === 'string' ? r : JSON.stringify(r);
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-jsto-'));
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[A] <js timeout_ms> sets the default timeout for every process call in the snippet');
+    const SLEEP = 'const r = await node(["-e", "setTimeout(()=>console.log(\\"woke\\"), 1500)"], { allowFail: true }); return { code: r.code, timedOut: !!r.timedOut, out: r.stdout };';
+    const perCall = await runJs(dir, 'const r = await node(["-e", "setTimeout(()=>console.log(\\"woke\\"), 1500)"], { timeoutMs: 300, allowFail: true }); return { code: r.code, out: r.stdout };');
+    ok(!/woke/.test(perCall), `a 1.5s process under a 300ms per-call timeout is killed (${perCall.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    const attr = await runJs(dir, SLEEP, { timeout_ms: 4000 });
+    ok(/woke/.test(attr) && /"code": *0/.test(attr), `the same process completes under <js timeout_ms="4000"> (${attr.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    const attrTight = await runJs(dir, SLEEP, { timeout_ms: 1000 });
+    ok(/"out": *""/.test(attrTight), `and is killed under <js timeout_ms="1000"> — the attribute is the default for the call (${attrTight.slice(0, 80)})`);
+    const perCallWins = await runJs(dir, 'const r = await node(["-e", "setTimeout(()=>console.log(\\"woke\\"), 1500)"], { timeoutMs: 4000 }); return { out: r.stdout };', { timeout_ms: 300 });
+    ok(/woke/.test(perCallWins), 'a per-call {timeoutMs} still wins over the snippet attribute');
+    const capped = await runJs(dir, 'return "ok";', { timeout_ms: 99999999 });
+    ok(/ok/.test(capped), 'an oversized attribute is clamped (30 min), not refused');
+
+    console.log('\n[B] pressing ⇱ on a running step ends the run: it is the developer\'s turn');
+    const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-jsto-proj-'));
+    await writeFile(path.join(proj, 'README.md'), 'x');
+    let calls = 0;
+    const adapter = {
+      async complete() {
+        calls++;
+        // Turn 1: a slow snippet the test will background. Turn 2 must NEVER be asked for.
+        if (calls === 1) return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'Starting a long job. <remote-workspace><js>const r = await node(["-e", "setTimeout(()=>console.log(\\"done\\"), 6000)"]); return r.stdout;</js></remote-workspace>' } }], usage: { total_tokens: 5 } };
+        return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'SHOULD NOT BE REACHED — the developer took the turn.' } }], usage: { total_tokens: 5 } };
+      },
+    };
+    const events = [];
+    let pressed = false;
+    const runP = runMinimalWorker({
+      adapter, model: 'test', signal: { aborted: false }, messages: [{ content: 'run the long job' }], projectDir: proj,
+      requireBranch: () => 'ok', requireApproval: () => true, onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {},
+      onChatEvent: (e) => {
+        events.push(e);
+        // The moment the tool-call row appears, the developer presses ⇱ on it (what /api/background-tool does).
+        if (!pressed && e.type === 'tool-call' && e.name === 'js_eval') { pressed = true; setTimeout(() => { const ids = backgroundToolCall(e.callId); events.push({ type: '_pressed', ids }); }, 700); }
+      },
+    });
+    const result = await Promise.race([runP, sleep(20000).then(() => ({ timeout: true }))]);
+    ok(!result.timeout, 'the run returned (it did not wait out the 6s job)');
+    const pressedEv = events.find((e) => e.type === '_pressed');
+    ok(!!pressedEv && pressedEv.ids.length === 1, `⇱ adopted the running process (${JSON.stringify(pressedEv && pressedEv.ids)})`);
+    ok(calls === 1, `the model was called ONCE — no turn after the backgrounded step (${calls})`);
+    const res = events.filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.name === 'js_eval').pop();
+    ok(!!res && /BACKGROUNDED/.test(String(res.content || '')), 'the tool result carries the backgrounded banner');
+    const closer = events.filter((e) => e.type === 'assistant-text').pop();
+    ok(!!closer && /Backgrounded/.test(String(closer.content || '')) && /Over to you/.test(String(closer.content || '')) && (pressedEv ? String(closer.content).includes(pressedEv.ids[0]) : true), `the run closes with a one-line hand-off naming the id (${closer && closer.content})`);
+    ok(result && result.terminated === 'stop' && /Over to you/.test(result.content || ''), 'and the worker reports a normal stop with that closer');
+    const live = bgStatus({ key: proj }).processes.filter((p) => p.alive);
+    ok(live.length >= 1, 'the process is still running in the background');
+    for (const p of live) killBackground({ key: proj, id: p.id });
+    await rm(proj, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.stack || e); }
+  finally { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
+}
+main();
+

+/**
+ * TWO THINGS THE PROMPT TELLS THE AGENT TO WRITE IN A <js> SNIPPET, AND THAT DID NOTHING.

+ *                                  arm the cross-turn wake, once <run> was gone.

=== add test/test-js-token-unmask.mjs
new 2b051def51ce776e3103a9cd
+/**
+ * TOKENS A SNIPPET GETS AT RUN TIME ARE UNMASKED TOO. H2 used to see only the snippet's code text, so a token the
+ * snippet came by while running — a plugin that masks its own result rows (maskFields → @@@(<field>#<id>)) — reached
+ * the next tool literally. Production, 2026-09-25: a query row's `@@@(dod_edi_pn_id#…)` passed to cuf_pull in a
+ * loop arrived at cuf-runner.jar as text (NumberFormatException).
+ *   [1] a token RETURNED by one plugin tool and passed to another is resolved before the second runs
+ *   [2] the same for a host function (curl body) — every snippet call gets H2, not just plugin tools
+ *   [3] a token the filter cannot resolve stops the call: the tool does not run, the snippet gets the reason
+ *   [4] no filter active: a token still stops the call (defense in depth), with the "not active" wording
+ *   [5] write() content may carry a token (docs, examples) — not refused
+ * Run: node test/test-js-token-unmask.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { runMinimalWorker } from '../src/agent/minimal-worker.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+const TOKEN = '@@@(dod_edi_pn_id#0123456789abcdef)';
+const REAL = '1234567890';
+// a stand-in for pii-testdata's pair: one known token, anything else token-shaped is left alone (the guard's job)
+const walk = (fn) => async function deep(v) {
+  if (typeof v === 'string') return fn(v);
+  if (Array.isArray(v)) return Promise.all(v.map(deep));
+  if (v && typeof v === 'object') { const o = {}; for (const [k, x] of Object.entries(v)) o[k] = await deep(x); return o; }
+  return v;
+};
+const filter = {
+  active: () => true,
+  mask: async (t) => (typeof t === 'string' ? t.split(REAL).join(TOKEN) : t),
+  unmask: async (t) => t.split(TOKEN).join(REAL),
+};
+filter.unmaskDeep = walk(filter.unmask);
+filter.maskDeep = walk(filter.mask);
+
+function mockAdapter(script) {
+  let i = 0;
+  return { async complete() { const c = script[Math.min(i++, script.length - 1)]; return { choices: [{ message: { content: c } }], usage: { total_tokens: 5 } }; } };
+}
+const pulled = [];
+const extraTools = {
+  person_query: {   // masks its own rows, as a plugin using maskFields does
+    schema: { description: 'rows', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } },
+    impl: async () => ({ content: JSON.stringify([{ edi: TOKEN }, { edi: '@@@(dod_edi_pn_id#fedcba9876543210)' }]) }),
+  },
+  cuf_pull: {
+    schema: { description: 'pull', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: { id: { type: 'string' } }, required: ['id'] } },
+    impl: async ({ id }) => { pulled.push(id); return { content: /^\d+$/.test(id) ? 'pulled ' + id.length + ' digits' : 'NumberFormatException: ' + id }; },
+  },
+};
+async function run(dir, code, contentFilter) {
+  const events = [];
+  await runMinimalWorker({
+    adapter: mockAdapter([`Going. <remote-workspace><js>${code}</js></remote-workspace>`, 'Done.']), model: 'test', signal: { aborted: false },
+    messages: [{ content: 'do the thing' }], projectDir: dir,
+    requireBranch: () => 'ok', requireApproval: () => true,
+    onChatEvent: (e) => events.push(e), onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {},
+    extraTools, extraVerbs: { person_query: 'person_query', cuf_pull: 'cuf_pull' },
+    ...(contentFilter ? { contentFilter } : {}),
+  });
+  return events.filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.name === 'js_eval').map((e) => String(e.content || '')).join('\n');
+}
+const withDir = async (fn) => { const d = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-jstok-')); try { await fn(d); } finally { await rm(d, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); } };
+
+console.log('\n[1] a token one plugin tool returned is resolved before the next tool gets it');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  pulled.length = 0;
+  const out = await run(dir, 'const rows = JSON.parse(await person_query({})); return await cuf_pull({ id: rows[0].edi });', filter);
+  ok(pulled[0] === REAL, `cuf_pull got the real value (${pulled[0]})`);
+  ok(/pulled 10 digits/.test(out), `and the snippet saw it succeed (${out.slice(0, 80)})`);
+  ok(!out.includes(REAL), 'the real value never reaches the transcript (H3 still masks the result)');
+});
+
+console.log('\n[2] a host function gets it too (the token sits in a curl body)');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  let seen = null;
+  const out = await run(dir, `try { await curl("http://127.0.0.1:1/x", { method: "POST", body: "${TOKEN}" }); } catch (e) { return "threw " + e.message; } return "ran";`, {
+    ...filter, unmaskDeep: async (v) => { const r = await filter.unmaskDeep(v); seen = r; return r; },
+  });
+  ok(JSON.stringify(seen || '').includes(REAL), `curl's args went through the filter (${JSON.stringify(seen).slice(0, 80)})`);
+  ok(!/UNRESOLVED/.test(out), `and were not refused (${out.slice(0, 80)})`);
+});
+
+console.log('\n[3] a token the filter cannot resolve stops the call — the tool does not run');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  pulled.length = 0;
+  const out = await run(dir, 'const rows = JSON.parse(await person_query({})); try { return await cuf_pull({ id: rows[1].edi }); } catch (e) { return "STOPPED: " + e.message; }', filter);
+  ok(pulled.length === 0, `cuf_pull never ran (${JSON.stringify(pulled)})`);
+  ok(/STOPPED: the test-data token @@@\(dod_edi_pn_id#fedcba9876543210\) reached cuf_pull\((?:…)?\) UNRESOLVED/.test(out), `the snippet was told why (${out.slice(0, 120)})`);
+  ok(/filter is active but (?:could|did) not resolve it/.test(out), 'naming the filter as active');
+});
+
+console.log('\n[4] no filter active: a token built at run time is still stopped, and says the filter is not active');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  pulled.length = 0;
+  const out = await run(dir, `try { return await cuf_pull({ id: "@@@" + "${TOKEN.slice(3)}" }); } catch (e) { return "STOPPED: " + e.message; }`, null);
+  ok(pulled.length === 0, 'cuf_pull never ran');
+  ok(/reached cuf_pull\((?:…)?\) UNRESOLVED/.test(out) && /No content filter is active/.test(out), `the reason names it (${out.slice(0, 120)})`);
+});
+
+console.log('\n[5] write() content may carry a token — not refused');
+await withDir(async (dir) => {
+  const out = await run(dir, 'const w = await write("doc.md", "example: @@@" + "(smiths.sponsor.ssn)\\n"); return w.ok ? "wrote" : "no: " + w.error;', null);
+  const body = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'doc.md'), 'utf8').catch(() => '');
+  ok(/wrote/.test(out) && body.includes('@@@(smiths.sponsor.ssn)'), `the file holds the example token (${out.slice(0, 60)})`);
+});
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

+/**
+ * AN EXPIRED API KEY, AND RESUBMIT (the developer, 2026-10-30): the gateway answers 401 "Your API key has expired.
+ * Create a new key in the portal." - "We need a process like the api key unlock for key expiration"; and "when a message
+ * fails and there's a resubmit button, it submits a second copy of your message instead of resubmitting the same

+ *   1. the run waits; the page asks for a new key, with the portal; saved, the run goes on with it - one message
+ *   2. declined: the run fails; the resubmit offer, clicked (with a good key now), runs THE SAME message - no copy
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-key-expired-e2e.mjs

+import path from 'node:path';

+// the mock: the old key is EXPIRED (the gateway's own words), the new one works
+const EXPIRED = { error: { type: 'unauthorized', message: 'Your API key has expired. Create a new key in the portal.', param: null, code: null } };
+const mock = { calls: [], reply: 'REPLY_ONE' };
+function startMock() {
+  const srv = createServer(async (req, res) => {
+    let raw = ''; for await (const c of req) raw += c;
+    const auth = String(req.headers.authorization || '');
+    mock.calls.push({ path: req.url, auth });
+    if (req.url.endsWith('/models')) { res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json' }); res.end(JSON.stringify({ data: [{ id: 'mock-model' }] })); return; }
+    if (!/new-key/.test(auth)) { res.writeHead(401, { 'content-type': 'application/json' }); res.end(JSON.stringify(EXPIRED)); return; }
+    let body = {}; try { body = JSON.parse(raw); } catch {}
+    if (body.stream) {
+      res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/event-stream' });
+      res.write('data: ' + JSON.stringify({ choices: [{ delta: { content: mock.reply }, finish_reason: null }] }) + '\n\n');
+      res.write('data: ' + JSON.stringify({ choices: [{ delta: {}, finish_reason: 'stop' }], usage: { prompt_tokens: 5, completion_tokens: 2, total_tokens: 7 } }) + '\n\n');
+      res.end('data: [DONE]\n\n');
+      return;
+    }
+    res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json' });
+    res.end(JSON.stringify({ choices: [{ message: { role: 'assistant', content: mock.reply }, finish_reason: 'stop' }], usage: { prompt_tokens: 5, completion_tokens: 2, total_tokens: 7 } }));
+  });
+  return new Promise((r) => srv.listen(MOCK_PORT, () => r(srv)));
+}

+/**
+ * "Stop the server" must not stop code_boss.
+ *
+ * Live, 2026-10-09 (live-dev-session-2): asked to stop a server it had started, the agent ran
+ *     Get-Process -Name node | Stop-Process -Force
+ * from inside `node --eval`. code_boss is a node process: the session died mid-run, and every other node program on
+ * the machine with it. Any command that stops node processes BY NAME is refused, with the way to stop just one.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-kill-all-node-refused.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { killsEveryNodeProcess, execute } from '../src/agent/tools.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+console.log('\n[1] what counts');
+for (const bad of [
+  'Get-Process -Name node -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Stop-Process -Force',
+  'Get-Process node | Stop-Process',
+  'Stop-Process -Name node -Force',
+  'taskkill /F /IM node.exe',
+  'taskkill /im node.exe /f',
+  'pkill -f node',
+  'killall node',
+  'wmic process where name="node.exe" delete',
+  "execFileSync('powershell', ['-Command', \"Get-Process -Name node -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Stop-Process -Force\"])",
+]) ok(!!killsEveryNodeProcess(bad), `refused: ${bad.slice(0, 70)}`);
+for (const fine of [
+  'Stop-Process -Id 4312 -Force',
+  'taskkill /PID 4312 /F',
+  'Get-Process -Name node',
+  'Get-Process java | Stop-Process',
+  'node server.js',
+  'npm run stop',
+  'kill 4312',
+  'console.log("the node process is still up")',
+]) ok(!killsEveryNodeProcess(fine), `allowed: ${fine}`);
+
+console.log('\n[2] through the snippet\'s own functions - the shape the live agent used');
+const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-killall-'));
+try {
+  const run = async (code) => { try { const r = await execute('js_eval', { code }, {}, { cwd: dir, projectDir: dir }); return typeof r === 'string' ? r : String((r && (r.content || r.error)) || ''); } catch (e) { return 'THROWN ' + (e?.message || e); } };
+  const live = await run('const ps = await node(["--eval", `const { execFileSync } = require("child_process"); execFileSync("powershell", ["-Command", "Get-Process -Name node -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Stop-Process -Force"]);`]); return ps.stdout;');
+  ok(/refused/.test(live) && /EVERY node process/.test(live) && /bgKill/.test(live), `nested inside node --eval: refused, and told what to do instead (${live.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').slice(0, 150)})`);
+  const stillHere = await run('const r = await node(["--eval", "console.log(6 * 7)"]); return r.stdout.trim();');
+  ok(/42/.test(stillHere), `an ordinary node run is untouched, and this process is still alive to say so (${stillHere.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').slice(0, 60)})`);
+} finally { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+

+  ok(!/BACKGROUND PROCESSES IN THIS PROJECT/.test(buildLiveTail({ projectDir: 'D:/x' })), 'and says nothing when there is none');

+  await waitBackground({ key, id, timeoutMs: 15000 });

+/**
+ * known-values.mjs — what a filter masked in one call's return, applied to the rest of the same snippet's output.
+ * The end-to-end proof (a search and a two-file read whose values used to reach the model) is in
+ * test-piiscan-session-e2e; this is the alignment itself.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-known-values.mjs

+import { kvPairs, kvApply, kvLayers, kvLearn, kvLearnArgs, kvRank, kvRemember, kvSession, _kvResetSession, KV_CODE, KV_TEXT, KV_PROSE } from '../src/agent/known-values.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const T = (t, id) => `[PII:${t}#${id}]`;
+
+console.log('\n[1] which substrings did the filter replace?');
+{
+  const p = kvPairs('support.contact_name=Rutherford Havisham\nclinic.postal_code=75201-1188\nplain=stays', `support.contact_name=${T('NAME', '473fb8d4')}\nclinic.postal_code=${T('ZIP', '409ec3f4')}\nplain=stays`);
+  ok(p.size === 2 && p.get('Rutherford Havisham') === T('NAME', '473fb8d4') && p.get('75201-1188') === T('ZIP', '409ec3f4'), 'a value at the end of its line, one per line');
+  const q = kvPairs('Rutherford,Havisham,482-11-9037,(214) 555-0182,x', `${T('NAME', 'aaaaaaaa')},${T('NAME', 'bbbbbbbb')},${T('SSN', 'cccccccc')},${T('PHONE', 'dddddddd')},x`);
+  ok(q.size === 4 && q.get('482-11-9037') === T('SSN', 'cccccccc') && q.get('(214) 555-0182') === T('PHONE', 'dddddddd') && q.get('Rutherford') === T('NAME', 'aaaaaaaa'), 'several on one line, at its start, lined up by the text between them');
+  ok(kvPairs('a=1\nb=2', 'a=1').size === 0 && kvPairs('key=value here', 'KEY=' + T('X', 'eeeeeeee')).size === 0, 'a different number of lines, or a line the filter re-wrote beyond its values: nothing is guessed');
+  ok(kvPairs('id=42', 'id=' + T('N', 'ffffffff')).size === 0, 'a value too short to replace safely elsewhere ("42") is not kept');
+  ok(kvPairs('x=@@@(smiths.sponsor.ssn) y', 'x=@@@(smiths.sponsor.ssn) y').size === 0, 'unchanged text teaches nothing');

+  ok(kvLayers('x', '.env') !== null && kvLayers('x', 'config/.env.local') !== null, '.env files are settings');
+  ok(kvLayers('x', 'build.gradle') !== null && kvLayers('// c', 'build.gradle')[0] === KV_TEXT, 'gradle is C-like');

=== add test/test-large-conversation-restore.mjs
new 6174c26cae6f119e5934de23
+/**
+ * Reload a chat with > 100 events. Verify:
+ *   - the SSE init shows ONLY the most recent 100 (cap is intentional —
+ *     keeps initial render fast and the payload small)
+ *   - scrolling the chat to the top triggers maybeLoadOlder, which
+ *     backfills via /api/events and prepends the older events
+ *   - after the backfill, the earliest "first message" is in the DOM
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-large-conversation-restore.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile, writeFile, mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { startServer } from '../src/server.mjs';
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29080;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+function ok(cond, label) {
+  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ok', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', label); }
+}
+function findChrome() {
+  const c = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe',
+             (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'];
+  return c.find(p => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+}
+function mockAdapter(scripts) {
+  let i = 0;
+  return { async complete() {
+    if (i >= scripts.length) throw new Error('script exhausted');
+    return { choices: [{ message: { content: scripts[i++] } }] };
+  }};
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const chrome = findChrome();
+  if (!chrome) { console.error('Chrome not found'); process.exit(2); }
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-rb-'));
+
+  // Pre-seed conversation.json with 112 events: "first message" at the
+  // very top, then 90 fillers, then "second message", then 20 more
+  // fillers. With the 100-cap, the initial SSE init only carries events
+  // 13..112 — "first message" and the first ~12 fillers should NOT be
+  // in the DOM until the user scrolls up and the backfill fires.
+  await mkdir(path.join(dir, '.code_boss'), { recursive: true });
+  const fakeEvents = [];
+  let id = 1;
+  fakeEvents.push({ id: `e${id++}`, appendedAtVersion: 1, type: 'user-message', content: 'first message' });
+  for (let i = 0; i < 90; i++) {
+    fakeEvents.push({ id: `e${id++}`, appendedAtVersion: id, type: 'assistant-text', content: `filler line ${i}` });
+  }
+  fakeEvents.push({ id: `e${id++}`, appendedAtVersion: id, type: 'user-message', content: 'second message' });
+  for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) {
+    fakeEvents.push({ id: `e${id++}`, appendedAtVersion: id, type: 'assistant-text', content: `post-second filler ${i}` });
+  }
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, '.code_boss', 'conversation.json'), JSON.stringify({
+    version: 1, savedAt: new Date().toISOString(), nextEventId: id, chatEvents: fakeEvents,
+  }), 'utf8');
+  console.log('  pre-seeded', fakeEvents.length, 'events on disk');
+
+  const html = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8');
+  const mosaicSrc = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'agent', 'mosaic-layout.mjs'), 'utf8');
+  const mosaicClean = mosaicSrc.replace(/^import\s+[^;]+;[ \t]*\r?\n?/gm, '')
+    .replace(/^export\s+(async\s+function|function|class|const|let|var)\b/gm, '$1');
+  const htmlWithConfig = html.replace('</head>',
+    '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script>' +
+    '<script>' + mosaicClean + '</script></head>');
+
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html: htmlWithConfig, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm',
+    port: PORT, adapter: mockAdapter([]), modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: dir,
+  });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    await page.setViewport({ width: 1100, height: 800 });
+    page.on('pageerror', (e) => console.log('     [pageerror]', e.message));
+    await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 500));
+
+    // After the SSE init: only the latest 100 events are in session.events.
+    const initialCount = await page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.events?.length);
+    ok(initialCount === 100, `initial event window is 100 (got ${initialCount})`);
+    let userBubbles = await page.$$eval('.bubble.user .bubble-content', (els) => els.map((e) => e.textContent.trim()));
+    ok(userBubbles.length === 1 && /second message/.test(userBubbles[0]),
+       `only "second message" rendered initially (got: ${JSON.stringify(userBubbles)})`);
+
+    // hasMoreOlder should be true on the snapshot so the client knows
+    // to keep the scroll-to-load hook armed.
+    const hasMoreOlder = await page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.hasMoreOlder);
+    ok(hasMoreOlder === true, `hasMoreOlder flag is true (got: ${hasMoreOlder})`);
+
+    // Scroll the chat to the top → maybeLoadOlder fires, fetches via
+    // /api/events?before=<id>&limit=50, prepends the next batch. Only
+    // 12 older events exist here (112 total - 100 already in window),
+    // so the backfill picks up exactly those 12.
+    await page.evaluate(() => { document.getElementById('logScroll').scrollTop = 0; });
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => (window.__sessionRef?.events?.length || 0) >= 112, { timeout: 5000 });
+    const afterLoad = await page.evaluate(() => window.__sessionRef?.events?.length);
+    // >= rather than ==: opening the project also appends a plugin-load row, so the total is the seeded events
+    // plus whatever the session legitimately added. What matters is that all 112 seeded ones came back — the
+    // assertion below reads the first of them off the top of the DOM.
+    ok(afterLoad >= 112, `scroll-to-top backfilled the older events (got ${afterLoad}, seeded 112)`);
+
+    // First message should now be in the DOM.
+    userBubbles = await page.$$eval('.bubble.user .bubble-content', (els) => els.map((e) => e.textContent.trim()));
+    ok(userBubbles.length === 2 && /first message/.test(userBubbles[0]),
+       `first message present at top after backfill (got: ${JSON.stringify(userBubbles)})`);
+
+  } finally {
+    await browser.close();
+    server.close();
+    await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }
+
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });

=== add test/test-launch-checks.mjs
new c26bf039c2d84014162c9a06
+// The startup CHECKS, against real processes and real ports.
+//
+// Mirroring the launcher log into the window was not enough on its own: the log "looked normal" — spawn, then
+// silence — because narrating what the launcher DID says nothing about what went wrong. These checks answer the
+// questions that separate cases which look identical from a spinner:
+//
+//   process gone            → node crashed; app-stdout.log holds the reason
+//   alive + port refused    → node is running but never opened the port
+//   alive + port silent     → the port is open but the app never answers HTTP
+//
+// Verified against actual conditions rather than mocks, because the whole point is that they report reality.
+import { createServer } from 'node:http';
+import { createServer as createRawServer } from 'node:net';
+import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
+import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { lcProbePort, lcProcAlive, lcStdoutTail } from '../src/launch-cmd.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[1] port state — the three cases are actually distinguished\n');
+{
+  ok(await lcProbePort(19301) === 'refused', 'nothing listening → refused');
+
+  const http = createServer((req, res) => { res.writeHead(200); res.end('ok'); });
+  await new Promise((r) => http.listen(19302, '127.0.0.1', r));
+  ok(await lcProbePort(19302) === 'answering', 'a real server → answering');
+  http.close();
+
+  // Accepts the connection and then says nothing — the case that looks identical to "still starting" from outside,
+  // and the one a plain up/down check cannot tell apart.
+  const raw = createRawServer(() => { /* deliberately no response */ });
+  await new Promise((r) => raw.listen(19303, '127.0.0.1', r));
+  const silent = await lcProbePort(19303);
+  ok(silent === 'silent', `open socket that never responds → silent (got ${silent})`);
+  raw.close();
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[2] process liveness — against a real child\n');
+{
+  const child = spawn(process.execPath, ['-e', 'setTimeout(()=>{},5000)'], { stdio: 'ignore' });
+  await sleep(300);
+  ok(lcProcAlive(child.pid), 'a running child reports RUNNING');
+  child.kill();
+  await sleep(500);
+  ok(!lcProcAlive(child.pid), 'a killed child reports GONE');
+  ok(!lcProcAlive(0) && !lcProcAlive(null), 'no pid is not mistaken for alive');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[3] the app output is surfaced — this is what "looks normal" was hiding\n');
+{
+  const base = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-chk-'));
+  const ctx = { base };
+  ok(lcStdoutTail(ctx) === null, 'no log file yet → nothing claimed');
+  await writeFile(path.join(base, 'app-stdout.log'), '', 'utf8');
+  ok(lcStdoutTail(ctx) === null, 'an EMPTY log is reported as nothing, not as a blank line');
+  await writeFile(path.join(base, 'app-stdout.log'),
+    'boot line 1\nnode:internal/modules/esm/resolve:275\n  throw new ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND(\nError: Cannot find module\n', 'utf8');
+  const tail = lcStdoutTail(ctx, 3);
+  ok(Array.isArray(tail) && tail.length === 3, 'the tail is returned, newest last');
+  ok(/Cannot find module/.test(tail.join(' ')), 'a crash message reaches the surface instead of dying in a file');
+  ok(!tail.some((l) => l.trim() === ''), 'blank lines are dropped so the feed stays dense');
+  await rm(base, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+}
+
+process.stdout.write(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed\n`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
+

new 43f401322706ee0386dc83e3
+// The startup window, in a real browser. Reported: "it loaded for a long time and then said start within 30 seconds
+// ... nothing in the diagnostic log or launch log that's helpful."
+//
+// Two defects behind that. The page counted ATTEMPTS (75 x 400ms) and called it "30 seconds", but the readiness
+// fetch had no per-attempt deadline — a socket that connects and then stalls hangs it forever, the counter stops
+// advancing, and the fixed message is eventually shown after far longer than 30s. And a spinner was the entire UI,
+// so a slow phase was invisible while it was happening.
+//
+// The launcher already logged its phases to launch.log; that same lcLog() now also feeds the window. This test
+// drives the REAL page with the REAL writer, because the load path it depends on is the one thing that could
+// silently not work: a file:// page cannot fetch() a sibling, and only loads one as a classic <script>.
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { lcWriteLoadingPage, lcLog } from '../src/launch-cmd.mjs';
+
+const chrome = [(process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe',
+  'C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe',
+  'C:\\Program Files (x86)\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+if (!chrome) { console.log('SKIP: Chrome not found'); process.exit(0); }
+let puppeteer;
+try { puppeteer = (await import('puppeteer-core')).default; } catch { console.log('SKIP: puppeteer-core not installed'); process.exit(0); }
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+
+const base = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-boot-'));
+const ctx = { base };
+const pagePath = lcWriteLoadingPage(ctx);
+lcLog(ctx, 'launcher (node v20.19.2) — runs the app installed in ' + path.join(base, 'app'));
+lcLog(ctx, 'scan: looking for a running instance / free port…');
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[1] the page no longer hardcodes a wait it does not measure\n');
+{
+  const html = await readFile(pagePath, 'utf8');
+  ok(!/did not start within 30 seconds/.test(html), 'the fixed "did not start within 30 seconds" text is gone');
+  ok(/DEADLINE_MS/.test(html) && /Date\.now\(\)-t0/.test(html), 'failure is decided on ELAPSED TIME, not an attempt count');
+  ok(/AbortController/.test(html) && /setTimeout\(function\(\)\{try\{ac\.abort/.test(html),
+    'each readiness attempt has its own deadline, so a stalled socket cannot freeze the counter');
+}
+
+// Port 1 never answers, so the page stays in its polling state for the whole test.
+const url = 'file:///' + pagePath.replace(/\\/g, '/').replace(/ /g, '%20') + '?port=1';
+const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+try {
+  const page = await browser.newPage();
+  const errors = [];
+  page.on('pageerror', (e) => errors.push(e.message));
+  await page.goto(url, { waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' });
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[2] the launcher log is visible IN the window as it happens\n');
+  // The whole point: a file:// page cannot fetch() a sibling file. If the <script> route were blocked too, the feed
+  // would stay empty and the user would be back to staring at a spinner.
+  await page.waitForFunction(() => /looking for a running instance/.test(document.getElementById('feed')?.textContent || ''), { timeout: 15000 })
+    .then(() => ok(true, 'boot-log.js loads from file:// and its lines render in the feed'))
+    .catch(() => ok(false, 'the feed never rendered — the file:// <script> route does not work'));
+  const feed = await page.$eval('#feed', (el) => el.textContent);
+  ok(/launcher \(node/.test(feed), 'earlier phases are shown, not just the newest');
+  ok(/\d+\.\ds/.test(feed), 'each line carries the elapsed time it happened at');
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[3] new phases appear WHILE the user is waiting\n');
+  lcLog(ctx, 'server: waiting for it to answer on port 1…');
+  await page.waitForFunction(() => /waiting for it to answer/.test(document.getElementById('feed')?.textContent || ''), { timeout: 15000 })
+    .then(() => ok(true, 'a line logged after the window opened shows up without a reload'))
+    .catch(() => ok(false, 'the feed does not update live'));
+  const sub = await page.$eval('#sub', (el) => el.textContent);
+  ok(/waiting for it to answer/.test(sub), 'the headline sub-text tracks the current phase');
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[4] the launcher can navigate the window itself\n');
+  // The page used to be the SOLE judge of readiness, via its own fetch on its own 30s budget. When the server came
+  // up near that boundary the launcher logged "server: up on port 18888" while the page had already given up — a
+  // log that looks completely normal beside a window saying it failed. Readiness is now published through
+  // boot-log.js, the one channel a file:// page can definitely read, and the page acts on it.
+  {
+    const before = await page.evaluate(() => location.href);
+    ctx._bootReady = 1;
+    lcLog(ctx, 'server: up on port 1 after 3.2s');   // republishes the feed, now carrying the ready flag
+    let moved = false;
+    try {
+      await page.waitForFunction((b) => location.href !== b, { timeout: 8000 }, before);
+      moved = true;
+    } catch {}
+    ok(moved, 'the page navigates on the launcher signal, without needing its own fetch to succeed');
+    ctx._bootReady = 0;
+    lcLog(ctx, 'server: waiting for it to answer on port 1…');   // republishes (clearing ready) AND restores the phase
+  }
+
+  // A clean page for the failure-path assertions below.
+  await page.goto(url, { waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' });
+  await page.waitForFunction(() => (document.getElementById('feed')?.textContent || '').length > 0, { timeout: 15000 }).catch(() => {});
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[5] the failure message reports what actually happened\n');
+  // Invoked directly rather than waiting out the real deadline — the wording and inputs are what matter here.
+  await page.evaluate(() => window.fail());
+  // VISIBILITY, checked before the wording. .err is display:none until the failed class lands, and textContent reads
+  // the same either way — an earlier CSS edit dropped the rule that reveals it and every wording assertion below
+  // still passed against a message nobody could see. Only a screenshot caught it.
+  const errShown = await page.$eval('#err', (el) => el.offsetParent !== null && getComputedStyle(el).display !== 'none');
+  ok(errShown, 'the failure message is actually VISIBLE, not just present in the DOM');
+  const spinnerGone = await page.$eval('.ring', (el) => getComputedStyle(el).display === 'none');
+  ok(spinnerGone, 'the spinner stops — it no longer looks like it is still trying');
+  const err = await page.$eval('#err', (el) => el.textContent);
+  ok(/after \d+ seconds/.test(err), `it states the REAL elapsed time (${(err.match(/after \d+ seconds/) || [])[0]})`);
+  ok(!/30 seconds/.test(err) || /after 30 seconds/.test(err), 'no fixed 30-second claim');
+  ok(/Last step:/.test(err) && /waiting for it to answer/.test(err), 'it names the phase it died on');
+  ok(/launch\.log/.test(err) && /app-stdout\.log/.test(err), 'it points at both logs by name');
+  ok(/port 1/.test(err), 'it names the port it was waiting on');
+  const failedFeedVisible = await page.$eval('#feed', (el) => getComputedStyle(el).display !== 'none');
+  ok(failedFeedVisible, 'the feed stays on screen after failure — the evidence is right there to screenshot');
+
+  ok(errors.length === 0, `no client-side JS errors (${errors.slice(0, 2).join(' | ') || 'none'})`);
+} finally {
+  await browser.close();
+  await rm(base, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+}
+
+process.stdout.write(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed\n`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

+/**
+ * launch subcommand (src/launch-cmd.mjs) — the Node launcher folded into code-boss.mjs as `node code-boss.mjs launch`
+ * (it REPLACED launch.vbs; security policy blocks .vbs/.hta). Tested by importing the module so no build is needed:
+ *   [A] lcWriteLoadingPage writes a valid loading page (polls no-cors, self-redirects, pure ASCII).
+ *   [B] lcClassifyPort marks a /api/state with status+version markers "ours", a bare 200 "foreign", nothing "down".
+ *   [C] lcFirstRunInstall self-installs the running bundle into app/ (copy / newer-upgrade / skip-when-same /
+ *       missing-source fails cleanly) and lcWriteIcons drops both shortcut icons byte-for-byte.
+ *   [D] lcBuildKillPs keeps the process-NAME narrowing (-Filter Name='node.exe' OR 'chrome.exe') AND the --kill
+ *       self-exclusion — the perf/scope guard the VBS had.
+ *   [E] lcWatchChildClose (THE regression fix) resolves even when the child exits during a BLOCKING gap and when the
+ *       child already exited before it was called — a one-shot 'exit' is never lost, so the launcher can't hang.
+ *   [F] end-to-end through the built bundle (if present): `node dist/code-boss.mjs launch --writeloading` works
+ *       (guards against a flat-bundle-scope name collision in the dispatch).
+ * Run: node test/test-launch-mjs.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile, writeFile, mkdir, stat } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync, statSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { spawn, spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
+import { createServer } from 'node:http';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import { cleanupDir } from './_helpers.mjs';
+import { lcContext, lcWriteLoadingPage, lcClassifyPort, lcFirstRunInstall, lcWriteIcons, lcBuildKillPs, lcWatchChildClose } from '../src/launch-cmd.mjs';
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+// lcContext reads CB_HOME from env; for tests we set env per-case then build the ctx (self = the "running bundle").
+function ctx(home, self, args = []) { process.env.CB_HOME = home; return lcContext(args, self || path.join(home, 'src.mjs')); }
+
+function mockServer(kind) {
+  return createServer((req, res) => {
+    if ((req.url || '').startsWith('/api/state')) {
+      res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json' });
+      res.end(kind === 'ours' ? JSON.stringify({ status: 'idle', version: '1.2.3' }) : JSON.stringify({ hello: 'world' }));
+    } else { res.writeHead(200); res.end('ok'); }
+  });
+}
+const listen = (s) => new Promise((r) => s.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', () => r(s.address().port)));
+
+async function main() {
+  // ── [A] loading page ──
+  console.log('\n[A] lcWriteLoadingPage writes a valid loading page');
+  {
+    const home = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-lc-load-'));
+    try {
+      const c = ctx(home);
+      const p = lcWriteLoadingPage(c);
+      const html = await readFile(p, 'utf8');
+      ok(existsSync(p), 'loading.html written');
+      ok(/mode:'no-cors'/.test(html) && /location\.replace/.test(html), 'polls no-cors + redirects via location.replace');
+      ok(/location\.search\.match/.test(html) && /port=/.test(html), 'reads the ?port= param from the URL');
+      ok(/[^\x00-\x7F]/.test(html) === false, 'pure ASCII');
+    } finally { await cleanupDir(home); }
+  }
+
+  // ── [B] port classification ──
+  console.log('\n[B] lcClassifyPort: ours / foreign / down');
+  {
+    const sOurs = mockServer('ours'); const pOurs = await listen(sOurs);
+    const sForeign = mockServer('foreign'); const pForeign = await listen(sForeign);
+    try {
+      ok(await lcClassifyPort(pOurs) === 'ours', 'status+version markers → ours');
+      ok(await lcClassifyPort(pForeign) === 'foreign', '200 without markers → foreign');
+      ok(await lcClassifyPort(51823) === 'down', 'nothing listening → down');
+    } finally { sOurs.close(); sForeign.close(); }
+  }
+  // A port that CONNECTS but does not answer in time is BUSY, never down: a live instance under load (six windows,
+  // each holding an event stream) answered late, was read as "down", and the launcher started the new server on
+  // its port — the new window then opened on the OLD instance with its project (2026-09-18).
+  console.log('\n[B2] a connecting-but-silent port is BUSY, not down — its port is never taken');
+  {
+    const { createServer } = await import('node:http');
+    const sSilent = createServer(() => { /* never responds */ });
+    const pSilent = await new Promise((r) => sSilent.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', () => r(sSilent.address().port)));
+    try {
+      const t0 = Date.now();
+      const cls = await lcClassifyPort(pSilent);
+      ok(cls === 'busy', `a socket that connects and stays silent → busy (${cls}, after ${Date.now() - t0}ms)`);
+    } finally { sSilent.closeAllConnections?.(); sSilent.close(); }
+  }
+  // The readiness wait must confirm the answering server is the one the launcher spawned: with expectPid, a port
+  // whose instance registry names ANOTHER pid while ours is gone resolves 'other' (the launcher then stops with a
+  // clear message) instead of true (which opened the window on the wrong instance).
+  console.log('\n[B3] lcWaitForServer with expectPid: an answering port owned by another instance is "other"');
+  {
+    const { lcWaitForServer } = await import('../src/launch-cmd.mjs');
+    const { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, rm } = await import('node:fs/promises');
+    const base = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-lc-wait-'));
+    const sOther = mockServer('ours'); const pOther = await listen(sOther);
+    try {
+      await mkdir(path.join(base, 'instances'), { recursive: true });
+      await writeFile(path.join(base, 'instances', pOther + '.json'), JSON.stringify({ port: pOther, pid: 999999, project: 'D:/some/other/project' }));
+
+      const gone = 2147483000;
+      const r = await lcWaitForServer(pOther, 4000, null, { expectPid: gone, base });
+      ok(r === 'other', `the registry names another pid and ours is gone → 'other' (${JSON.stringify(r)})`);
+      await writeFile(path.join(base, 'instances', pOther + '.json'), JSON.stringify({ port: pOther, pid: process.pid }));
+      const r2 = await lcWaitForServer(pOther, 4000, null, { expectPid: process.pid, base });
+      ok(r2 === true, 'the registry names OUR pid → true');
+      const r3 = await lcWaitForServer(pOther, 2000, null, {});
+      ok(r3 === true, 'without expectPid the first answer still wins (the reuse path)');
+
+    } finally { sOther.close(); await rm(base, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+  }
+
+  // ── [C] self-install + icons ──
+  console.log('\n[C] lcFirstRunInstall self-install + lcWriteIcons');
+  {
+    const home = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-lc-inst-'));
+    const src = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-lc-src-'));
+    try {
+      const self = path.join(src, 'code-boss.mjs');
+      await writeFile(self, '// fake bundle v1\n', 'utf8');
+      const c = ctx(home, self);
+      const installed = path.join(home, '.code_boss', 'app', 'code-boss.mjs');
+
+      const r1 = lcFirstRunInstall(c);
+      ok(r1.ok && existsSync(installed), 'fresh install copies the running bundle into app/');
+
+      // a NEWER self upgrades in place
+      await sleep(20); await writeFile(self, '// fake bundle v2 (newer)\n', 'utf8');
+      const now = new Date();
+      // bump mtime forward to be safe
+      const r2 = lcFirstRunInstall(c);
+      ok(r2.ok && /v2/.test(await readFile(installed, 'utf8')), 'a newer bundle upgrades the installed app');
+
+      // running FROM the install location (self === app copy) is a no-op ok, never errors
+      const c2 = ctx(home, installed);
+      const r3 = lcFirstRunInstall(c2);
+      ok(r3.ok, 'running from the install location is a clean no-op (no self-copy error)');
+
+      // missing source, app already present → still ok (nothing to refresh)
+      const c3 = ctx(home, path.join(src, 'gone.mjs'));
+      ok(lcFirstRunInstall(c3).ok, 'a missing source with an installed app is still ok');
